The chosen shape is recorded in the draw (`BasicProgramShapeEXT`) rather than re-derived at replay, and `GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D` now takes the real stride for its binding (it hard-coded 16) with that stride folded into its key. **Vulkan therefore joins `TranslatesDeclarations()`** in the shared REMED-GFX-216/234 suite, with a comment saying exactly which families are converted and which still refuse by name. **Evidence, both halves:** the shared suite -- `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest`, `DeclarationGuardTest`, `IndexedDrawDeferredTest` -- is **26 passed, 0 failed**, and the translating control renders all three colliding declarations (`colorPosition16`, `positionTextureColor24`, `positionColorPadded32`), which is the assertion the first attempt could not satisfy. New CTest `Vulkan_DeclaredVertexLayout`, **6/6**: two 24-byte declarations with the colour at 12 vs 20 draw their own colours and get their own pipelines (`0 -> 1 -> 2` cache entries), a buffer with no declaration keeps the stride path, and Position+Colour padded to 32 renders its colour instead of being read as a lit vertex. **Mutation probes from the first attempt still apply and were re-run: layout out of the key -> legs B and C fail with `(0,0,64)`, the UV floats read as a colour; layout in the key but not in the attributes -> only B fails. **Suite:** `-R '^Vulkan_'` 224/225, full `ctest` **9059/9081** -- both numbers, per F-21. |
| VULKAN-147 | Drive the alpha-test, environment-map and skinned pipelines from the declaration | ✅ | **Done, the same way `VULKAN-146` ended up doing it, and with one thing that row did not need.** `GetOrCreatePipelineAlphaTest3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineEnvMap3D`, `GetOrCreatePipelineSkinned3D` and its `VertexLit` sibling take the layout, overwrite their baked attributes when it is complete, and carry its hash in the key. Their input tables were read from the shaders themselves. **No shape record was needed here**, unlike the BasicEffect bundle: the family is already decided by the caller's own `needsAlphaTest`/`needsEnvMap`/`needsSkinned` flags and none of these strides is ambiguous, so only the table is selected. **What alpha test makes concrete:** its one vertex shader serves strides 20 and 32 with the UV remapped to location 1, and the factory guessed the offset as `uvOffset = (stride == 32) ? 24 : 12` -- a guess about what a 32-byte record contains, written in the source. The comment stays, next to the call that now replaces it. **The new piece: an integer input cannot take a float attribute.** `BlendIndices` may legally be spelled `Byte4` or `Vector4` (`plans/plan_fx.md` FX-127) and the skinned shader declares `uvec4`; handing it a float attribute is a Vulkan usage error, not a conversion. `BuildVulkanVertexInputLayoutEXT` now compares the element's numeric type against the input's **primary** format -- deliberately not against `alternateFormat`, which records a legal *spelling* and not a bindable one -- and reports a mismatch as unrepresentable, so the caller refuses by name. **That makes `Vector4`-spelled `BlendIndices` a Vulkan refusal where EasyGL accepts it: `VULKAN-151`.** **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` (`vulkan_declared_effect_layout_test.cpp`), **7/7**. Each family draws the same geometry twice at the same stride with the UV element moved from 24 to 12, over a two-texel texture with UVs picked so the two offsets address opposite halves: canonical reads `(230,30,30)`, moved reads `(30,60,230)`, and each pair leaves **two** pipeline entries. `Vulkan_VertexInputLayout` gains leg E2 for the integer-format rule and is **13/13**. **Mutation:** with the attribute override disabled, all three families read `(230,30,30)` for *both* draws -- the stride-derived offset, and the defect itself. **Regression:** all 68 `Vulkan_(AlphaTest|EnvironmentMapEffect|SkinnedEffect|Pbr|BasicEffect|DualTexture|Model)*` tests green, and the full `ctest` measured below. |
| VULKAN-150 | Give the Vulkan `DualTextureEffect` a second UV channel (F-20) | ✅ | **Done, and it is the first row in this plan that changed a SHADER rather than a binding.** `dual_texture3d.vert.glsl` and `dual_texture_colored3d.vert.glsl` gain `in vec2 inUV1` and a fourth varying; `dual_texture3d.frag.glsl` samples `uTexture2` with it instead of with `fragUV`. SPIR-V regenerated with the repo's own `compile_shaders.py` (libshaderc), and the regeneration is **reproducible**: only the three `kDualTexture*Spv` arrays changed in `spirv_shaders.hpp`, nothing else moved. `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D` takes the declaration-derived layout, and its baked 20/24 binding stride became the record's own (`VULKAN-158`'s rule -- see below, it is why this row took two attempts). **The aliasing rule, which is the design decision this row owns:** a record that declares only `TextureCoordinate0` -- every stride-20 and stride-24 record this family has ever been handed -- gets `inUV1` pointed at `TextureCoordinate0`'s **own element**, not at a made-up offset. That reproduces the previous behaviour bit for bit instead of leaving a Vulkan shader input unbound, and it is applied only to `TextureCoordinate1`, only when `TextureCoordinate0` is present. Where neither can be found the layout stays incomplete and the draw goes back through the stride path and the fidelity guard, unchanged. **What this fixes for a game:** `DualTextureEffect` on Vulkan sampled `Texture2` with `TEXCOORD0`. XNA samples it with `TEXCOORD1`. Every lightmapped model with an independent second UV set rendered the wrong picture -- or rather, would have, because the declaration guard refused the record first, which is what F-20 recorded and why this was a skip and not a red test. **Evidence, both halves of the row's stated acceptance:** `Vulkan_DualTextureSlotSamplerContract` now runs its whole `A..M` body -- **106/106** -- where it previously ran 5 palette checks and skipped the rest; and EasyGL's own SAMPLE-073 fixture (`easygl_dualtextureeffect_independent_uv_test.cpp`) is registered for Vulkan as `Vulkan_DualTextureEffect_IndependentUV` and **passes**, reading `(255,0,0)` -- the red that only appears when `Texture` uses its left texel and `Texture2` uses its right one. That also closes the substance of `VULKAN-259`. **Mutation:** with the dual-texture layout builder stubbed to return an empty layout, the slot-sampler contract falls straight back to `[SKIP] A..M: this renderer does not implement DualTextureEffect (... the declaration carries TextureCoordinate0@12 Vector2, which this renderer's native layout for a 28-byte record does not bind at all ...)` and 5/5. **A false start worth recording:** the first attempt looked like a shader bug -- the stride-40 fixture rendered black with the new shader in place. It was not: `GetOrCreatePipelineDualTex3D` baked its binding stride, so the declaration's offsets were read out of records the fetch never reached. That is F-26, it affected three other families too, and it was fixed first as `VULKAN-158`. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-151 | Accept a `Vector4`-spelled `BlendIndices` on the skinned route | ✅ | **Done with ONE shader, not the second variant the row proposed, because Vulkan has a format for exactly this.** The five skinned vertex shaders (`skinned3d`, `_color`, `_vertexlit`, `_vertexlit_color`, `pbr3d_skinned`) now declare `in vec4 aBoneIndices` and subscript the palette with `int(...)`. `Vector4` binds natively; **`Byte4` binds through `VK_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_USCALED`**, which converts integer components to float **without** normalising -- precisely what `glVertexAttribPointer(..., GL_UNSIGNED_BYTE, GL_FALSE, ...)` does, so this is not a Vulkan trick but the conversion EasyGL has always relied on. A bone palette is a handful of entries, far inside float's exact-integer range. **The one risk, measured rather than assumed:** `_USCALED` is not in Vulkan's mandatory vertex-buffer format list. `spikes/vulkan-vertex-format-spike/` asks each physical device directly -- **RADV (AMD 780M) and llvmpipe both answer YES**, for `R8G8B8A8`, `R16G16` and `R16G16B16A16`. The renderer queries it on the device it actually selected and, where it is absent, refuses **by name** and names the way round (`Supply a VertexDeclaration spelling BlendIndices as Vector4, which binds natively.`) rather than letting `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` fail at `Present`. **The conversion is deliberately narrow:** it is offered only where the declaration named the input's own `alternateFormat` -- a spelling the program's table already sanctions -- so an arbitrary integer element that happens to line up with a float input is still reported unrepresentable, and a float element at an *integer* input still always is, because no format converts that direction. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_VertexInputLayout` is **16/16** -- leg E2 rewritten to the resolved contract (both spellings bind, both keep the declaration's own offset, and a device without `_USCALED` still *reports* rather than binds) and a new leg E3 for the narrowness rule. `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` gains leg **C2** and is **11/11**: the same skinned quad drawn twice, once with `Byte4` indices and once with `Vector4`, asserted as **equality of the two readbacks** exactly as this row asked -- plus a second assertion that the picture is the sampled texel, because two blank frames are equal too. The bone index used is **1**, and bone 0 is a transform that puts the quad off screen, so a draw that ignored the attribute would render nothing. **A shared test needed updating, and it is worth saying why that is not a weakened assertion:** `GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy.EverySkinnedPbrShaderConsumesThePaletteAndInfluenceCount` greps the shader source for `bb.bones[aBoneIndices.y]`, which is now `bb.bones[int(aBoneIndices.y)]` -- the same shape EasyGL's own row in that table has always had. What it asserts is unchanged. It is grepped from disk, so this failure was live in **every** renderer configuration until the row was corrected. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-148 | Drive the PBR and skinned-PBR pipelines from the declaration | ✅ | **Converted, and the row's acceptance is corrected: `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource` cannot go green here, and the reason is `VULKAN-152`.** `GetOrCreatePipelinePbr3D` and `GetOrCreatePipelinePbrSkinned3D` take the layout, override their attributes when it is complete, and carry its hash in the key, with input tables for all five variants (48, 60, 68, 76, 80) read from `pbr3d.vert.glsl` and `pbr3d_skinned.vert.glsl`. **`RequirePbrStrideEXT`'s list is now what a buffer with NO declaration is judged by** -- both the draw-time check (`VULKAN-346`'s) and the two factories' own throws are gated on `!IsComplete()`, so a declaration that supplies every input of the shader its stride selects is not held to 48/60 or 68/76/80. That is a real capability gain: glTF content whose PBR record is padded or reordered now renders instead of being refused. **Why the row's own acceptance was wrong:** `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp` draws its PBR legs from a **20-byte position+texture** buffer created as `VertexBuffer(dev, 6)` -- **no declaration at all**. There is nothing to take offsets from, and `pbr3d.vert.glsl` needs a normal and a tangent the record does not contain. EasyGL renders it because an unbound GL attribute takes a constant `(0,0,0,1)`; Vulkan cannot leave an attribute unbound. So this test needs the **default-attribute** mechanism, not the declaration path, and that is `VULKAN-152` -- the same unsupplied-input question `VULKAN-146`'s re-scope raised, now with a concrete second caller. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout` gains leg D2 and is **9/9**: a `PbrEffect` draw at stride **64** -- on no list, with four bytes of padding after each of the last three elements, the shape an aligning content pipeline produces -- renders from its declaration, and the *same bytes* with no declaration are still refused by name. Both halves matter; losing the second would turn an honest refusal into a draw from undefined bytes. **A mistake worth recording:** the first attempt gated only the draw-time helper and left both factories' own stride throws in place, so the refusal simply moved to `Present()`. The test caught it, which is what leg D2's *pair* of assertions is for. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-152 | Decide what an unsupplied shader input binds to (the question `VULKAN-146` raised and `VULKAN-148` hit) | ✅ | **Decided, and the answer is a refusal rather than the defaults buffer this row originally proposed. The premise that design rested on was wrong.** The row said EasyGL renders `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp`'s skinned/PBR legs because an unbound GL attribute takes a constant `(0,0,0,1)`, and that Vulkan therefore had to reproduce that constant to reach parity. It does not: that test carries a compile-time boundary constant, `kSkinnedFamiliesAcceptPositionTexture`, and **EasyGL is already on its declining side** -- with WEBGPU, DIRECTX9, DIRECTX11, DIRECTX12 and LLGL. Six renderers skip those legs by declaring that their skinned and PBR families do not accept a stride-20 `VertexPositionTexture` stream. Vulkan was on the accepting side, which is what made the legs run and fail. **What was actually wrong was not the missing default; it was that Vulkan's answer was inconsistent.** Its PBR pair refused by name; its skinned family reduced the stride to 52, read past the record and drew **nothing** while reporting success -- the defect this row's investigation found, F-25, fixed as `VULKAN-156`. With all three families refusing, this renderer belongs in the list, and the boundary comment there now says so and why. **Why not the defaults buffer, stated as a decision:** it would make Vulkan the *only* renderer that renders this fixture, i.e. reproduce a behaviour EasyGL itself declines, invent a normal and a bone palette the caller never supplied, and add a permanent second vertex binding to every stock pipeline to do it. The row's own question (a) -- "is this XNA's behaviour or GL's?" -- answers itself once the constant is read: it is neither; it is a fixture convenience that most renderers decline. **The guard is not weakened** (question (b)): a *declared* element the program cannot bind is still refused, and a declaration that supplies every input is still honoured whatever the stride -- `Vulkan_DeclaredEffectLayout`'s leg D2 draws PbrEffect at stride 64 for exactly that reason. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource` is **20/20 legs, 0 crashed**, and `ctest -R '^Vulkan_'` is **228/228** -- the dedicated suite is green for the first time since this plan opened. **A skip is not a pass, and this row does not pretend otherwise:** the six skipped family legs are a declared boundary, and the behaviour behind them is asserted positively elsewhere, by `Vulkan_UnsuppliedInputContract` (`VULKAN-156`), which requires each family to *either* render the sampled texel *or* refuse by name and fails if one silently draws nothing. That pairing is the reason the boundary declaration is honest rather than convenient. **Note for `VULKAN-476`:** a green `^Vulkan_` run is one row of the Phase 13 gate, not the gate. |
| VULKAN-149 | Drive the instanced pipeline from the declaration | ✅ | **Converted, and it moved the *program* choice as well as the offsets -- which is the half the row's own text did not anticipate.** `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D` takes the layout, overwrites its per-vertex attributes when it is complete and carries its hash in the key. The per-INSTANCE binding is untouched: its four matrix columns are a separate stream at locations 4..7 on binding 1, appended after the applicator runs (which overwrites from index 0 and resets the count, so ordering there is load-bearing), and `MultiStreamVertexInput` stays `false`. **`PackedColorOffsetForStride` was not only an offset table.** It decided *which of the two vertex shaders ran* -- `instanced_colored3d` at strides 16 and 24, `instanced3d` everywhere else -- so converting the offsets alone would have left REMED-GFX-234's defect in place in both directions: a Position+Colour record at any other stride loses its colour, and a padded position-only record at 16 gets a colour attribute aimed at its own padding. The declaration now answers both, and the shape is read back off the layout it produced (one attribute is position-only, two is coloured -- there is no third shape). An **absent** declaration keeps the stride table, and a declaration that leaves one of the two inputs unsupplied falls back to the stride for the shape *and* the attributes together, because a shader picked from the declaration and fed from the table is the worst of both. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DeclaredInstancedLayout` (`vulkan_declared_instanced_layout_test.cpp`), **7/7**. Every record carries a **decoy** colour at offset 12 -- the offset the stride table would have used -- in a different colour from the real one, so a stride-driven implementation cannot agree with a declaration-driven one by accident. Leg A is a stride-20 record with `Color0` declared at 16 (the stride table lists 20 as position+texture, with no colour in it at all); leg B is *the same bytes* with no declaration, which must stay position-only; leg D is a stride-16 declaration naming Position only, which must render white rather than its own padding. **Mutation:** with the builder stubbed to return an empty layout, A is **refused** by the fidelity guard verbatim (`the declaration carries Color0@16 Color, which this renderer's native layout for a 20-byte record does not bind at all`), D renders the decoy `(30,230,30)`, and the cache separates two stride-16 declarations into 1 pipeline instead of 2 -- 3/7. **Regression:** `Vulkan_DrawInstanced_3Instances` and `Vulkan_InstancedVertexColor_Cardinality` green; the shared `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest`/`DeclarationGuardTest`/`IndexedDrawDeferredTest`/`*Instanced*` set is 86 passed / 16 skipped / 0 failed. **Suite:** measured below. |
| VULKAN-153 | Stop `CnaInputTests` from being able to hang a whole `ctest` run (F-22) | ✅ | **Split on contact: this row is now the containment half only, and the deadlock itself is `VULKAN-157`.** The two are separable, the containment is worth having whether or not the deadlock is ever diagnosed, and the deadlock needs a stack this machine cannot currently take (**no `gdb` installed**), so bundling them would have parked a one-line fix behind a tooling problem. **Done:** `cmake/UnitTests.cmake`'s registration gains `TIMEOUT 1200`. It had **none**, which is why `ctest` let it run 45 minutes and the `VULKAN-148` baseline could only be finished by killing the process by hand; every other long suite here is bounded. 1200s is generous against the measured range -- **221 s** run alone, **628 s** as the slowest healthy run seen under `-j6`. **Evidence:** the generated `cmake-build-vulkan/CTestTestfile.cmake` line for the test now reads `... LABELS "input" SKIP_RETURN_CODE "77" TIMEOUT "1200" ...`, where before it had no `TIMEOUT` property at all -- the `CTestTestfile.cmake` diff this row's acceptance asked for. **Already exercised in anger:** the `VULKAN-152` full run reaped it at the timeout (`8773 - CnaInputTests (Timeout)`) and finished, instead of stalling. **What this does not do, stated plainly:** it does not fix the deadlock, and a run that hits it still loses that suite's 578 tests. It converts an unbounded stall into a named failure, which is the difference between a baseline that can be taken and one that cannot. |
| VULKAN-163 | Refuse a `Texture3D` binding instead of ignoring it (F-31) | ✅ | **The hole was three times wider than the finding said, and the row's own suggestion about that turned out to be the wrong shape.** It asked me to check whether `BindTextureCube` had the same `{}` default before assuming F-31 was the only hole, and to give a sibling its own row if so. Both siblings do — `VulkanEffectRenderer` overrode **none** of `IEffectRenderer`'s three `Bind*` methods, so `ShaderEffect::SetTexture` was accepted and discarded for **Texture2D, TextureCube and Texture3D alike** — but they are not separate defects. They are one missing override, closed by one fix; three rows would have described one line of code. Recorded here rather than followed blindly. **What changed:** all three are overridden to throw a named `NotSupportedException` that says what this renderer does instead — it supplies a custom effect's texture from the `SpriteBatch` draw that uses it, and has no per-unit binding path. **Nothing regressed because nothing worked:** no test in the suite called `SetTexture` before this row's own, and the call did nothing when it was called, so refusing can only turn a silent nothing into a loud one. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SamplerAddressW` grows 6/6 → **7/7**; its leg C stops characterising today's behaviour and asserts the refusal, exactly as that leg's comment predicted it would, and a second assertion covers the `Texture2D` overload — because a test that only covered the one the finding named would leave the other two to be rediscovered. **`VULKAN-164` is unaffected**: refusing loudly is not sampling, and a renderer that wants `sampler3D` still has to grow one. |
| VULKAN-164 | Give the Vulkan renderer a `Texture3D` sampling path (F-31) | ⬜ | **The real feature, and it is a feature rather than a fix — `Texture3DStorage` is what this renderer claims and what it delivers.** `VulkanTexture3DRenderer` would need a sampled image view, a descriptor binding for `sampler3D`, and the effect renderer's `BindTexture3D` wired to it; Task 863 implemented the EasyGL side only and said so. **Acceptance:** `VULKAN-094`'s originally proposed test becomes writable — a `Texture3D` sampled with `AddressW` wrap versus clamp at a W coordinate outside `[0,1]` gives two different pixels — and the capability profile gains whatever row describes sampling as distinct from storage. **Not urgent:** no XNA stock effect samples a volume, so this is reachable only from a custom `ShaderEffect`. `VULKAN-163` should land first, so until this exists the attempt fails loudly. |
| VULKAN-162 | Narrow the pipeline key's sample mask to the bits that can matter (F-30) | ✅ | **An unbounded cache axis closed without evicting anything, which is the point: a bound you get by not creating the entries beats a bound you get by destroying them.** `NarrowSampleMaskEXT(mask, samples)` masks off every bit above the sample count, and the key term at all **34** sites is now the narrowed value. **Both cautions the row named were real.** The sample count used is the one *that pipeline* was built with (`msaa ? SampleCountToInt(sampleCount_) : 1`), never the device maximum — three sites take it from a parameter instead of the member, and one of them was caught only by the compiler refusing `sampleCount_` in a scope that has `sampleCount`. And `ms.pSampleMask` is set from the **same** narrowed value, compared against all-ones **in the narrowed space**, so a fully-set mask still leaves the pointer null — Vulkan's own default, and byte-identical to before — while what the key says can never drift from what the pipeline does. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_PipelineCacheBound` grows 5/5 → **7/7**, and its leg C stops being a measurement and becomes an assertion: 32 distinct masks now collapse onto **one** non-MSAA pipeline, where `VULKAN-394` measured **+30**. **The leg that keeps the narrowing honest** is the one next to it: a mask clearing bit 0 must still get its OWN pipeline, because at one sample it selects nothing — and the test asserts both that it does and that the frame then draws nothing. A size-only assertion would have accepted a narrowing that merged two pipelines which really do differ. **Mutation:** with the narrowing returning the mask unchanged, the same sweep gives `4 -> 34`. |
| VULKAN-160 | Bound the sampler cache with eviction (F-29) | ✅ | **Bounded at 256 with LRU eviction, routed through the existing retirement queue.** The bound is chosen to never bite on real content and always bite on the unbounded key: 9 filters × 3 address modes cubed × a few anisotropy values is a few hundred, and every device measured here allows 32768 live samplers. **LRU rather than arbitrary, and that is not decoration:** the pathological case is a game sweeping a LOD bias, where the entries worth keeping are exactly the handful it keeps returning to, so each cache entry carries a `lastUsed` stamp ticked on hit and on insert. **Where it runs is the whole safety argument.** `TrimSamplerCacheEXT` is called at the one instant both gates are open — where `RecordCommandBuffer` clears the frame's pending queues. The CPU gate: the queues are empty, so no unrecorded draw still holds a sampler handle. The GPU gate: nothing is destroyed, it is **retired**, and `ProcessRetiredResources` frees it only once the frame that could reference it has provably completed — `RetiredResources` gained a `samplers` vector for exactly that. That is the same two-gate reasoning the MRT-proxy list spells out. **Samplers a device slot currently points at are pinned whatever their age**, along with `defaultSampler_`; those are handles the next draw will use. **Every single-sampler descriptor set keyed on an evicted sampler dies with it** — the same reverse lookup `EvictSampledViewFromCaches` performs for a dying view, with the other half of the `(view, sampler)` key. **The seven per-frame effect caches are the one place a reverse lookup is impossible**, because `EffectDescSetEntry` records the views an entry references (REMED-GFX-076) but not the samplers — those are only hashed into the key. Rather than widen seven caches and their insert sites for a path that runs only past the bound, **every entry is dropped when any sampler is evicted**. It costs one frame of re-population, for a game that has already exceeded 256 distinct sampler states. That trade is recorded here rather than left to be rediscovered. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SamplerCacheBound` grows 7/7 → **9/9** with leg F, which needs **both** of its assertions: sweeping **512** distinct states leaves the cache holding exactly **256**, *and* a draw afterwards still samples the right texel — a bound that evicted a sampler the next draw needed would pass the first and fail the second. Zero validation messages, so nothing dangles where the layer can see it. **Mutation:** with the trim disabled the same sweep leaves **577** entries. |
| VULKAN-161 | Make sampler exhaustion loud instead of white (F-29) | ✅ | **`GetOrCreateSamplerEXT` refuses by name instead of returning `VK_NULL_HANDLE`, and the three callers that turned that null into a substituted resource are gone with it.** The old comment read `// caller keeps whatever it already had`, and the row's own note wondered whether the slot setter's reading of it was reasonable. **It was not, and that is the part worth recording:** `RebuildSlotSamplerEXT` kept the PREVIOUS slot sampler, so a game that had just assigned a new `SamplerState` would have gone on rendering with the old filtering and addressing — a silent wrong picture, not a graceful degradation. The compiled-effect path substituted `defaultSampler_`, running a pass with filtering its own `sampler_state` block did not ask for. The descriptor path substituted `defaultWhiteDescSet_`, which is F-06's shape exactly. All three are removed; the function now returns a real sampler or throws, so the guards were dead code once it did. **The message names the numbers a reader needs**: the `VkResult`, how many samplers were live, and what the device allows. **The one guard deliberately kept** is `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet`'s null check, and its comment now says why the two nulls it catches mean different things and that neither is an allocation failure any more — a null *view* is an untextured draw and white is intended; a null *sampler* means the slot has never been given a sampler state, because `slotSamplers_` is zero-initialised and only `ApplySampler*` fills it. Left as one branch because both want the same answer, documented so nobody reads it as "any problem here draws white". **Reaching the arm at all:** the device allows **32768** live samplers, so exhausting it for real means creating tens of thousands — slow, and on a driver that over-delivers, unreachable. `SetSamplerCreationFailuresForTestEXT` injects it, the same shape `VULKAN-390` used for the descriptor pool. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SamplerCacheBound` grows 4/4 → **7/7** with leg E: the refusal is by name, the failed creation leaves **nothing** behind in the cache, and the same draw succeeds once the injection is disarmed — without that last one a test could pass by leaving the renderer wedged. **Mutation:** restoring the null return and the slot setter's guard, the injected failure produces `the draw SUCCEEDED with a sampler creation failure injected` — the defect, stated by the test in its own words. |
| VULKAN-159 | Make the platform destructor safe against `exit()` from inside its own lock (F-28) | ✅ | **Answered with option (a) — track the owning thread — and the two rejected options are recorded so neither is rediscovered as a shortcut.** **The hazard:** `~Sdl3Platform` locks `SdlGlobalStateMutex()`, and ten methods hold that same non-recursive mutex across calls into SDL. `exit()` runs static destructors **on the calling thread**, so reaching it from inside any of them makes the destructor re-lock a mutex its own thread already owns, and it never returns. Captured with `gdb` (2026-09-05): `pthread_mutex_lock ← ~Sdl3Platform ← exit ← _XDefaultError ← _XError ← XSync ← X11_PumpEvents ← SDL_CreateWindow ← Sdl3Platform::CreateWindow`. `VULKAN-154` removed the one route that was firing; this row is the hazard underneath, which outlives that route. **Done:** every acquisition of the mutex — six in `Sdl3Platform.cpp`, four in `Sdl3GraphicsServices.cpp` — now goes through a new `SdlGlobalStateLock` that records the owning thread beside the mutex, and `~Sdl3Platform` skips re-locking when `SdlGlobalStateHeldByThisThread()` says it already owns it. **Routing *every* site through that type is the load-bearing part, not a tidy-up:** a single surviving raw `std::lock_guard` on the same mutex would leave the owner unset and turn the destructor's check into a silent lie, which is worse than the deadlock because it would skip the lock while another thread genuinely held it. **Why skipping is safe there and only there:** the thread that owns the mutex is this one, so there is nothing to exclude, and every *other* thread remains shut out by the lock this thread still holds. **(b) rejected:** `std::recursive_mutex` is one line and silently sanctions re-entrancy in the ten methods that must not have it. **(c) not taken:** leaking the platform singleton would also work and is the usual answer for a process-global, but it changes shutdown semantics for every host, where (a) changes only the destructor's behaviour in the one situation that is already pathological. **Test:** `Sdl3PlatformExitUnderLockTest`, three cases in a forked child because the subject is a process that ends and the failure mode is a **hang**, not a crash — the parent waits with its own deadline and reports the failure itself rather than leaving `ctest`'s TIMEOUT to kill the binary with no verdict. The **control** child does everything the subject does except hold the lock: if the platform could not be constructed in this environment at all, both would behave alike and the subject's success would mean nothing, so holding the lock is the only difference between them and a subject-only hang names it exactly. A third case asserts the ownership query directly, because a query that answered "held" unconditionally would make the fix look correct while disabling the mutex for every ordinary destruction. **Mutation-checked, and the first attempt failed that check** — which is the part of this row most worth keeping. With the unconditional re-lock restored, the subject child *passed*: the child had held the lock through a **static** `SdlGlobalStateLock`, and statics are destroyed in reverse order of construction, so the guard was released **before** `~Sdl3Platform` ran. The test was asserting against a defect it had already stepped around. Making the guard a plain local and calling `exit()` from inside its scope — `exit()` does not unwind, so it is never released — is what makes the child faithful. Re-checked afterwards: with the defect restored the subject hangs and is failed by the parent's own deadline after 19.5 s while the control still passes in 42 ms; with the fix all three pass in 85 ms. **Registration:** none needed — `gtest_discover_tests` picks the three up as their own ctest cases, and they need no display, since the child creates the platform without acquiring video. **One boundary gate fired and was followed rather than bypassed:** the configure-time non-production SDL audit refused the new test as an unclassified SDL-referencing file. It is registered in `tools/platform/nonproduction_sdl_budget.json` as `platform-sdl3-integration` with a ceiling of **1** reference — the tightest entry in that category. Running `--update` also dropped ten stale entries: nine `skia/examples/*` files that no longer exist after that renderer's retirement, and `directx10_smoke_test.cpp`, which still exists but now references SDL **zero** times, so its removal is the tool tightening to a real floor. Each was checked individually rather than accepted because the tool wrote them. **Evidence:** the three new cases pass in 85 ms; full `ctest` **9087/9106**, with **19** failures — three FEWER than the previous run and **none new**, the three that vanished being the known contention flakes. That matters more than the count here: this row changed ten lock acquisitions in a layer every renderer shares, so "no platform test regressed" is the claim worth making, and the whole suite is what supports it. **Owner:** still the platform layer. `plans/plan_platform.md` has no row for it (checked 2026-09-06), so it was done here; if that plan adopts the area, this row should move rather than be duplicated. |
| VULKAN-158 | The record stride must reach the vertex binding and the pipeline key (F-26) | ✅ | **Found while implementing `VULKAN-150`, when its own fixture -- a stride-40 dual-texture record -- rendered black, and the cause turned out not to be the dual-texture shader at all.** An inventory of all fifteen factories' `VkVertexInputBindingDescription` initialisers found three baking a constant where the declaration-driven conversion had already made the offsets the caller's: `EnvMap3D` and both `Skinned3D` variants. `GetOrCreatePipelineEnvMap3D` gained a `stride` parameter to be able to bind one. **The two `LitTextured3D` factories keep their constant, and the reason is written where they are:** the lit-textured route is selected by `stride == 32` and by nothing else, so the record's stride *is* 32 whenever they are reached -- with a note that widening the selection rule makes the constant the same defect. **The key half:** the record stride is now folded into the cache key on the seven converted factories that did not already do it, and only when the layout is complete, so every stride-derived key is byte-identical to before. **`GetOrCreatePipelineFogColored3D` was deliberately left alone, and this is the row's sharpest lesson:** it already folds the raw stride unconditionally (`VULKAN-146`), the fold is an XOR, and a second one **cancels the first**. Adding it there collapsed two strides onto one pipeline and turned four `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest` cases red -- while `ctest -R '^Vulkan_'` stayed **green**. That is F-21 happening a second time to the same plan, caught only because §8's rule was followed. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_RecordStrideBinding` (`vulkan_record_stride_binding_test.cpp`), **6/6**. Every leg probes **two widely separated pixels** -- `(8,8)` and `(56,56)` -- because one vertex being right is precisely the symptom, and a single centre probe would have passed leg A. Leg C is PbrEffect at stride 64, the **control**: that factory always bound the record's own stride, so it must pass before and after. Leg D asserts that a second stride with identical element offsets builds its own pipeline. **Mutation:** with `recordStride` pinned to the baked constant and the key folds disabled, A reads `(8,8)=(0,0,0) (56,56)=(230,30,30)` -- half a quad -- B reads black at both, D's cardinality reads `3 -> 3`, and C still passes: **3/6**. **Regression:** `-R '^Vulkan_'` and the shared `VertexDeclarationLayoutTest`/`DeclarationGuardTest` suites, measured below. **Unblocks `VULKAN-150`.** |
| VULKAN-157 | Diagnose the `CnaInputTests` deadlock (F-22, the half `VULKAN-153` did not fix) | ✅ | **Diagnosed, and it was not a deadlock in the input suite at all -- it is F-27, the same Xlib defect as F-23, fixed by `VULKAN-154`.** The row said the first step was to get a stack and that there was no `gdb` on this machine; the owner installed one on 2026-09-05, and the very first attach answered it. **Reproduction, which the row also lacked:** run **two copies of `CnaInputTests` concurrently** -- `--gtest_shuffle --gtest_repeat=5`, on `:99`. One of the two hung within ~11 minutes on the first attempt, which is what made the stack available. That is a recipe worth keeping: single-copy runs passed 5/5 and told us nothing. **The stack settled every suspect the row listed.** It is not udev, not a fork in a threaded process, not a static-destructor race: it is `exit()` called by Xlib from inside `Sdl3Platform::CreateWindow`'s own critical section, re-entering `~Sdl3Platform` which locks the same non-recursive mutex. See F-27 for the full frame list. **`VULKAN-153`'s `TIMEOUT` stays, and is still the right thing to have:** it bounds any future stall whatever its cause, and it is what made this session's baselines finishable while the cause was unknown. **Verified against the reproduction that produced it:** three rounds of two concurrent copies -- **6/6 runs finished clean**, where the pre-fix attempt hung one of the first two within ~11 minutes. Six clean runs of a race is evidence, not proof, and this row says so; what makes it more than that is the stack, which names a mechanism that no longer exists. **One thing this row deliberately does NOT do, and it is recorded rather than done:** the underlying hazard -- a platform destructor that blocks on a lock its own thread may already hold -- is still there for any *other* route to `exit()`. That is F-28 / `VULKAN-159`, and it belongs to the platform layer rather than to this plan. |
| VULKAN-154 | Do not let one X protocol error end a whole test binary (F-23) | ✅ | **Done, and it closes `VULKAN-157` with it, because `gdb` showed they were one defect (F-27).** Part (a), containment, is implemented exactly as this row proposed: `Sdl3Platform` installs a non-fatal Xlib error handler as soon as the video subsystem is up, so a protocol error is **recorded and returned from** instead of ending the process. Xlib's default handler calls `exit()`; that is the whole mechanism behind both findings. **Part (b), the request itself, turned out to be the less important half** -- the failing request is a `BadWindow` in a create/destroy race between two X clients, and it is not CNA's to prevent; what was CNA's is that one such error cost 578 tests or an unbounded stall. **Deliberately NOT touched: `XSetIOErrorHandler`.** An I/O error means the connection is genuinely gone and that handler must not return; only the protocol-error hook is installed. **Resolved through `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT, "XSetErrorHandler")`, not by linking Xlib** -- libX11 is loaded only when SDL chose its x11 driver, and a diagnostic must not add an X11 build dependency. A no-op on every other driver. **Evidence:** new test `Sdl3XErrorHandlerTests.cpp` and its own CTest `CnaPlatformXErrorHandlerTests`, registered with `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11` and the virtual display forced -- it is the one platform test that needs a **real** X connection rather than SDL's dummy driver, and the gtest-discovered copy in the shared binary would inherit the shell's `DISPLAY` (F-22's own lesson). It issues a deliberate `X_ChangeProperty` against a resource id that cannot be a window, forces delivery with `XSync`, and asserts three things: the process is still running, the **decoded** error is `error_code=3` (BadWindow) / `request=18` (X_ChangeProperty) / `resource=0xdeadbeef`, and the connection is still usable -- another real window is created and destroyed afterwards. **Surviving is not a weak assertion here: crashing IS the defect**, and before the fix this test could not have reported a verdict at all. **A mistake the test caught immediately:** the handler mirrors `XErrorEvent` so the platform needs no X11 headers, and the first version transposed `resourceid` and `serial` -- it printed a perfectly plausible message with the resource id in the serial field. Asserting the decoded VALUES rather than merely that something was printed is what turned that into a one-minute fix. |
| VULKAN-155 | The legacy `DrawColoredPrimitives` pair must not mis-read a non-16 stride (F-24) | ✅ | **Reproduced first, then fixed -- in that order, because F-24 was read from source and a plan that fixes an unreproduced defect is guessing.** **Measured before any change:** a stride-20 `Position@0 Vector3, Color@12 Color` record through `DrawColoredPrimitives` drew `(0,0,0)` -- the clear colour -- where the same record at stride 16 drew `(230,30,30)`; `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` did the same. No exception, no validation message. F-24's reading was right and its severity was if anything understated: it is not a subtly wrong picture, it is no picture. **Answer (a), the row's own recommendation:** `RequireLegacyColoredStrideEXT` refuses a stride that is not 16, by name, naming the stride it got and what to use instead (`Use DrawPrimitivesEx, whose layout comes from the VertexDeclaration.`). **Why not (b), the declaration-driven layout the other eleven factories have:** these two entry points take no `GpuDrawParams`, so the layout machinery is not reachable from them; the pair has **no production caller** (`DrawPrimitivesEx`'s interface default falls back to it, and Vulkan overrides `DrawPrimitivesEx`); and D3D9 refuses the same stride by name. Adding a binding path nothing calls, to a route the interface documents as a colored-vertex convenience, is work spent to make a legacy surface more capable than the modern one. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_LegacyColoredStride` (`vulkan_legacy_colored_stride_test.cpp`), **5/5**. Leg A and the first half of C are the stride-16 controls -- the behaviour that must not change; legs B and C assert the contract as *refuse by name OR render the same colour*, never a different picture, so the test does not bake in the refusal as if it were the only acceptable answer. **Mutation:** the two guard calls removed, B and C fail with `drew (0,0,0) from a 20-byte record read at 16 bytes` -- which is how the defect was measured in the first place. **Regression:** `-R '^Vulkan_'` is **229/229**. |
| VULKAN-156 | `SkinnedEffect` must refuse a stride it cannot bind instead of drawing nothing (F-25) | ✅ | **Found while scoping `VULKAN-152`, by asking what each family actually does with the fixture that row is about rather than what the failing test reports.** `RequireSkinnedStrideEXT` now refuses a stride that is neither 52 nor 56 **by name**, at the draw, and both factories refuse it again -- `VULKAN-148`'s lesson: gating only the draw-time helper moves the refusal to `Present()`. A declaration that supplies every input of the shader is **not** held to the list, exactly as `VULKAN-147` made possible, so this narrows nothing that a real declaration can express. **Why refuse rather than default the missing inputs:** EasyGL, D3D9, D3D11, D3D12, LLGL and WebGPU all decline this same fixture (`rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp`'s `kSkinnedFamiliesAcceptPositionTexture`), CNA's platform contract says an unsupported combination refuses explicitly, and the alternative -- inventing a normal, a bone palette and weights -- renders a picture the caller did not ask for. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_UnsuppliedInputContract`, **6/6**, which states the contract as the thing it asserts: a family either renders the sampled texel or refuses by name, and *drawing nothing while reporting success is not a third option*. Its `A0` leg is a harness control -- the same bytes with a declaration -- because the first version of this file was wrong in a way worth recording: it built the fixture by `SetDataRaw(quad, 6, 20)` over an array of `VertexPositionTexture`, whose `sizeof` is **32**, not 20; every vertex but the first came from the middle of its predecessor and *every* family drew nothing. The control leg is what distinguishes a broken harness from a broken renderer, and it is why the records are now built byte by byte. **Regression:** `-R '^Vulkan_'` is **227/228**, the one failure being `VULKAN-152`; the shared `*Skinned*`/`*VertexDeclaration*`/`*DeclarationGuard*`/`*StockEffect*`/`*Model*` set is 580 passed / 6 skipped / 1 failed, that one being `CnjStockEffectTest.CustomGlslEffectStillWorks`, which fails identically at `dc0cb2057`. **Consequence for `VULKAN-152`:** all three of Skinned, Pbr and SkinnedPbr now refuse the fixture, which is precisely the boundary six other renderers already declare, so that row's answer changes -- see it. |

---

## 17. Phase 5 — Textures (`VULKAN-170`–`VULKAN-209`)

The block-compressed and packed-format rows are one coherent feature, split into independently
committable pieces. They are **not** the modern engine layer's float-format work (`MOD-2223`), which
stays out of scope.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-170 | Implement `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` on Vulkan, answering from `vkGetPhysicalDeviceFormatProperties` | ✅ | **Done, and the row's own test criterion turned out to be unable to fail — which is written down here rather than papered over, because the three rows that depend on this one need to know it.** **Done, part 1 — the classification.** `VulkanRenderer::ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` now answers in two gates, in order: a format with no entry in this renderer's storage table is **`Defer`**, never `Unsupported` (the framework's own rule applies then, and claiming to have judged a format never looked at would be the same lie in the other direction); a format that *is* in the table is decided from the physical device's real `VkFormatProperties`, requiring `SAMPLED_IMAGE`|`TRANSFER_DST` in `optimalTilingFeatures` — exactly the usage `VulkanTextureRenderer` creates every image with, so a verdict can never name something `vkCreateImage` would refuse. **Done, part 2 — the table, and why it is the substance of this row.** `VulkanRenderer::MapSurfaceFormatToStorageEXT` is the single place saying which `SurfaceFormat` has native storage and in which `VkFormat`, and **`VulkanTextureRenderer`'s constructor now reads it** instead of the `VK_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM` and `* 4` it had hardcoded in five places (image format, view format, and three staging sizes/strides). With `Color` the only entry this is byte-for-byte the old behaviour; what changed is that the verdict and the allocation can no longer disagree, because they are the same table. That is the blocker `VULKAN-172`/`-173`/`-174` were waiting on. **Done, part 3 — F-11.** `VulkanTextureRenderer::GetSurfaceFormatEXT()` reports the ordinal the `ImageData` carried, and `GetVkFormatEXT()` reports the native format it was allocated in. **Done, part 4 — the refusal names itself, which it did not.** The row required *“an unsupported-on-this-device format still refuses by name”*. Measured: it did not. The shared `Unsupported` arm threw `"Texture2D SurfaceFormat has not passed the renderer's promotion gate."` — no format, no device (`Texture2D.cpp:120`), while the sibling `Defer` arm one branch down has always named the ordinal. It now reads `"Texture2D: SurfaceFormat <n> has not passed the renderer's promotion gate -- this renderer classified it Unsupported on this device."` A shared one-line change; nothing matched the old text. **Test:** new CTest `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` (`vulkan_surface_format_classification_test.cpp`), **37/37**. It sweeps **all 27** `SurfaceFormat` values, keeps the `GraphicsProfile`'s own eleven Reach refusals separate from the renderer's verdict (different question, different exception type), and asserts per format that `Supported`⇒constructs, `Unsupported`⇒refused by the renderer *and named*, `Defer`⇒follows the framework rule. Nothing in it lists which formats are expected to be supported, so the rows that add formats need no edit here. Verdicts today: **1 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 26 Defer**. **The `Unsupported` arm is otherwise unreachable, so it is forced.** Every driver this renderer runs on reports `SAMPLED_IMAGE|TRANSFER_DST` for `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`, so the only format it stores is the only one it could refuse and no test could ever see the refusal. New `SetSurfaceFormatUnsupportedForTestEXT(ordinal)` forces it — the same instrument, and the same justification, as `VULKAN-215`'s `SetDepthFormatPreferredUnsupportedForTestEXT` — with controls on both sides (leg I1 before, I5 after) so the injection cannot be mistaken for the refusal. **What this test CANNOT do, measured rather than assumed, and it changes what the next three rows must contain.** Two mutations were tried and **passed**: (a) adding `Bgr565` to the table without any storage work, and (b) hardcoding a different `VkFormat` in the constructor. Neither is caught, for one reason — `Texture2D::GetData` is served from the shared CPU shadow (`cpuPixels_`), and nothing in a construct-and-read test observes the GPU at all. A third mutation (the renderer claims nothing, always `Defer`) **does** fail it, on legs G, I2, I3 and I4. **So: this row's acceptance is met and its sweep is a contract test, not a storage test. `VULKAN-172`/`-173`/`-174` each need a PIXEL test — upload, draw, read the backbuffer — because a construction test cannot tell a format that is stored from a format that is claimed.** **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **255/255**, 0 `[Vulkan Validation]` lines. Full `ctest` in §7.5; it is mandatory here because `modules/graphics/src/Xna/Texture2D.cpp` is shared by every renderer.
| VULKAN-171 | Implement `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` and `ClassifyColorTransferFormatEXT` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Both halves done, in different rows, and the render-target answer is deliberately EIGHT formats narrower than the texture one.** **`ClassifyColorTransferFormatEXT` was implemented by the rows that needed it:** `VULKAN-174` overrode it for the signed-normalized pair and `VULKAN-172` extended it to the block-compressed three, both refusing a `Color`-shaped transfer the framework's byte-width rule would have admitted. Nothing was left for this row there. **`ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` is the substance.** The row's instruction — *“answer it from `VK_FORMAT_FEATURE_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_BIT` rather than reusing `VULKAN-170`'s answer”* — is right and the reason is sharper than renderability merely being narrower: **`VulkanRenderTargetRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` create their colour image in `owner_->swapchainFormat_`, unconditionally.** The requested `SurfaceFormat` reaches neither constructor. So `Color` is the only format this renderer genuinely renders into, and it is answered from the device's real `VkFormatProperties` for that swapchain format — `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_BIT` **and** `SAMPLED_IMAGE_BIT`, because every render target here is also created `USAGE_SAMPLED_BIT` so it can be read back and sampled. Everything else is `Defer`. **What the sweep exists to stop, stated as a number:** storability is **9** formats and renderability is **1**. Reusing the texture verdict would claim the packed 16-bit trio, the signed-normalized pair and the three block-compressed formats as render targets — nothing renders into a BCn surface at all, and the other five would be silently substituted for the swapchain format, which is precisely the *“asked for one format, got another”* `MOD-115` forbids. **Test:** `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` grows **54/54 → 109/109** with a render-target sweep over all 27 formats: per format the verdict must predict `RenderTarget2D` construction, `GraphicsDevice::SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT` must agree with the verdict (that query's whole promise), and one invariant leg asserts renderability is **strictly narrower** than storability — so a future widening is caught even if every individual construction happened to agree. **Mutation-checked:** replacing the body with `return ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT(surfaceFormat)` — the exact shortcut the row warned against — turns it red on **four** legs: `RT Dxt1`, `RT Dxt3`, `RT Dxt5` and the narrowness invariant (`renderability (9) is strictly narrower than storability (9)`). **A shared-layer observation the mutation surfaced, recorded because it is a real trap and is not this plan's to fix.** Under that mutation `RenderTarget2D(dev, 4, 4, false, Dxt1, …)` **constructed**. `RenderTarget2D`'s constructor takes the renderer's `Supported` verdict and breaks out of the switch **without consulting the profile** (`RenderTarget2D.cpp:47`-`59`), so `Texture::IsRenderTargetFormatAllowedByProfileEXT` — which exists precisely to say that nothing renders into a block-compressed surface at either profile — is never applied. `Texture2D`'s constructor asks the profile FIRST and the renderer second. The asymmetry is in `modules/graphics`, not in this renderer, and no Vulkan verdict reaches it today; it belongs to `plans/plan_graphics.md`. This row's sweep is what would catch it here if a future Vulkan change ever did claim one. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **260/260**, render-target verdicts **1 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 26 Defer** against texture verdicts **9 Supported**. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-170` ✅ (and `VULKAN-172`/`VULKAN-174`, which supplied the colour-transfer half).
| VULKAN-172 | Implement block-compressed `Texture2D` storage and transfer (`Dxt1`/`Dxt3`/`Dxt5`) on Vulkan | ✅ | **All three stored natively as BC1/BC2/BC3 and pixel-verified; one of this row's own premises was wrong, and the mutation that was supposed to prove the fix passed instead — which found a better assertion.** **Done: the storage.** `Dxt1` → `VK_FORMAT_BC1_RGBA_UNORM_BLOCK` (8 bytes/block), `Dxt3` → `VK_FORMAT_BC2_UNORM_BLOCK` (16, explicit 4-bit alpha), `Dxt5` → `VK_FORMAT_BC3_UNORM_BLOCK` (16, interpolated alpha). **`BC1_RGBA`, not `BC1_RGB`**: XNA's `Dxt1` carries the 1-bit punch-through alpha the block encoding itself defines, and the RGB spelling would discard it. Conditional on `VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures.textureCompressionBC`, which is now **enabled** at device creation and not merely read — a BCn image on a device where the feature was left false is invalid however encouraging `VkFormatProperties` looks — and then still subject to `VULKAN-170`'s per-format second gate, because the feature promises the family and the properties promise the member. **Done: the sizing, in one place.** The storage description gained `blockExtent` (1 or 4) and a shared `LevelByteCountEXT(storage, w, h)`; the staging allocation, the level upload and the whole-level `UpdatePixels` path all go through it, so the compressed and uncompressed routes cannot drift. **A deliberate divergence from EasyGL, recorded here as §5 requires:** `ClassifyColorTransferFormatEXT` returns **`Unsupported`** for the three, where EasyGL defers. The framework's rule is *“any format whose texel is a multiple of four bytes”*, and `Dxt1`'s reported size is **8** and the others' **16** — all multiples of four, so the rule would admit a `Color`-shaped transfer. Those bytes are **blocks**: a `Color*` upload would write `w*h*4` bytes into an image holding `ceil(w/4)*ceil(h/4)*8`. EasyGL can defer because its storage goes through a decode where a `Color` transfer means something; this renderer stores the blocks themselves, where it means nothing. **The premise this row carried that was wrong:** *“a device without BC support keeps returning false so the shared decode fallback runs.”* **There is no shared decode fallback.** `Texture2D::SetData(const uint8_t*)` routes to `SetCompressedDataBytes` **only** when `IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT` is true, and otherwise demands `ByteEXT` and throws. EasyGL's fallback is *inside EasyGL* — it claims the formats unconditionally and decodes when the driver lacks S3TC. So the honest Vulkan behaviour on a device without BC is to **claim nothing and refuse at construction**, which is what it does; a CPU decoder in this renderer would be a second implementation of EasyGL's, not this row's scope. **The mutation that was supposed to prove it, and did not.** This row's own reasoning — inherited from the test's header — is that *“a mis-sized block shows as a wrong half.”* Measured: doubling `Dxt1`'s block size from 8 to 16 leaves `Vulkan_DxtFormat` at **3/3**. The size never reaches the GPU; it decides only how many bytes the staging copy reads, and the image walks BC1 blocks by its `VkFormat` regardless. So the real consequence of that mistake is a **buffer over-read** of the caller's level bytes — invisible to pixels, exactly the class `VULKAN-130`/`-131` closed for vertex and index buffers. **So a leg was added where the two numbers actually meet:** `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` now asserts, for every format the renderer claims, that its storage description agrees with the **framework's own** arithmetic — `bytesPerTexel == Texture::GetFormatSizeEXT` and `blockExtent² == Texture::GetBlockSizeSquaredEXT`. The shared layer sizes every upload with those; this renderer sizes its staging buffer with its own table; a disagreement is the over-read. **That leg fails the mutation** (`16B x 4^2 vs 8B x 16`) while the pixel test stays green. **Tests, both the ones this row named:** `Vulkan_DxtFormat` — the renderer-agnostic REMED-GFX-244 source, two blocks side by side so a mis-walked block stream shows as a wrong half — **3/3**, zero mismatched texels on the first run. And `Vulkan_Dxt1FromStream` (`dxt1_texture_test.cpp`), which is the **control**: `FromStream` force-decodes block content to `Color` by default, so it proves the native path did not disturb the decode route. Both new registrations. **A third shared source needed the same de-OpenGL-ing `VULKAN-173` began:** `easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp` guarded its three DXT legs on a five-profile GL macro list and its own comment said the Vulkan registration *“stores no block-compressed content”* — true when written, false now. Claim-driven, and the stale sentence is corrected rather than deleted. `Texture2DTests.cpp`'s sweep gains a Vulkan DXT clause **derived from the renderer** for the same reason `Bgra4444`'s is: the promotion is a device fact. **Cross-checked on EasyGL**, since three shared sources changed: `EasyGL_DxtFormat`, `EasyGL_SurfaceFormat_Throws`, `EasyGL_Packed16Format` **3/3**, and EasyGL's own `UnsupportedFormatConstructionTest`/`HiDefFormatConstructionTest` **23/23**. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` **42/42 → 54/54**, verdicts now **9 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 18 Defer**; `^Vulkan_` **260/260**. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-170` ✅. **Non-goal:** a CPU block decoder for devices without `textureCompressionBC`, and `Bc7EXT`/`Dxt5SrgbEXT`, which no row asks for.
| VULKAN-173 | Implement the packed 16-bit formats (`Bgr565`, `Bgra5551`, `Bgra4444`) on Vulkan | ✅ | **Two of the three implemented; the third is refused for a reason that is a fact about this renderer's Vulkan version rather than a gap, and it holds its own row.** **Done: `Bgr565` and `Bgra5551`.** Two entries in `VULKAN-170`'s storage table, nothing else — which is the point of that row: the classification, the image format, the view format and every staging size follow from the table, so a format is offered and allocated in one step. **Component order was the whole risk, and it is derived rather than tried.** XNA's names read the channels in little-endian MEMORY order; Vulkan's read them from the high bit of the packed word down, which is why the two spellings look reversed and describe the same 16 bits. `Bgr565` is `D3DFMT_R5G6B5` — R 11..15, G 5..10, B 0..4 — which is `VK_FORMAT_R5G6B5_UNORM_PACK16` field for field; `Bgra5551` is `D3DFMT_A1R5G5B5` — A 15, R 10..14, G 5..9, B 0..4 — which is `VK_FORMAT_A1R5G5B5_UNORM_PACK16` field for field. Both are **core Vulkan 1.0**, so no extension and no version bump. Both passed on the first run with **zero** mismatched texels. **`Bgra4444` is NOT implemented, and the reason is exact:** its Vulkan spelling `VK_FORMAT_A4R4G4B4_UNORM_PACK16` arrived with `VK_EXT_4444_formats` and is core only in **1.3**, while `VulkanRenderer` requests `VK_API_VERSION_1_1` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2498`). Claiming a format from a version the instance did not ask for is precisely the widening `VULKAN-170`'s two gates exist to stop, so the verdict stays `Defer` and the format refuses by name. **`VULKAN-179`** owns it. **Test: `Vulkan_Packed16Format`, 3/3** — the renderer-agnostic REMED-GFX-244 source, registered here. It uploads through the typed `PackedVector` overloads and **samples the texture in a real draw**, which is exactly what `VULKAN-170` measured a construct-and-read test cannot do: `Texture2D::GetData` is served from the shared CPU shadow and round-trips a completely wrong upload. Full-intensity primaries are used so 31/31, 63/63 and 15/15 expand back to exactly 255 and a correct result is EXACT — no tolerance can hide a swap. **The shared source needed one change, and it is a de-OpenGL-ing rather than an accommodation.** Its per-format expectations were `#if defined(CNA_GL_PROFILE_OPENGLES3) || …` — an OpenGL fact compiled into a file more than one renderer family registers. Each leg now asks the **renderer's own** `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT`: a claimed format must pass the pixel check, an unclaimed one must **refuse by name**, and neither is skipped. So the Bgra4444 leg is an assertion rather than a hole, and the day this renderer claims it the leg becomes a pixel test with no edit. **`easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp` needed the same correction** for the same reason — three `#if defined(CNA_GL_PROFILE_*)` blocks covering `Bgra5551`, `Bgr565`, `Bgra4444`, `NormalizedByte2` and `NormalizedByte4` — and it would otherwise have gone red the moment this row landed, having encoded "not an ES-3 GL profile" as "must throw". Replaced by the same claim-driven helper. **Mutation-checked, and it is the mutation `VULKAN-170` said this row needed:** mapping `Bgr565` to `VK_FORMAT_B5G6R5_UNORM_PACK16` — the channel-reversed twin, which allocates and uploads perfectly happily — turns the leg red with `expected (255,0,0) got (0,0,255)` and `expected (0,0,255) got (255,0,0)` while `Bgra5551` stays green. A construction test would have passed that mutation. **A third shared source had to change, and only the FULL suite found it — which is F-21's lesson arriving again.** `^Vulkan_` was 257/257 while `UnsupportedFormatConstructionTest.Bgra5551Throws` and `…EverySurfaceFormatEitherWorksOrThrowsClearly` (`modules/graphics/tests/…/Texture2DTests.cpp`) went red: both pin each renderer's **promoted format set** by name, and Vulkan's had just grown. Those two are deliberately **not** converted to the claim-driven form the other two files use — deriving `supported` from `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` there would make the sweep a tautology against `Texture2D`'s own gate and stop it asserting anything about the renderer. A `vulkanPacked16` flag was added beside the existing `easyGlPacked16` one instead, separate rather than merged **because the two renderers promote different sets** and merging them would hide exactly that. **Cross-renderer check, because three shared sources changed:** `EasyGL_SurfaceFormat_Throws` and `EasyGL_Packed16Format` — **2/2** — and EasyGL's own `UnsupportedFormatConstructionTest`/`HiDefFormatConstructionTest` — **23/23** — rebuilt and run in `cmake-build-easygl`, all unchanged. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` **37/37 → 39/39** with verdicts now **3 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 24 Defer**; `^Vulkan_` **257/257**. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-170` ✅.
| VULKAN-174 | Implement `NormalizedByte2`/`NormalizedByte4` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Both implemented, both pixel-verified, and the colour-transfer refusal the row asked for is the half that needed an argument rather than a mapping.** **Done: the storage.** Two more entries in `VULKAN-170`'s table — `VK_FORMAT_R8G8_SNORM` and `VK_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_SNORM`, both core Vulkan 1.0, both with the plain component order (X→R, Y→G, Z→B, W→A), so unlike `VULKAN-173`'s packed pair there is no bit-field reasoning that could be wrong. What IS different from every other entry is the **range**: a SNORM texel samples to `[-1, 1]`. **Done: `ClassifyColorTransferFormatEXT`, overridden for the first time on this renderer.** It returns `Unsupported` for both and `Defer` for everything else — the same answer EasyGL gives, for the same reason, and the reason is not the obvious one. The framework's rule is *“any format whose texel is a multiple of four bytes”*, and **`NormalizedByte4` satisfies it** — it is exactly four bytes wide. Those four bytes are signed, so a `Color`-shaped `GetData`/`SetData` over them would read or write the wrong values while looking perfectly well-formed. This is the renderer overriding a width rule with a semantics one. `NormalizedByte2` is named beside it because the pair stands or falls together and because the width rule already excluding it today is not a reason to depend on that. **Test: `Vulkan_NormalizedByteFormat`, 6/6** (`vulkan_snorm_format_test.cpp`). Upload through the typed `PackedVector` overloads, draw through `SpriteBatch` into a `Color` render target, read back. Values are `0.0`/`±1.0` only, which land on bytes `0`/`±127` and sample back exactly, so the comparison needs no tolerance — and a tolerance is where a channel swap hides. **The leg that makes this a SNORM test rather than a texture test:** texel 3 of the four-channel leg is `(-1, -1, -1)`. Under SNORM it samples to `-1` and the `Color` target clamps it to **black**; under UNORM the same byte `0x81` reads as `129/255` and comes back **mid-grey**. So "black" here is a positive result. `NormalizedByte2`'s blue is asserted as **0** for the mirror reason: there is no third byte, and a renderer that quietly stored it four-wide would return the caller's next texel in it. **Mutation-checked, and the mutation is the one that matters:** mapping `NormalizedByte4` to `VK_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM` — which allocates and uploads without complaint — turns the leg red on **all four** texels with `expected (255,0,0) got (127,0,0)` for the positives and `expected (0,0,0) got (129,129,129)` for the negative, exactly the arithmetic above. The `NormalizedByte2` leg stays green, so the failure names the format rather than the feature. **The shared sweep needed teaching again**, the same way `VULKAN-173` taught it: `UnsupportedFormatConstructionTest`'s `NormalizedByte2Throws`/`NormalizedByte4Throws` and `EverySurfaceFormatEitherWorksOrThrowsClearly` pin each renderer's promoted set **by name**, so a `vulkanSignedNormalized` flag joins `vulkanPacked16` — separate rather than merged into EasyGL's, because the two renderers promote different sets and one predicate would stop the sweep saying which. `easygl_surface_format_throws_test.cpp` needed nothing: `VULKAN-173` already converted its two SNORM legs to the claim-driven form, and they flipped on their own. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` verdicts now **5 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 22 Defer**; `^Vulkan_` **258/258**; the shared sweep 23/23 on **both** `cmake-build-vulkan` and `cmake-build-easygl`. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-170` ✅.
| VULKAN-175 | Prove `Texture3D` partial-box **writes** on Vulkan | ✅ | **Registered and green on the first run, no production change — which is a real result and is said as one.** `easygl_texture3d_partial_box_test.cpp` is registered as `Vulkan_Texture3D_PartialBox`. **What was unproven and now is not:** this renderer already proved the READ half (`Vulkan_Texture3D_PartialBox_Readback`, the sibling source registered beside it), while the write half — `vkCmdCopyBufferToImage` with a sub-`imageOffset`/`imageExtent` — was checked by **code inspection only**, and `docs/texture3d-texturecube-support.md:56` said so. A sub-box write that mis-computed its offset would have put the caller's voxels in the wrong place and the existing readback test, which reads what it wrote through the same coordinates, could not have noticed. This one writes a sub-box and reads the WHOLE volume, so an untouched voxel that moved is a failure. **Evidence:** the test's per-voxel legs all pass, `^Vulkan_` **262/262**. Full `ctest` not re-run: only this file's registration changed, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-176 | Prove `TextureCube` per-face round-trip on Vulkan | ✅ | **Registered and green on the first run, no production change.** `easygl_texturecube_faces_test.cpp` is registered as `Vulkan_TextureCube_Faces`: six distinct face payloads written and read back, so no face can alias another. **Why this axis and not another:** this renderer already proved the cube's mip and partial-rect round-trips, which both address ONE face. What had no test was the six **array layers** being addressed independently — and that is the exact axis `VULKAN-406` found undefined for a cube *render target* three rows ago, where the fixture asked for one face and the defect was on all six. A cube whose `SetData`/`GetData` swapped, aliased or clamped a layer would have passed every other cube test in the suite. **Evidence:** all six faces' per-pixel legs pass, `^Vulkan_` **262/262**. Full `ctest` not re-run: only this file's registration changed. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-177 | Prove disposed-texture and bound-resource disposal behaviour on Vulkan | ✅ | **Three tests, and the row's own extra condition — *“the test must reach that eviction, not merely dispose an unused texture”* — turned out to need a fourth thing the two named sources do not provide.** **The two the row named, registered and green on the first run:** `easygl_disposed_resource_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_DisposedResource` (a disposed `Texture2D`, `BasicEffect` and `RenderTarget2D` each throw `ObjectDisposedException` at the public seam) and `easygl_bound_resource_dispose_test.cpp` → `Vulkan_BoundResourceDispose` (8/8: a 2D target, a cube face and an MRT slot each destroyed **while bound**, each unbinding cleanly and each followed by a working fresh bind cycle). **Neither reaches `EvictSampledViewFromCaches`**, which is measured rather than assumed: that function is about the `(VkImageView, VkSampler)` descriptor-set cache, and an entry is only created by **sampling a texture through a non-default `SamplerState`**. The first test disposes unused resources; the second disposes render targets. So the Vulkan-specific half of this row was still uncovered after both. **New CTest `Vulkan_DisposedTextureEviction`** (`vulkan_disposed_texture_eviction_test.cpp`), **6/6**: draw a red texture through `PointWrap` so an entry is cached, dispose it, then draw a blue one — very likely handed the freed `VkImageView` **handle value**, since Vulkan reuses those — and assert it draws as itself. **It needed one new accessor to be able to say anything**, `GetSampledDescriptorSetCacheSizeEXT()`, and that is the substance rather than a convenience: without it a test can only assert that the picture came out right afterwards, **which a renderer that never cached anything would also satisfy**. The measured sequence is **0 → 1 → 0**. **Mutation-checked, and the result is the argument for the accessor.** With `EvictSampledViewFromCaches` removed from `VulkanTextureRenderer::ReleaseVulkanResources`, leg B fails (`1 -> 1`) — and legs **C and D still pass**: the replacement texture drew its own colour and the Khronos layer said nothing. On this driver the stale entry is invisible to both pixels and validation, so a test built only on those two would have reported REMED-GFX-076's exact defect as green. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **265/265**. Full `ctest` not re-run: two registrations, one new test source, and one `const noexcept` accessor that reads a container's `size()`. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-178 | Audit `Texture2D::FromStream` / `SaveAsPng` / `SaveAsJpeg` on Vulkan | ⬜ | These go through readback and upload paths that differ per renderer. Record which are covered on Vulkan today and open rows for those that are not. |
| VULKAN-179 | `Bgra4444` needs a Vulkan version or extension this renderer does not ask for | ✅ | **Done by route (a) — enable `VK_EXT_4444_formats` where the device offers it — and the choice is recorded because this row existed to make it.** **Why (a) and not (b).** Raising the instance's `apiVersion` to 1.3 would move a decision that every other path in this renderer depends on (device selection, feature structs, what the validation layer expects) in order to gain one texture format. The extension leaves all of that untouched, and a device that does not offer it keeps refusing the format **by name** rather than being handed a different layout. Route (c) — rotating channels into the core-1.0 `VK_FORMAT_B4G4R4A4_UNORM_PACK16` the way EasyGL rotates into GL's layout — was not needed, and the mutation below shows what it would have to get right. **Done:** `CreateLogicalDevice` enumerates the device's extensions, and when `VK_EXT_4444_formats` is present it queries `VkPhysicalDevice4444FormatsFeaturesEXT` through `vkGetPhysicalDeviceFeatures2` (core in 1.1, so no second version problem) and enables the extension **only if `formatA4R4G4B4` is actually reported** — an advertised extension whose feature is false permits nothing, and the two are checked separately for exactly that reason. `formatA4B4G4R4` is explicitly left false: CNA names no such format. **`MapSurfaceFormatToStorageEXT` became a member**, which is the structural half of this row. `Bgra4444` is the only conditional entry in the table, and what this renderer stores is therefore a property of the **device it opened**, not of the build — a static function could not express that and would have had to lie in one direction or the other. **Test: none added, by design, and that was `VULKAN-173`'s doing.** `Vulkan_Packed16Format`'s leg C gates on the renderer's own `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` verdict, so the moment this row landed it **flipped by itself** from asserting the refusal to asserting the four sampled colours — 3/3 with zero mismatched texels on the first run. That is the acceptance this row was written with, met without touching a fixture. **Mutation-checked on BOTH branches of the condition, because a conditional feature has two:** (1) mapping `Bgra4444` to the core-1.0 near-twin `VK_FORMAT_B4G4R4A4_UNORM_PACK16` — which allocates and uploads perfectly happily — turns leg C red with `expected (255,0,0) got (0,255,255)` and `expected (0,255,0) got (255,0,255)`, the exact channel rotation the two layouts differ by, while legs A and B stay green; (2) forcing `formatA4R4G4B4Supported_ = false` after the probe — a device without the extension — turns leg C back into the **refusal** assertion, still 3/3, and `UnsupportedFormatConstructionTest.EverySurfaceFormatEitherWorksOrThrowsClearly` follows it. So neither branch is untested and neither can silently become the other. **One line in the shared sweep**: `Bgra4444` is the single entry in `EverySurfaceFormatEitherWorksOrThrowsClearly` derived from the renderer rather than named, because its promotion is a device fact — naming it would be wrong on every device without the extension, omitting it wrong on every device with one. Everything else there stays named, so the sweep does not become a tautology against `Texture2D`'s own gate. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_Packed16Format` **3/3 with all three legs now pixel-verified**; `Vulkan_SurfaceFormatClassification` **39/39 → 42/42**, verdicts **6 Supported, 0 Unsupported, 21 Defer**; `^Vulkan_` **258/258**. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-170` ✅, `VULKAN-173` ✅.
