## 18. Phase 6 — Render targets (`VULKAN-210`–`VULKAN-249`)

Vulkan is the **stronger** renderer in most of this area (§10.6). The tasks here close the few
EasyGL-only rows and audit the parts neither renderer proves.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-210 | Prove the bound-MSAA-target alpha case on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_gfx164_bound_msaa_alpha_test.cpp`. `GFX164` was a real cross-renderer defect (alpha lost when sampling a bound MSAA target's resolve); Vulkan resolves on unbind through its own path and has no equivalent proof. |
| VULKAN-211 | Prove `RenderTargetCube` depth-format fidelity on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_rendertargetcube_depthformat_test.cpp`. `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_DepthFormatFidelity` covers the 2D half (Task 911); the cube half has no test on Vulkan, and `CreateRenderTargetCube` takes the same `depthFormat` parameter. |
| VULKAN-212 | Prove the plain two-attachment MRT case on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_mrt_test.cpp`. Vulkan has `MRT_MixedFormats`, `MRT_MsaaResolve` and the cube-MRT cases but not the plain "one draw writes two attachments with different content" source EasyGL uses. |
| VULKAN-213 | Audit render-target lifetime across deferred work end to end | ⬜ | Vulkan is already strong here (`Vulkan_RenderTarget_GetDataLifetime`, `_DeferredResourceLifetime`, `_BoundTargetLifetime`, `_CubeFaceReadbackDependency`). Audit the remaining shapes: a target disposed while its content is still pending a flush; a target used as a source in the same frame it was written and then disposed; a `RenderTargetCube` face disposed between faces. Each hole found gets a row. |
| VULKAN-214 | Audit `RenderTargetUsage::PreserveContents` across a swapchain recreation | ⬜ | `REMED-GFX-136` fixed cube preserve-contents. A resize destroys and rebuilds render passes; whether a `PreserveContents` target created before a resize still preserves after it is untested. Test: draw into a preserved target, resize the window, draw again, assert the first draw survived. |
| VULKAN-215 | `RenderTargetCube.DepthStencilFormat` reports the request, and no interface lets it report anything else | ✅ | **Fixed at all three levels, and the interface change is additive so no other renderer is touched.** **Observed:** `RenderTarget2D` asked the renderer and stored the answer (`RenderTarget2D.cpp:105`-`111`); `RenderTargetCube` stored the raw constructor argument (`RenderTargetCube.cpp:64`) and asked nobody, because `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` declared no applied-format virtual at all. An override written for the Vulkan cube during `VULKAN-348` failed to compile for exactly that reason. **Done:** `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` gains `GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT` with the **same identity default** its 2D twin has — every renderer that does not substitute keeps today's behaviour without a line changing — `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` overrides it from the cube's own `depthVkFormat_`, and `RenderTargetCube`'s constructor asks it, one line below where it already asks for the multisample count. **The third time this class has lagged its 2D twin the same way**, which is recorded in the code: `MOD-107` was the surface format, the multisample count was the second. **The test could not be written the obvious way, and finding that out is the substance of this row.** The first draft asked for `Depth16` and its **control leg failed**: `PickDepthFormat` honours `Depth16` verbatim wherever `D16_UNORM` is supported, so nothing was substituted and the cube leg would have passed by comparing a request with itself. `Depth24` failed the control too — llvmpipe supports `X8_D24_UNORM_PACK32` as well. **On this device no request is substituted at all**, so no reachable case existed in which the report could differ. New `SetDepthFormatPreferredUnsupportedForTestEXT` forces the fallback, the same way `SetSwapchainOutOfDateForTestEXT` forces a state the window manager owns; it affects render targets only, since the back buffer's `FindDepthFormat()` never offers those two formats. **Without the control leg both earlier drafts would have been reported green and written up as verified.** **Test:** `Vulkan_AppliedFormats` grows 3/3 → **5/5**. Leg D is the control — `RenderTarget2D` must show the substitution, proving the environment really substitutes — and leg E asserts the cube reports what its 2D twin does. **Mutation-checked:** with the cube's ask removed, E fails as `cube 2, flat 3` while D still passes, so the failure names the cube rather than render targets in general. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **250/250**, full `ctest` **9088/9107** with **19** failures — none new, none in `Vulkan_*`, and the two load-sensitive cases from the previous row did not recur. The full suite is mandatory here: the change adds a virtual to a shared renderer interface and alters a shared XNA class. |

## 19. Phase 7 — Effects and shader-driven ordinary rendering (`VULKAN-250`–`VULKAN-299`)

Compiled `.fx` bytecode behaviour is **`plans/plan_fx.md`'s** (`FX-065`, `FX-110`, `FX-112`;
`CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS`). Cross-dialect shader portability is **`plans/plan_csl.md`'s**. This
phase owns Vulkan's own stock-effect and `ShaderEffect` surface.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-250 | Declare the shader dialect the Vulkan renderer actually requires | ✅ | **Declared: `ShaderDialectEXT::GlslVulkan`.** It is not device- or build-dependent -- this renderer has exactly one custom-effect intake and `CompileProgram` rejects anything that is not SPIR-V words. **And the review the row asked for concluded the enumerator is genuinely imprecise, so the coordination row is opened rather than waved at:** `IGL`'s Vulkan backend reports the **same** `GlslVulkan` and takes GLSL **source** (`IglEffectRenderer::CompileProgram` -> `igl::ShaderStagesCreator::fromModuleStringInput`, and `igl_spritebatch_shadereffect_test.cpp` gates on exactly this value before handing it a GLSL string), while this renderer takes compiled **bytecode**. An application that queried the dialect and acted on it would still have to guess which of the two to send -- which is the one thing this query exists to prevent. Narrowing it needs a new enumerator, a C-ABI change through `MapShaderDialect` (`modules/c-api/src/CnaCApiGraphics.cpp:144`), and that is **`VULKAN-264`**. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` (`vulkan_shader_dialect_contract_test.cpp`), **4/4** -- the dialect is declared and is `GlslVulkan`; ordinary Vulkan-flavoured GLSL source of exactly the shape IGL compiles is **refused** here (`SPIR-V size must be a multiple of 4 bytes`) rather than accepted and drawn from nothing; and the ambiguity itself is asserted, so a future enumerator that resolves it turns this leg red and says so instead of leaving a stale comment behind. That a valid SPIR-V pair is accepted and renders is not re-proved -- `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` owns that end to end with a real tinted draw. **Suite:** 219/220. |
| VULKAN-264 | Coordinate a shader-dialect enumerator that distinguishes source from bytecode (F-08's tail) | ⬜ | **Opened by `VULKAN-250`, which measured the ambiguity rather than assuming it.** **Observed:** `ShaderDialectEXT::GlslVulkan` is reported by two renderers that want different payloads -- IGL's Vulkan backend compiles GLSL **source** through `fromModuleStringInput`, CNA's Vulkan renderer requires already-compiled **SPIR-V words** and refuses source by name. Both answers are honest under the enumerator's own wording ("GLSL compiled to SPIR-V"); the enumerator is the thing that is short. **Why it matters:** `GetShaderDialectEXT` exists so an application need not infer the payload from the build identity, and today it cannot answer that question for a Vulkan target. **Intended:** a distinct enumerator (working name `SpirV`) meaning "already-compiled SPIR-V bytecode", with `GlslVulkan` retained for a renderer that compiles Vulkan GLSL itself. **This is a C-ABI change** -- `CNA_ShaderDialect` and `MapShaderDialect` (`modules/c-api/src/CnaCApiGraphics.cpp:144`) -- so it must be agreed with `plans/plan_binding.md`/`plan_bindings_upstream.md` before either side moves, and appending rather than inserting is mandatory so no existing ordinal shifts. **Acceptance:** the new enumerator exists, Vulkan reports it, IGL keeps `GlslVulkan`, `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract`'s leg C is rewritten to assert the *resolved* contract instead of the ambiguity, and the C ABI's own dialect test covers the added value. **Non-goal:** making one `ShaderEffect` source run on both renderers -- that is `plans/plan_csl.md`. **Second piece of evidence, from `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot (2026-09-05):** the capability profile already encodes the right answer and the query cannot. `GetShaderDialectEXT()` returns `GlslVulkan`, while **every** `RendererFeature::ShaderDialect*` row for this renderer reads `unsupported` — correctly, because it consumes SPIR-V bytecode and no source dialect. Two answers to one question at one seam. **Added to the acceptance:** once the enumerator can express "already-compiled SPIR-V", `Vulkan_CapabilitySnapshot` gains the invariant that a non-`Unknown` `GetShaderDialectEXT` agrees with the profile's dialect rows — an assertion deliberately NOT added today, because a red test asserting a contract that does not exist yet is noise rather than evidence. |
| VULKAN-265 | Refuse the four array uniform setters until `VULKAN-252` implements them | ✅ | **Scope, stated precisely because the first draft of this row got it wrong:** `VULKAN-252` has owned *implementing* the array setters since the planning session, and this row does not do that. What `VULKAN-027`'s contract table found is what `VULKAN-252` leaves untouched while it waits — the calls are **silently accepted**, so a caller cannot tell an unimplemented path from a working one. This row closes that interim, and is a predecessor of `VULKAN-252` rather than a duplicate. **Observed:** `IEffectRenderer` declares four array setters whose bodies are `{}` (`IGraphicsRenderer.hpp:931`–`947`). `VulkanEffectRenderer` overrode the six scalar/vector setters (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:373`–`378`) and none of the four, presenting itself as a working effect renderer and then discarding every array upload without a word. **Classification:** implementation bug — a silent no-op on a reachable public call, the same class as F-31. **Reachability, checked rather than assumed:** `ShaderEffect` lives in `modules/graphics`, the XNA-public module; its array setters carry the `CNAEXT` marker but no `CNA_CNAEXT` build guard (`ShaderEffect.hpp:95`–`128`, forwarding at `ShaderEffect.cpp:77`), so the call compiles and runs in every build. **EasyGL evidence:** overrides all four (`EasyGLRenderer.hpp:516`–`522`) and uploads them. **Vulkan evidence:** the `ShaderEffect` payload is a fixed 128-byte push-constant block with fixed slots — one `mat4`, one `vec4`, eight floats (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:450`–`452`) — and no shader reflection, so an array of arbitrary length has nowhere to go. That is why the setters were never written, and it is the same reason `VULKAN-252` says a uniform buffer is required. **Intended behaviour, by §5's authority order:** XNA has no opinion — these are CNA extensions — so the governing rule is this project's own, that unsupported behaviour refuses explicitly rather than pretends (`CLAUDE.md`, Platform Boundary). **Done:** new `RefuseEffectUniformArrayEXT(setter, name, count)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:4074`), deliberately separate from `RefuseEffectTextureBindEXT` because the limit it reports is a different one, and four one-line overrides that call it. **Test, and why it discriminates:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` gains four legs asserting the throw **plus the control that was already there** — the red centre pixel proves the same `fx` still reaches the shader through `SetUniformVec4`, so four throws cannot be explained by an effect that is broken end to end. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the silent no-op on `SetUniformMat4Array` alone turns the test red with `SetUniformMat4Array was accepted and silently ignored` while the control still reports `centre=(255,0,0) bg=(0,255,0)` — so the leg fails for the defect it claims, and for that defect only. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` still passes, `^Vulkan_` **238/238**. **Consequence handed to a new row rather than assumed away:** three CNAEXT passes call these setters, so with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` they now throw where they previously fell silent. That build does not exist here, so it was **not** verified — `VULKAN-266` owns it. **Explicit non-goal:** the uniform-buffer implementation, which stays with `VULKAN-252`. |
| VULKAN-266 | Determine whether the three CNAEXT passes can reach the now-refusing array setters on Vulkan | ⬜ | **Opened by `VULKAN-265`, which changed what those calls do and could not verify the consequence in the build it was written in.** **Observed:** three engine-layer passes call the array setters that `VULKAN-265` turned from silence into a throw — `SsaoPass.cpp:284` (`SetUniformVec3Array("uKernel", …)`), `ShadowMap.cpp:344` (`SetUniformMat4Array("uBones", …)`) and `ClusteredForwardEffect.cpp:351` (`SetUniformVec3Array("uProbeCoefficients", …)`). Before `VULKAN-265` each was a no-op on Vulkan and the pass rendered wrong output without saying so; after it, reaching one of those lines ends the frame with `System::NotSupportedException`. **Classification:** test gap, and possibly an engine-layer bug — not a Vulkan implementation bug, since throwing is the intended answer. **Why it is not already settled:** `ShadowMap` and `ClusteredForwardEffect` each ask one capability query before running; **`SsaoPass` asks none** (zero occurrences of `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`/`SupportsShadowSamplingEXT`/`SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT` in that file), and its `isSupported` gate checks only that its two effects compiled, which they do. Whether `apply()`'s `ready && haveInputs` path is nevertheless unreachable on a renderer reporting `FloatRenderTargets = false` could not be decided by reading, and **was not verified at runtime**: `cmake-build-vulkan` has `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`, so none of this code is compiled there, and `cmake-build-cnaext/` is an EasyGL configuration. **Intended:** either show that all three passes early-out on Vulkan before the setter — in which case record the evidence and close — or, if `SsaoPass` can reach it, fix the missing capability guard in the pass (a `plans/plan_modern.md` change, coordinated there, not a Vulkan change). **Test:** a CNAEXT-enabled Vulkan configuration that runs the SSAO, shadow and clustered-forward passes and asserts each either early-outs or completes; the discriminating part is that it must fail today if `SsaoPass` reaches the throw. **Validation:** configure a `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` Vulkan build, then `ctest -R 'Ssao|ShadowMap|ClusteredForward'`. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-265`. **Explicit non-goal:** implementing array uniforms on Vulkan to make the passes work — that is the feature `VULKAN-265` declined, and it needs `VULKAN-264` first. |
| VULKAN-251 | Prove `ShaderEffect` uniform delivery through `SpriteBatch` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Done with **no new SPIR-V**, which is a simplification found by reading rather than a shortcut — and the row's real requirement turned out to be sharper than "author the twin".** **What the row asked for:** that the drawn colour be a *function* of the uniform, so a dropped uniform fails. **What was already there:** `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` set `uColor` **once**, to red, and asserted the centre was red. That does catch a uniform dropped *entirely* — the shader would draw the untinted white texture and `G <= 50` would fail — but it cannot catch a value delivered to the wrong push-constant slot, or with only one channel arriving, because "reddish" satisfies it either way. One value is not a function. **Done:** two batches with two distinct values into two regions — red on the left, blue on the right — and both are asserted. The existing tint shader already reads `uColor`, so the "author the SPIR-V twin" step the row anticipated was unnecessary; recording that is more useful than quietly skipping it. **Checked before relying on it:** two batches in one frame do not collapse onto the last value, because `End()` copies the effect's push constants into the batch's snapshot (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1355`) — the same snapshot-at-submission discipline `VULKAN-132` measured for vertex buffers. Had that not been so, this test would have been measuring a defect instead of the uniform. **Mutation-checked:** making `SetUniformVec4` write a constant tint regardless of its arguments turns the right-hand region red and fails the leg — **and would have passed the single-value check completely**, which is the clearest statement of what this row added. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **253/253**. Full `ctest` not re-run: the change is confined to one test source. |
| VULKAN-252 | Implement the array uniform setters on `VulkanEffectRenderer` | ⬜ | `SetUniformFloatArray`, `SetUniformVec2Array`, `SetUniformVec3Array`, `SetUniformMat4Array` are implemented on EasyGL and absent on Vulkan, so a custom effect cannot receive a bone palette or a kernel. The 128-byte push-constant block cannot hold them; a uniform buffer is required. Test: a custom effect that reads a 4-element `mat4` array and renders a result that differs per element. **Interim state since `VULKAN-265` (2026-09-05):** the four setters no longer fall silent — they throw `System::NotSupportedException` naming the push-constant limit. Implementing them here therefore means **replacing a refusal, not filling a void**: the four `Refuse…` bodies and the four refusal legs of `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` must be removed in the same commit, or this row's own test cannot pass. |
| VULKAN-253 | Implement `IEffectRenderer::BindTexture` on `VulkanEffectRenderer` | ⬜ | Today a custom effect can only sample whatever `SpriteBatch` bound at binding 0. Test: a custom effect samples an explicitly bound second texture and the output depends on it. |
| VULKAN-254 | Implement `BindTextureCube` / `BindTexture3D` for custom effects on Vulkan | ⬜ | EasyGL proves both (`EasyGL_ShaderEffect_TextureCube`, `_Texture3D`); IGL proves the volume case on its Vulkan backend, so this is reachable rather than a boundary. Test: the SPIR-V twins of those two EasyGL tests, sampling a cube face and two Z slices. Depends on `VULKAN-253`. |
| VULKAN-255 | Support a `ShaderEffect` driving a 3D draw with a custom vertex layout on Vulkan | ⬜ | `EasyGL_ShaderEffect_3D` and `_CustomVertexLayout` have no Vulkan equivalent; `VulkanEffectRenderer::GetOrCreatePipeline` bakes the SpriteBatch vertex input. Either extend it to take the caller's `VertexDeclaration`, or refuse the combination by name so a game does not get a silently mis-fed shader. Depends on `VULKAN-139`'s stride verdict. |
| VULKAN-256 | Record the GLSL-source shader-test divergence rather than porting 37 tests | ⬜ | Write the decision into `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` and §9.3 here: `ShaderEffect` takes renderer-specific source by contract; the 37 EasyGL `*_Shader`/`Bloom_*`/`ShaderEffect_*` tests carry GLSL payloads and are not a Vulkan parity requirement. What *is* required is `VULKAN-250`–`VULKAN-255` (an equally capable Vulkan custom-effect surface) plus a named dependency on `plans/plan_csl.md` for one source serving both. Closing this task requires that the SPIR-V equivalents chosen for `VULKAN-251`/`VULKAN-254` are the ones that exercise capability, not the ones that are easiest to translate. |
| VULKAN-257 | Close the `BasicEffect` evidence gaps on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_basiceffect_combinations_test.cpp`, `_default_lighting_`, `_lit_vertex_color_`, `_position_normal_`, `_vertex_color_clamp_`, `_world_scale_precision_`. `VertexColorClamp` and `WorldScalePrecision` are the two that have historically found real defects; keep them separate commits from the rest if the first run is not green. |
| VULKAN-258 | Close the `AlphaTestEffect` evidence gaps on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_alphatest_modes_test.cpp`, `alpha_test_integration_test.cpp`, `alpha_test_effect_test.cpp`. Vulkan covers the compare-function sweep, fog, null texture and vertex colour already. |
| VULKAN-259 | Prove `DualTextureEffect` independent UV sets on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_dualtextureeffect_independent_uv_test.cpp`. The second UV channel is a vertex-layout question on Vulkan (the dual-UV strides 60/76 in `MakeExt3DKey`), so this also exercises `VULKAN-130`'s territory. |
| VULKAN-260 | Prove the `EnvironmentMapEffect` Fresnel **gradient** on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_environmentmapeffect_fresnel_gradient_test.cpp`. `Vulkan_EnvironmentMapEffect_Fresnel` proves the term exists; the gradient test is the one shaped to catch a per-vertex/per-pixel interpolation defect — the exact defect the D3D9 oracle corpus found on EasyGL (`NEXT.md`, `D9-A6`). Because EasyGL is a *suspect* here, the expected values come from XNA/FNA semantics, not from EasyGL's output. |
| VULKAN-261 | Close the `SkinnedEffect` evidence gaps on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_skinned_effect_bones_test.cpp`, `easygl_skinnedeffect_vector4_bone_indices_test.cpp`, `skinned_effect_integration_test.cpp`. Vulkan already has more skinned coverage than EasyGL elsewhere; these three are the remaining shapes. |
| VULKAN-262 | Prove PBR material maps on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_pbr_material_maps_test.cpp`. `Vulkan_Pbr_TextureSlots` proves slot mapping and `Vulkan_PbrEffect_Golden` proves the BRDF; the per-map contribution test (normal/metallic-roughness/occlusion/emissive each changing the result) is the gap. |
| VULKAN-263 | Prove `Effect::Clone` and `CurrentTechnique` on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_effect_clone_test.cpp` and `easygl_effect_current_technique_test.cpp`. Mostly shared logic, but a cloned effect must not share renderer-side pipeline or descriptor state — which is a Vulkan-specific property. |

## 20. Phase 8 — Model, content and glTF renderer-facing behaviour (`VULKAN-300`–`VULKAN-329`)

Parser-side glTF/`.cnj` correctness stays with `plans/plan_gltf.md` and `plans/plan_cnj.md`. The
question here is only whether the Vulkan renderer executes the same already-parsed scene and
material semantics.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-300 | Prove basic `Model` drawing on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_model_draw_test.cpp`. The seven `ModelJsonReader`/hierarchy/skinned-playback sources already run on Vulkan; this one does not. |
| VULKAN-301 | Prove tangent handedness and mirrored tangents on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_gltf_tangent_handedness_test.cpp` and `easygl_gltf_mirrored_tangent_test.cpp`. The bitangent sign lives in `TANGENT.w` and is consumed by the renderer's own normal-mapping code, so a sign flip is a renderer defect, not a parser one. |
| VULKAN-302 | Prove glTF sampler wrap and per-map texture transforms on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_gltf_sampler_wrap_test.cpp` and `easygl_gltf_texture_transform_per_map_test.cpp`. Per-map transforms need per-map UV state in the shader; Vulkan's PBR descriptor set carries seven views (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:7681`) and this proves each honours its own transform. |
| VULKAN-303 | Prove glTF alpha blending, transmission ordering and base-colour factor × texture on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register the three EasyGL sources. Transmission ordering is a draw-order property the deferred Vulkan command model could plausibly change, which is what makes it worth a Vulkan run rather than a shared-code argument. |
| VULKAN-304 | Prove skinned PBR under non-uniform joint scale on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_gltf_skinned_pbr_nonuniform_joint_test.cpp`. Non-uniform scale is the case where an inverse-transpose is required and a shortcut shows; `Vulkan_SkinnedEffect_WorldNormal` proves the stock-effect half already. |

## 21. Phase 9 — Device, presentation and lifecycle (`VULKAN-330`–`VULKAN-369`)

Vulkan has no GL context loss, and this phase does **not** translate EasyGL's context-recovery
machinery. It compares the observable CNA contract.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-330 | Implement `SetPresentationMode` on Vulkan (F-03) | ✅ | **Implemented, and the mutation check caught a silent behaviour change hiding inside the fix — which is the part of this row most worth reading.** **Observed:** `void SetPresentationMode(int) override {}` (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:1477`), the only empty override body in either renderer, so a game selecting `Letterbox` silently got Vulkan's uniform height-derived scale. Same class as F-31: the call is accepted and discarded. **Done:** the mode is stored, `GetPresentedRectEXT` computes the presented rectangle, and it is applied wherever "full target" is resolved **for the back buffer** — the pass-level default *and* both replay sites. **Two replay sites, not one:** every batch and every 3D draw overrides the viewport from its own captured state (REMED-GFX-062), so changing only the pass default would have letterboxed nothing, and changing only the 3D site would have letterboxed 3D draws while sprites kept the whole image. **Render targets are deliberately untouched:** a presentation mode describes how the virtual resolution reaches the *window*, not how a game's own targets are laid out. **The algorithm is EasyGL's, reproduced rather than shared.** `EasyGLSurfaceState::GetDefaultViewportRect`'s own comment names it the established reference for these semantics, and the modes must mean the same thing on both renderers or implementing them here is pointless. Duplicated because this row's non-goal forbids touching the shared contract or another renderer; the duplication is now three-way (OpenGL2, SdlGpu, EasyGL, Vulkan) and is worth folding into a shared helper by whoever is allowed to. **The default was wrong and the mutation check is what exposed it.** `presentationMode_` was first initialised to `Letterbox`; with the no-op body restored the test still passed, because the *default* was doing the letterboxing. `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationMode` forwards to the renderer **only when a game calls it** (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:3499`-`3503`), so the renderer's default is load-bearing — and EasyGL's is `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`, which is exactly the height-derived scale this renderer already had. `Letterbox` as the default would have silently letterboxed **every game that never sets a mode**: an unrequested behaviour change for all existing content, hidden inside a fix for something else. Corrected to `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`. **Test:** `Vulkan_PresentationMode` (`vulkan_presentation_mode_test.cpp`), **5/5**. The virtual resolution is set to half the drawable's height, so `Letterbox` has bars to produce: measured 800×240 at (0,120) inside an 800×480 drawable. One probe near the top edge separates the modes — a bar under `Letterbox`, geometry under `Stretch`. **Leg C is what stops leg B passing for the wrong reason:** a renderer that drew nothing, or clipped everything away, would satisfy "the top edge is background" while implementing nothing at all. **Mutation-checked, after the default was fixed:** restoring the no-op fails leg A (the rectangle is the full 800×480) and leg B2 (the top edge is geometry), while both `Stretch` legs still pass. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **253/253**. **An inconsistency this row INTRODUCES, stated plainly rather than filed as a distant follow-on:** `TransformWindowToLogical`/`TransformLogicalToWindow` map window coordinates through the height-derived scale alone (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:11281`-`11305`), where EasyGL maps them through the presented rectangle — subtracting its origin and scaling by its size (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp:757`). Before this row that was consistent, because there were no bars to ignore. **After it, a game using `Letterbox` renders correctly and maps input wrongly**, offset by the bar height. `GetDefaultViewportRect` likewise still reports the full drawable. Both are `VULKAN-331`, whose dependency on this row is now satisfied and which must land before `Letterbox`/`Overscan` are usable rather than merely visible. Shipping the rendering half first is deliberate — it is independently testable and independently correct — but it is half, and saying so is the difference between a staged change and a regression nobody wrote down. **Full-suite evidence, gathered in four numeric chunks rather than one run:** **9091/9110**, **19** failures — 7 audio/content, 7 lifecycle, 0 in `4801-6400`, 5 net/platform/C-API — all in the known set, none new, and **no `Vulkan_*` test among them**. The chunking is not a shortcut: four consecutive whole-suite runs were OOM-killed by another session's compile spikes on this shared machine, twice at `-j2`, once at `-j3` and once even at `-j1`, each losing everything. Splitting by test number (`ctest -I`) made a kill cost one chunk instead of the lot, and the union of the chunks is the same set of tests as one run over the same tree. |
| VULKAN-331 | Implement `GetDefaultViewportRect` on Vulkan (F-04) | ✅ | **Closes the inconsistency `VULKAN-330` introduced, which is why it was done immediately rather than left in the backlog.** **Two consumers needed the real rectangle, not one.** The row named `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow`, and that half is a one-line override forwarding to `GetPresentedRectEXT` — the framework already splits logical from physical correctly (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:3421`-`3429`: `Viewport.Width/Height` stays the game's virtual size while the physical rectangle goes to the GPU). **The half the row did not name is the one that mattered more:** `TransformWindowToLogical`/`TransformLogicalToWindow` mapped through the height-derived scale alone. That was equivalent while no mode produced a smaller rectangle; once `VULKAN-330` made `Letterbox` real it meant **a game rendered correctly and picked wrongly**. Both now map through the presented rectangle exactly as `EasyGLSurfaceState`'s do — subtract the origin, scale by the size, in client units via `displayScale`. **Test:** `Vulkan_PresentationMode` grows 5/5 → **8/8**. D asserts `GetDefaultViewportRect` reports the presented rectangle. E takes a window point one pixel from the top — inside the bar — and asserts it maps **outside** the logical area: measured `y = -119`. F asserts `logical → window → logical` round-trips, which catches a fix applied to one direction only. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the height-only transform makes E read `y = 0.5` — the bar point mapping *inside* the game area, so a click on the letterbox would be delivered as a click on the game — and F reads `(50, 85)` instead of `(100, 50)` because the two directions no longer agree. **Why E is the leg worth having:** "the viewport rectangle is right" is checkable by reading; "a click on the bar is not a click on the game" is the thing a player would notice, and it is what the height-only transform got wrong. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **253/253**, and the full `ctest` recorded on this row covers `VULKAN-330` as well — one serial run over a tree containing both halves, stated here and in that row so neither looks as though it skipped it. **Full-suite evidence, gathered in four numeric chunks rather than one run:** **9091/9110**, **19** failures — 7 audio/content, 7 lifecycle, 0 in `4801-6400`, 5 net/platform/C-API — all in the known set, none new, and **no `Vulkan_*` test among them**. The chunking is not a shortcut: four consecutive whole-suite runs were OOM-killed by another session's compile spikes on this shared machine, twice at `-j2`, once at `-j3` and once even at `-j1`, each losing everything. Splitting by test number (`ctest -I`) made a kill cost one chunk instead of the lot, and the union of the chunks is the same set of tests as one run over the same tree. |
| VULKAN-332 | Implement `SetSwapInterval` on Vulkan (F-05) | ✅ | **Implemented, and the mechanism was already there.** `swapInterval_` was read once, at swapchain creation, so `GraphicsDeviceManager.SynchronizeWithVerticalRetrace` + `ApplyChanges()` -- which does reach `SetSwapInterval` through `GraphicsDevice::Reset` -- changed nothing. `CreateSwapchain` already picks its `VkPresentModeKHR` from `swapInterval_` and this renderer already rebuilds its swapchain on every resize; the override records the request and reruns that path when the interval actually differs. `IGraphicsRenderer`'s own comment naming Vulkan as a renderer that "cannot change VSync at runtime" described the old implementation, not the API, and is corrected in place. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_SwapInterval` (`vulkan_swap_interval_test.cpp`), **7/7**, stable over three consecutive runs. The oracle is the **live swapchain's own `VkPresentModeKHR`** -- written only by `CreateSwapchain`, so it cannot move unless the swapchain was genuinely rebuilt -- observed going FIFO -> IMMEDIATE -> FIFO through the public `ApplyChanges()` route, with a drawn quad read back after each rebuild. `easygl_present_interval_test.cpp` is also registered (`Vulkan_PresentInterval`, 5/5) for the renderer-agnostic round-trip half. **Two things learned the hard way, both now written into the test:** (1) the recreation *counter* is useless as an oracle -- `Reset` also re-applies the virtual resolution and presentation format, and the same no-change `ApplyChanges()` cost 0 recreations before a draw and 1 after one, so asserting it tests `Reset`'s bookkeeping rather than this; it is printed, not asserted. (2) The first version had an **escape hatch that excused the defect**: FIFO is the only present mode Vulkan guarantees, so it inferred "this surface has no unsynchronised mode" from the mode not changing -- and with `SetSwapInterval` reverted to its no-op the test still passed 7/7. The branch now turns on a *measured* fact, `SupportsUnsynchronisedPresentModeEXT()`, read from the surface's own present-mode list. **Mutation probes, both run against the fixed test:** the pre-task no-op gives 6/7 (vsync off never leaves FIFO); recording the interval without rebuilding gives 5/7 (the mode lags the request in both directions -- worth noting, because `Reset`'s other rebuilds would eventually apply a recorded interval, just at the wrong time). **Suite:** 222/223. |
| VULKAN-333 | Implement `GetSwapIntervalEXT` on Vulkan | ✅ | **Implemented, and the row's registration instruction is deliberately NOT followed -- with the reason, per §26.6.** `GetSwapIntervalEXT()` now returns `swapInterval_`, so the `-1` that means "this renderer does not record what it was asked for" -- the honest answer while `SetSwapInterval` was the inherited no-op -- is gone. **Why `easygl_graphicsdevicemanager_vsync_test.cpp` is not registered on Vulkan:** its driver half is `SDL_GL_GetSwapInterval`, which has no meaning on a window created for Vulkan. Registered verbatim, its check A (`interval == 0` with vsync off) would pass **vacuously** on the zero left in an untouched `int`, and check B would take the skip path -- which is precisely the failure mode that file's own header documents as the reason `REMED-GFX-243` existed at all ("deleting the very fix this file was written to guard left it reporting PASS and SKIP"). Registering it here would recreate that, one renderer over. **What is done instead:** the two questions it asks are asked in `Vulkan_SwapInterval` with the probe that means something on this renderer. Its `P1`/`P2` forwarding half becomes legs `E`, read through `IGraphicsRenderer&` rather than the concrete type -- the same cross-renderer seam -- and its GL driver half becomes `VULKAN-332`'s `GetAppliedPresentModeEXT()` assertion, which is a real driver-side observation rather than a CNA-internal echo. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_SwapInterval` is now **10/10**: the interval is not `-1`, `SynchronizeWithVerticalRetrace=false` reads back as `0` and `true` as non-zero, alongside `VULKAN-332`'s present-mode legs. **Suite:** 222/223. |
| VULKAN-334 | Decide and document the Vulkan answer to device loss | ⬜ | EasyGL implements `SetContextRecoveryEnabled`/`DebugSimulateContextLoss`/`DebugRestoreContext` for GL context loss. Vulkan's analogue is `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`, which is not the same event and is not simulable the same way. Audit what CNA promises a game across such an event, record whether Vulkan meets it, and either implement the equivalent or record the refusal with the reason in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md`. **Explicit non-goal:** a fake Vulkan context-loss state machine built by analogy with GL. |
| VULKAN-335 | Prove `PresentationParameters` round-trip on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_presentation_parameters_test.cpp`. Interacts with `VULKAN-023`: an echoed sample count or depth format the device did not apply is exactly what this test should catch. |
| VULKAN-336 | Prove backbuffer resize on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_backbuffer_resize_test.cpp`: change `PreferredBackBufferWidth/Height`, `ApplyChanges()`, assert the viewport, the readback dimensions and a drawn quad's position all follow. Vulkan recreates the swapchain here; every render pass, framebuffer and depth resource is rebuilt. |
| VULKAN-337 | Prove real window resize on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_real_window_resize_test.cpp` — a platform-driven resize rather than an API-driven one, reaching `OnSurfaceChanged` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:9951`) rather than `SetVirtualResolution`. |
| VULKAN-338 | Prove repeated swapchain recreation, zero-sized and minimized windows on Vulkan | ⬜ | Vulkan-specific, with no EasyGL twin. Drive at least 20 resize cycles including a 0×0 (minimized) step and a restore, and assert: no validation message, every pre-existing `RenderTarget2D`/`Texture2D`/`VertexBuffer` still valid and readable afterwards, and rendering still correct. `RecreateSwapchain` returns early on a zero extent (`:3163`) — the test must cover what happens to a frame submitted while in that state. |
| VULKAN-339 | Prove GPU-handle release and double dispose on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_handle_release_test.cpp` and `easygl_double_dispose_test.cpp`. `HasRenderer()` false after the first `Dispose()` and no second release attempt — on Vulkan this is the path that also runs `vkDeviceWaitIdle` and the retirement lists. |
| VULKAN-340 | Prove no resource leak over many create/dispose cycles on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_resource_leak_test.cpp` (80 resources over 20 iterations). Extend it on Vulkan with a device-memory or allocation-count assertion if one is cheaply available, since `GetTrackedResourceCount() == 0` proves the CNA side only. |
| VULKAN-341 | Prove move semantics do not double-free Vulkan handles | ⬜ | Register `easygl_move_semantics_test.cpp`. Vulkan's resources register themselves in `liveVertexBuffers_`/`liveIndexBuffers_`/`liveRenderTargets_` on the renderer, so a move must not leave two entries pointing at one object — a shape the EasyGL registry does not have. |
| VULKAN-342 | Prove device/resource destruction ordering on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_device_dispose_order_test.cpp`. `~VulkanRenderer` explicitly releases externally-owned resources and disconnects their owners (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1779`+); destroying the device before the resources is the case that machinery exists for and nothing tests. |
| VULKAN-343 | Prove resource and device-reset events on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_resource_events_test.cpp` and `easygl_device_reset_events_test.cpp`. `ApplyChanges()` on a live device is a full `Reset` on Vulkan. |
| VULKAN-344 | Prove `GraphicsDevice` argument validation on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_device_validation_test.cpp` — including `Present()` while a render target is bound, which on Vulkan interacts with the deferred pass machinery rather than with a bound FBO. |
| VULKAN-345 | Prove the `Clear` overload and `ClearOptions` matrix on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `easygl_clear_overloads_test.cpp`. Vulkan has `_ClearOptions`, `_ClearDepth`, `_ClearStencil` and `_OrderedClear` but not the overload/argument-validation matrix, including the depth-range guard being flag-gated. |
| VULKAN-346 | Stop an unsupported combination from ending the process (F-16) | ✅ | **Both cases fixed, and the first turned out to be a real renderer defect rather than only a harness fault.** **Case 1, `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource`:** the stride refusal (`Vulkan PbrEffect requires vertex stride 48 or 60`) was raised by `GetOrCreatePipelinePbr3D`, which is called from `RecordCommandBuffer` -- at `Present()`. So the draw was **accepted**, the leg recorded its failure and unbound its target, and the exception then arrived at a frame boundary the caller could no longer associate with anything, outside every recovery it had. That is §6.3's failure exactly: not refused at the point of the mistake. `RequirePbrStrideEXT` now asks the same question where the draw is **queued** (both `Draw*PrimitivesEx` routes), with the same two messages, so nothing is queued that the replay cannot build. **Measured:** the `[CRASH] leg B1: killed by signal 6 (Aborted) (core dumped)` is gone; the leg reports `[FAIL] leg B1: an unexpected exception escaped: Vulkan PbrEffect requires vertex stride 48 or 60` and the CTest exits 1 -- the verdict it printed. It stays red until `VULKAN-144` supplies the capability, which is the correct state for a capability gap. **Case 2, `Vulkan_DualTextureSlotSamplerContract`:** a harness fault, as the row predicted. Its support probe calls `Render()`, which binds a render target before the draw it is probing with; the refusal unwinds past the unbind, the shutdown `Present()` correctly refuses, and nothing catches it, so an orderly `[SKIP]` became a `std::terminate`. The probe's catch now unbinds and resets device state first -- the same recovery `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp` already documents for its own legs. **Measured:** prints `=== 5/5 PASS ===` and exits **0**. **The second clause needed no new test:** "ending a frame with a render target bound is either legal or refused at the point of the mistake" is already owned, end to end, by `present_lifecycle_contract_test.cpp` (`Vulkan_PresentLifecycle`, passing) -- it reads the contract out of FNA's own `GraphicsDevice.Present`, establishes that it is REJECT rather than auto-unbind, asserts each link of the chain separately, and reproduces the process-level abort deliberately. Duplicating it here would add nothing. **Suite:** 218/219 -- only `Vulkan_RenderTarget_EffectSource` remains, and it is `VULKAN-144`'s. |
| VULKAN-347 | `PresentationParameters` must not echo back a multisample count the device did not apply | ✅ | **Fixed, and the fix is one line; the work was establishing which seam actually carries the defect.** **Observed:** `VulkanRenderer` did not override `GetAppliedMultiSampleCountEXT`, so it took the identity default (`IGraphicsRenderer.hpp:1948`) while the renderer really clamps — `sampleCount_ = PickSampleCount(physicalDevice_, args.multiSampleCount)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1838`) returns the highest supported count **≤** the request. Measured on llvmpipe, which offers 1× and 4× only: requests 1, 2, 3 apply 0 and reported 1, 2, 3; requests 5, 7, 8, 9, 16 apply 4 and reported 5, 7, 8, 9, 16. **Classification:** implementation bug — incorrect reporting of applied state. **Done:** the override returns `GetMultiSampleCount()` and ignores its argument, matching GDI (`GdiRenderer.hpp:70`), which is the renderer that established the semantic: both call sites pass the current request and use the answer as a write-back, so the question is *what is in effect*. **The 0-versus-1 trap, checked first:** `SampleCountToInt` maps `VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_1_BIT` through its `default:` arm to **0**, so "no multisampling" keeps XNA's spelling and `PresentationParametersTests`' two `EXPECT_EQ(…, 0)` cases are untouched. **Where a game can actually read the wrong value — corrected twice, by measurement, and this is the part worth keeping:** the row as opened claimed direct construction (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:3309`) was the observable path, because that line's own comment says it exists to stop an unapplied request being retained. It is not: Game startup reaches `Reset()` afterwards, which writes the count back from `ApplyMultiSampleCount`'s **own return** (`:663`-`:665`) and corrects it first. `ApplyChanges` does the same. **Two successive drafts of leg C passed with the fix reverted** — one through `ApplyChanges`, one through direct construction — before the third found the seam that survives: `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationParameters` (`:2798`), the CNAEXT store-only path, whose comment says it does not reconfigure MSAA and relies on the renderer reporting what is in effect. A test that cannot fail is worse than no test, and only a reverted-fix run tells the two apart. **Test:** `Vulkan_AppliedMultiSampleCount` (`vulkan_applied_multisample_test.cpp`), **11/11**. Legs A/B sweep nine requests and compare the report against what `ApplyMultiSampleCount` actually reaches, labelling each as clamped or control — the powers of two must **not** change, so a fix returning a constant fails. Leg C1 proves the 8× request really was clamped on this device before C2 asserts what was stored, so C2 cannot pass vacuously. The renderer's original count is restored afterwards, since `ApplyMultiSampleCount` tears down every sample-count-keyed render pass and pipeline. **Mutation-checked:** with the override removed, eight of the nine A legs go red with the reported-versus-applied pair printed, and C2 reads `stores the applied count (8 == 4)`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **241/241**, full `ctest` **9078/9099**. The 21 full-suite failures are the known audio/content/lifecycle/C-API set, unchanged from the previous row, and no `Vulkan_*` test is among them. The full suite was run rather than skipped because this changes a value the shared `GraphicsDevice` writes into `PresentationParameters`, which is precisely the F-21 shape. |
| VULKAN-348 | Decide what the applied back-buffer and depth-stencil formats are, in XNA terms | ✅ | **Both now report what is in effect, following the two precedents already in this renderer rather than inventing a third rule.** **Observed:** neither `GetAppliedBackBufferFormatEXT` nor `GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT` was overridden, so `NormalizeAppliedPresentationFormats` (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:133`) wrote the *requested* formats into `PresentationParameters` as though they had been honoured — while the swapchain takes what the surface offers (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2767`-`2770`) and `FindDepthFormat()` picks a depth format the request does not name. **The decisions this row owed, and how each was settled.** *(a) The mapping.* `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED` → `None`, `D16_UNORM` → `Depth16`, `X8_D24_UNORM_PACK32` and `D32_SFLOAT` → `Depth24`, `D16/D24/D32_*_S8_UINT` → `Depth24Stencil8`. Two rows are lossy on purpose and the code says which way they round: a 32-bit format reports as the nearest XNA name that agrees about the **stencil plane**, because that is the property a caller can act on — `StencilEnable` either works or it does not — and precision has no XNA enumerator to be precise with. *(b) Request or substitute.* Substitute. `VULKAN-347` settled the identical question for the sample count, and `SupportsCapability(StencilBuffer)` (`:1924`-`:1932`) already answers from `depthFormat_` rather than from anything requested, for a reason it states: on a device that took the no-stencil fallback, `StencilEnable` cannot work however correctly the pipeline maps it. *(c) `DepthFormat::None`.* Report the real format. `CreateDepthResources()` calls `FindDepthFormat()` **unconditionally** (`:4416`), so the back buffer always has a depth buffer; reporting `None` would describe a buffer that exists as absent, and the capability query already reports it as present. **Two findings that only appeared during implementation.** `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` **declares no applied-format virtual at all** — an override written for `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` failed to compile, and was removed rather than worked around, so a `RenderTargetCube`'s applied depth format is simply not reportable through this seam. And the back buffer and render targets **disagree about `None`**: a render target honours it (`depthVkFormat_` stays `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED`, so the mapping reports `None` with no special case) while the back buffer allocates depth regardless. Whether that unconditional allocation is itself right is **not** answered here — this row reports what exists; if it is a defect it earns its own row. **Test:** `Vulkan_AppliedFormats` (`vulkan_applied_formats_test.cpp`), **3/3**, driven through `GraphicsDevice::SetPresentationParameters` for the same reason `VULKAN-347` had to use it: `Reset()` and `ApplyChanges` write the count back from `ApplyMultiSampleCount`'s own return, so a leg driven through either passes with the fix reverted. `Depth16` is the discriminating request because `FindDepthFormat()` prefers stencil-capable formats and never returns `D16_UNORM` for the back buffer. **Leg C is the one worth having:** it asserts the stored depth format and `SupportsCapability(StencilBuffer)` **agree about the stencil plane**, not merely that the stored value differs from the request. **Mutation-checked:** with both overrides removed all three legs fail, and leg C fails as `stored says no, capability says yes` — two answers to one question at one seam, which is F-08's shape and what this row removes. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **250/250**, full `ctest` **9086/9107**. 21 failures, none in `Vulkan_*`. Two differ from the previous row's list — `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCount…` and `TwoProcessLoopbackTest.HostMigrationPromotes…` — and **both pass in isolation** (1.32 s and 0.79 s). Worth stating precisely, because it corrects the shorthand used in earlier rows: this run was **`-j1`**, so these are not ctest-parallelism flakes. They are sensitive to machine load, and this machine is shared with another session's builds and test suites. Passing in isolation is what separates them from a regression; "contention" was the right instinct and the wrong mechanism. |
| VULKAN-349 | Decide whether the back buffer should allocate depth when `DepthFormat::None` was requested | ✅ | **Measured, left alone — and the cost that matters turned out not to be the one the row was opened about.** **(c) The machinery, which decides the answer on its own:** `depthFormat_` is compared against `VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED` in exactly **one** place in the whole renderer — `SupportsCapability(DepthStencilBuffer)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1933`). Everywhere else a depth attachment is assumed to exist. Gating the allocation would therefore need a no-depth variant of the swapchain render pass *and* of its load/store variants, of the framebuffers, and of every pipeline keyed on depth state. That is a change to the core of the renderer. **(b) The memory it would save:** the depth image is `swapchainExtent` × `sampleCount` at 4 bytes per sample for `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT` — 1.5 MB at 800×480, 8.3 MB at 1080p, 33 MB at 1080p with 4× MSAA. Real, bounded, and out of proportion to a core render-pass change for a request XNA games rarely make (the XNA default is `Depth24`). **(a) The real cost is portability, not memory, and it is worth stating because the row did not anticipate it.** A game that asks for `DepthFormat::None` on Vulkan gets a working depth buffer: `DepthStencilState.DepthBufferEnable` has an effect where on a renderer that honours `None` it would not. So a game *developed against Vulkan* could depend on depth under `None` and break elsewhere. That is a `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE`, not a resource leak, and it is the thing to weigh if this is ever reopened. **What `VULKAN-348` already did about the visible half:** `PresentationParameters.DepthStencilFormat` now reports the format that really exists rather than the `None` that was asked for, so the divergence is at least **legible** to a game that looks — which is the smallest honest response short of the core change. **Verdict: left alone, with the divergence recorded rather than the allocation removed** (§26 rule 3). The row's explicit non-goal was removing it because it is unrequested; the measurement is what turns that into a decision. **No test added:** `Vulkan_AppliedFormats` already asserts the reporting half, and a test asserting that an unrequested depth buffer exists would pin a divergence in place as though it were a contract. |

---

## 22. Phase 10 — Queries and other ordinary facilities (`VULKAN-370`–`VULKAN-389`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-370 | Answer `PixelCountIsPreciseEXT` deliberately on Vulkan | ✅ | **F-14 said "probably correct"; it was not.** A Vulkan occlusion query is only required to produce an exact count when the device's `occlusionQueryPrecise` feature is **enabled** and the query is begun with `VK_QUERY_CONTROL_PRECISE_BIT`. This renderer did **neither** -- `CreateLogicalDevice` did not request the feature, `vkCmdBeginQuery(... , 0)` passed no flags -- and inherited the shared `true` default. So it promised XNA's real Direct3D 9 tally while asking the device only "did anything pass", and the lensflare idiom (`PixelCount()` over an area) is exactly what that breaks. **Fix:** enable the feature where the device offers it, pass the PRECISE bit only then (passing it without the feature is a usage error), and override `PixelCountIsPreciseEXT()` to report that same flag -- so on a device that cannot count, the honest `false` reaches `OcclusionQuery::isPixelCountPreciseEXT()`. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_Precision` (`vulkan_occlusionquery_precision_test.cpp`), **4/4**. An occluder covers the left half at a nearer depth and the queried quad covers the whole frame at a farther one, so the surviving fragment count is known in advance: **2048** exactly, asserted as that number rather than as `> 0` (a boolean-shaped query answers 1). **The measurement that shaped the test:** llvmpipe counts exactly *even without* the precise bit, so "asked for precise" and "did not ask and got lucky" are indistinguishable through the public API -- which is precisely how the unearned promise survived unnoticed. Leg D closes that from the outside: passing the PRECISE bit without the feature is `VUID-vkCmdBeginQuery-queryType-00800`, so a silent validation log is what shows the bit and the feature agree. **Probes, both run:** with the feature not enabled the report becomes `precise=false` and the test takes its honest branch (3/3 legs, no failure -- it does not punish a device for being honest); with the feature claimed but not enabled, leg D fails with `vkCmdBeginQuery(): flags includes VK_QUERY_CONTROL_PRECISE_BIT, but occlusionQueryPrecise feature was not enabled.` **Suite:** 220/221. |
| VULKAN-371 | Prove occlusion-query reset and reuse on Vulkan | ⬜ | Register `occlusion_query_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_OcclusionQuery_Cycle`): the same query object reused across frames, `IsComplete()` transitions, and a `Begin` without an `End`. Vulkan needs a `vkCmdResetQueryPool` per use, which is exactly the thing a reuse test catches. |
| VULKAN-372 | Audit the remaining ordinary facilities EasyGL exposes and Vulkan is expected to support | ⬜ | Sweep for anything reachable from the public XNA/CNA surface not covered by Phases 2–9 — `GraphicsAdapter`/`DisplayMode` enumeration, `GraphicsDevice.Textures`/`VertexTextures` collections, `GetBackBufferData` variants, `MouseCursor::FromTexture2D`'s colour-transfer route. Each gap found becomes a row before this closes. |

---

## 23. Phase 11 — Validation, robustness and stress (`VULKAN-390`–`VULKAN-429`)

Correctness and bounded resource behaviour, not optimization. No row here may be created from a
guess about performance; §13 of the instruction that produced this plan names the four pathologies
that qualify, and each row below cites the one it addresses.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-390 | Make descriptor-pool exhaustion fail loudly instead of drawing white (F-06) | ✅ | **Fixed, and F-06 understated it.** `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet` now chains another pool of `MaxDescriptorSets` when every existing one refuses, and raises a **named** exception if the device refuses that too (`...Refused rather than drawing a substituted texture.`). Each cache entry carries the pool it came from, because a set freed against the wrong pool is undefined behaviour and there is no longer only one; eviction routes through the existing `poolDescriptorSets` retirement path, and teardown destroys the chained pools. **What the mutation probe showed, which changes the finding's severity:** restoring the old `return defaultWhiteDescSet_` does not draw white on the SpriteBatch path -- it **binds `VK_NULL_HANDLE` and segfaults**. `EnsureDefaultWhiteTexture()` is called from the three 3D draw routes only (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:11171`, `:11454`, `:11762`), so in a 2D-only frame `defaultWhiteDescSet_` has never been created. Measured: `vkCmdBindDescriptorSets(): pDescriptorSets[0] (VK_NULL_HANDLE) ...`, `vkCmdDrawIndexed(): ... uses set #0 but that set is not bound`, then `Segmentation fault (core dumped)`, exit 139. F-06 called this a silent white sprite; on the route it is most likely to be reached from it was a crash. **Reaching the arm at all was the hard part, and the measurement is the reason the test is shaped as it is:** the exhaustion is **not reachable by volume** on either driver here. `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` keeps succeeding past the pool's `maxSets` -- which the spec permits, since running out is a runtime error an implementation *may* report and not a usage violation the layer flags -- and **4000** simultaneously live pairs still did not trigger it. Shrinking the pool to 8 sets did not either. A test built on volume alone would have passed while executing none of the new code. The failure is therefore **injected**, through the test-only `SetTexSamplerDescriptorAllocationFailuresForTestEXT` knob -- the other option this row allows, and the seed of the hook `VULKAN-391` needs. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DescriptorPoolOverflow` (`vulkan_descriptor_pool_overflow_test.cpp`), **5/5** -- one injected failure chains exactly one pool and the sprite drawn across it still shows its own texture; 640 distinct live textures in one frame each draw their own (the production-shape volume leg, with the pool count printed as evidence that this driver did not enforce `maxSets`); an injection the chained pool cannot satisfy either is refused by name; the layer stays silent. **Mutation:** the old fallback fails leg A (`1 -> 1 pool(s)`) and then crashes, as above. **Non-goal honoured:** the descriptor strategy is unchanged -- this is a chained pool, not a redesign. **Suite:** 217/219. |
| VULKAN-391 | Make the eleven `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` failure paths consistent | ✅ | **One contract, applied to all eleven, and the contract is the observable one: a caller never receives `VK_NULL_HANDLE`, and a refusal is BY NAME, at the draw.** That is the only contract worth having here -- the sole thing a caller can do with a descriptor set is bind it, and `VULKAN-390`'s mutation probe already measured what binding a null costs: `pDescriptorSets[0] (VK_NULL_HANDLE)` and a segfault. **What changed:** the seven `return VK_NULL_HANDLE` arms (DualTexture, EnvMap, LitTextured, FogTex3D, Skinned, Pbr, PbrSkinned) now throw, each naming its own family; `EnsureDefaultWhiteTexture`'s site, which did not even **look** at the result, throws too; and all eleven now go through `AllocateOneDescriptorSet`, leaving exactly one raw `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` call in the file -- inside that helper. **Routing them through it is what made the arms testable at all**: the helper carries `VULKAN-390`'s fault-injection hook, and it used to sit beside the one pool that called it, which is precisely why the other ten had no test. It moved to the top of the file. The hook is renamed `SetDescriptorAllocationFailuresForTestEXT` to match what it now covers. **The one place the contract is NOT uniform underneath, recorded rather than papered over:** the shared combined-image-sampler pool *recovers* by chaining another pool (`VULKAN-390`) before it refuses; the seven family pools refuse straight away. Observably identical -- never a null, always a named refusal -- and different in effort, because only the shared pool has a chaining path. **Evidence:** new CTest `Vulkan_DescriptorContractUniformity` (`vulkan_descriptor_contract_uniformity_test.cpp`), **13/13**. Each family is drawn once to build its pool and cache, then drawn again with a DIFFERENT texture and one injected allocation failure, then a third time with the injection disarmed -- so the test asserts the refusal names the right family AND that the refusal left the renderer usable. **The hook gained a `skipFirst` parameter, and finding out why is the interesting part:** leg A's draw takes a set from the *shared* pool before it takes one from its own, so an unskipped failure was absorbed by the chaining path and the draw simply **succeeded** -- the test caught its own first version asserting a refusal that never came. **Mutation:** restoring `return VK_NULL_HANDLE` in the PbrEffect arm alone does not fail the test -- it **kills the process, exit 139**. That is the finding F-06 recorded as "returns null", measured. **Regression:** `Vulkan_DescriptorPoolOverflow` (VULKAN-390's own) stays 5/5. |
| VULKAN-392 | Remove the per-buffer-dispose full-device stall (F-13) | ✅ | **Removed, and the removal is counted rather than asserted: 120 buffer create/destroy cycles now cost 0 full-device stalls, where before they cost exactly 120.** **Observed:** `VulkanVertexBufferRenderer::ReleaseVulkanResources` and the index-buffer twin each opened with `vkDeviceWaitIdle`. `DrawUserPrimitives`/`DrawUserIndexedPrimitives` allocate a throwaway `VertexBuffer` per call (`GraphicsDevice.cpp:1753`+), so on Vulkan every such draw ended in a full-device stall — the pathology §13 names, on a route a game may use every frame. **Done:** both paths retire their `VkBuffer` and `VkDeviceMemory` into the same fence-gated queue textures and render targets already use (`RetiredResources`, `RetireResources`, `ProcessRetiredResources`). `RetiredResources` gains a `buffers` vector, freed **before** the memories in the same drain loop, because a `VkBuffer` must be destroyed before the memory it is bound to. **Why retiring is safer than the stall it replaces, not merely cheaper** — this is the part that made the change small: the deferred replay in `RecordCommandBuffer` binds only renderer-owned per-frame buffers (`spriteVB_`, `frame3DVB_`, `frame3DInstVB_`) and **never** a user buffer handle, because a queued draw carries copied vertex and index bytes (`Pending3DDraw::vbData`/`ibData`); the only command that ever names this buffer is the staging copy inside `SetData`, which `EndOneTimeCommands` has already waited on. So there was no in-flight reference for the stall to protect, and the fence gate is a strictly stronger guarantee than the immediate destroy that followed it. **The counter, and why it is trustworthy:** "we removed the stall" is a claim a test must be able to check, so every `vkDeviceWaitIdle` in this renderer now goes through a single `DeviceWaitIdleEXT()` funnel that increments `GetDeviceWaitIdleCountEXT()`. The count is complete by construction — a stall reintroduced **anywhere**, not only in the two functions this row touched, fails the test. `GetRetiredBufferCountEXT()` was added alongside it so the test can show the cycles it is pricing really happened instead of inferring them from a pixel. **Test:** `Vulkan_BufferDisposeStall` (`vulkan_buffer_dispose_stall_test.cpp`), **4/4**, on the scene `VULKAN-134` unblocked. Leg A is the control — 40 `DrawUserPrimitives` per frame render a red quad, without which "zero stalls" is satisfied by drawing nothing. Leg B prices the draw path, counting from **after** a warm-up frame so one-time pipeline creation is not charged to it. Leg C shows 120 buffers really reached the retirement queue. Leg D asserts the validation layer captured nothing — retiring a handle still in use would be a use-after-free it sees. **Mutation-checked:** restoring the stall in the vertex-buffer path alone turns leg B red with `120 full-device stalls`, one per destroyed buffer, which is the defect quantified. **Discovered while counting, handed to a new row rather than folded in:** three of the four remaining stalls are teardown or reconfiguration and unarguable; the fourth, in `FlushDeferredRenderTarget`, is mid-frame and on the render-target-sampling path. That is `VULKAN-399`, and it is deliberately opened as a *measure first* row rather than an optimization. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **246/246**, full `ctest` **9078/9100**. |
| VULKAN-393 | Gate the Vulkan suite on zero CNA-attributable validation messages | ✅ | **Done, and it gates every test rather than a representative one — which is the row's own acceptance met by a wider instrument than it proposed.** **What was built:** a block at the **end** of `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` that reads the directory's `TESTS` property and appends `\\[Vulkan Validation\\]` to every registration's `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`. That string is the exact prefix the debug messenger prints for every message the Khronos layer reports at severity WARNING or above (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2596`), so the gate is *any* message, not a message class. **All 254** Vulkan CTests carry it; the position in the file is load-bearing and is commented as such, because a registration added below the block would be silently ungated. **Why an output gate and not the in-process assertion the row proposed.** `VULKAN-405`'s leak was reported *during* `vkDestroyDevice` — after the last line of the test body, out of the very object whose `validationMessages_` an in-process check would have read. Teardown is where object tracking names leaks, so a gate that cannot see teardown cannot see that whole class. The process's output can. **Existing patterns are preserved, not replaced.** `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` is a *list*, so the nine registrations that already carried one (`VUID-vkCmdDraw-None-09600`, `VUID-|Validation Error|Validation Warning|SYNC-HAZARD`, …) keep it and gain this one. Checked in the generated `CTestTestfile.cmake`: `Vulkan_MrtMipFinalization` reads `"VUID-|…|SYNC-HAZARD;\\[Vulkan Validation\\]"`. The block also skips a test that already has the pattern, so `VULKAN-405`'s explicit one on `Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats` is not duplicated. **And it matters that the neighbours' pattern was not enough:** an Object Tracking report contains none of `VUID-`, `Validation Error`, `Validation Warning` or `SYNC-HAZARD`, so the pre-existing gates would have let `VULKAN-405`'s message straight through. **The allowlist, corrected.** This row asked for *“a documented allowlist of driver/layer messages with a reason each”*. Measured: after `VULKAN-404`, `-405` and `-406` there are **zero** messages across the suite, and not one of `VULKAN-009`'s original three was a driver or layer artefact — all three were ours. So there is no message to allow, and a per-message allowlist would be a mechanism with nothing in it. What exists instead is a per-**test** exemption list, `CNA_VULKAN_VALIDATION_GATE_EXEMPT`, **empty**, in one auditable place, whose comment requires the exact message and the reason beside any name added to it. `VULKAN-477` requires that no entry is ever CNA-attributable. **It cannot pass vacuously**, which is the failure mode a zero-message gate has: four tests assert `VulkanRenderer::IsValidationActiveEXT()` before judging anything — `Vulkan_RenderTarget_ProducerConsumer`, `Vulkan_Swapchain_Sync`, and since `VULKAN-404`/`-406` `Vulkan_BackbufferReadbackDimension` and `Vulkan_CubeVolume_SetDataContract` — so a build where the layer never loaded goes red rather than reporting a clean suite. **Sync validation** stays opt-in (`sRequestSyncValidation`) and is still exercised by `Vulkan_RenderTarget_ProducerConsumer_SyncVal` and `Vulkan_Swapchain_Sync`, which enable it in process; the gate judges their output like everyone else's. **Mutation-checked, and deliberately not by re-breaking a fixed defect** — that would only re-test `VULKAN-404`/`-406`'s own tests, which have in-process assertions of their own and would fail anyway. Instead the gate's *pattern* was swapped for a string the suite really prints (`CNA: graphics renderer: VULKAN`) and CMake re-run with no recompilation: `Vulkan_SpriteBatch_Scale` and `Vulkan_NpotTexture` — two tests that had **no** `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` before this row — both went red. That is the proof the gate is live on the tests it was added to rather than only on the ones that already had a pattern. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254** with the gate active on all 254. Full `ctest` **not** re-run: the change is CMake-only and touches nothing but Vulkan test properties, so nothing outside this renderer's own test set can move. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-009` (the inventory), and `VULKAN-404`/`-405`/`-406`, which emptied it.
| VULKAN-394 | Bound the pipeline caches under ordinary state churn | ✅ | **Measured on every axis, and the row's own two outcomes both happened — most of the key is bounded and closes the row by recording it, one axis is not and gets `VULKAN-162`.** New CTest `Vulkan_PipelineCacheBound` (`vulkan_pipeline_cache_bound_test.cpp`), **5/5**. **Bounded:** 32 identical draws build **no** further pipelines (leg A, the control); an ordinary four-blend-state mix stops growing the moment every state has been seen and adds **nothing** over 16 further frames (leg B — this is the case the row cares about, and it is the one that must not grow with frame count); and the declaration axis is bounded by how many declarations exist rather than by frames — five distinct ones build five pipelines and drawing all five again builds none (leg D). **Not bounded:** 32 distinct `MultiSampleMask` values build **30** pipelines. It is a 32-bit integer the game sets, so the axis has no ceiling, and — the part that makes it a defect rather than a cost — most of those bits **cannot change the result**, because a sample mask only selects among samples that exist and the pipeline is single-sampled unless the draw is MSAA. Recorded as F-30, owned by `VULKAN-162`, which can bound it by narrowing the key term rather than by evicting anything. **A mistake worth keeping:** leg D's first version swept the *stride* as well as the offset, which measures program selection rather than the declaration axis — the fidelity guard refused it by name, which is the guard doing its job and is why the leg now holds one program fixed. |
| VULKAN-395 | Bound the sampler cache | ✅ | **Answered the way the row allowed for: the key space was established, the bounded part was asserted, and the unbounded part opened a row.** New CTest `Vulkan_SamplerCacheBound` (`vulkan_sampler_cache_bound_test.cpp`), **4/4**. **The key space is not what the row assumed.** `SamplerStateKey` has seven fields and five are enumerations — filter, three address modes, anisotropy — which would give a few hundred keys and a real bound. The other two are **caller-supplied numbers**: `MaxMipLevel` is an `int` and `MipMapLevelOfDetailBias` a `float`. **Measured:** 32 draws with one sampler state create **no** further samplers — leg A, the control, without which a growth measurement proves nothing — while 64 distinct bias values add **63**, one per distinct value. The 64th is not a miss: bias 0.0 is the state already cached. **Why that matters rather than being a curiosity:** animating a LOD bias is what a LOD bias is for, and this renderer allocates a `VkSampler` per frame for one. `maxSamplerAllocationCount` is finite and the test reads it from the device — 32768 here — so it is a route to exhausting a hardware ceiling, not just to using memory. **A second defect fell out of reading the failure path**, and it is not this row's to fix: when `vkCreateSampler` does fail, the null propagates into `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet`, which answers it with `defaultWhiteDescSet_` — the silent white substitution `VULKAN-390` and `VULKAN-391` removed from all eleven descriptor-allocation sites, still alive one layer down. **Both are recorded as F-29 with owners: `VULKAN-160` (bound it, which needs descriptor invalidation and the retirement queues, so it is not a one-liner) and `VULKAN-161` (make exhaustion loud).** The diagnostics this row added — `GetSamplerCacheSizeEXT` and `GetMaxSamplerAllocationCountEXT` — are what both rows will test against. |
| VULKAN-396 | Measure the cost of `vkQueueWaitIdle` per one-time command and decide | ✅ | **Measured, and the decision is: the wait itself stays, but the number it produced opened `VULKAN-401`.** **How it was measured, rather than reasoned about:** `EndOneTimeCommands` now times its own `vkQueueWaitIdle` and counts its submissions (`GetOneTimeCommandWaitNanosEXT`, `GetOneTimeCommandCountEXT`), so the figure comes from the production path under a real workload instead of from a microbenchmark of a call. **The workload:** 48 `Texture2D` creations of 256×256, which is what content loading actually looks like — a burst of uploads through the staging path. **The numbers, on llvmpipe under Xvfb:** 144 one-time commands for 48 uploads, **128.2 µs per queue wait**, 45.6 ms of work, and the waits are **40.5 %** of that wall clock. **The decision on the wait itself:** it stays. It exists because `EndOneTimeCommands` frees the command buffer on the next line and freeing one still executing is illegal, so it is a correctness requirement of the shape the function has — not a defensive habit. Nothing measured here is a robustness problem: no incorrectness, no race, no leak, and this row's own rule reserves a remediation task for that. **What the measurement did find, and why it earns a row anyway:** three of those waits are paid per texture where one would do. The upload is a barrier, a copy and a barrier, and each opens its own command buffer because `TransitionLevelLayout` allocates one per call rather than recording into a caller's. That is `VULKAN-401`, opened *with* the measurement exactly as this row requires, and classified as structure rather than robustness so the next reader is not misled about its severity. **Test:** `Vulkan_OneTimeCommandCost` (`vulkan_one_time_command_cost_test.cpp`), **2/2**, kept as a permanent record rather than a one-off script. It splits its output the way `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot splits its entries: the **structural** facts are asserted because they hold on any device — at least one command per upload, and the waits are real time rather than a no-op — while the **timing** is printed and never failed on, because a suite that fails on an absolute duration reports the GPU rather than the renderer. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **249/249**. |
| VULKAN-397 | Run the Vulkan example suite under ASan/UBSan for CNA-owned CPU code | ⬜ | Build `build-asan`/`build-ubsan` per `CLAUDE.md` (never a new directory, never in the scratchpad) and run the Vulkan suite. Judge by UBSan lines, ASan errors and CNA-frame leak stacks — never by the `SUMMARY:` line, which always reports driver allocations at exit. `VULKAN-130`/`VULKAN-131` are expected to show here. |
| VULKAN-398 | Repeated-use stress across the whole surface | ⬜ | One test that runs many frames of: `Begin`/`End` cycles, render-target bind/unbind cycles, texture `SetData` updates, buffer updates, and a resize every N frames — asserting correctness at the end, not only at the start, and asserting no unbounded growth in the counters `VULKAN-394`/`VULKAN-395` establish. This is the "still correct after repeated use" clause of §6.8 made testable. |
| VULKAN-399 | Decide whether `FlushDeferredRenderTarget`'s full-device stall can be replaced by a fence wait | ✅ | **Measured, left alone — and the row's own premise was wrong, which is the finding.** **What this row assumed when opened:** the stall is reached "whenever a game renders to a target and samples it in the same frame — shadow maps, post-process chains, reflection passes", i.e. a routine path. **What the code says:** `FlushDeferredRenderTarget` has exactly **two** callers, `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer::GetData` and its cube twin (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:1175`, `:14687`). It is a **CPU readback** path, not a rendering one. Rendering into a target and sampling it never reaches it — the deferred model records both and replays them in one ordered stream at `Present()`. **And even a readback often does not reach it:** the function returns early when the target has no pending cycle (`if (!hasPendingCycle) return;`), so a `GetData` issued after anything has drained the queues costs nothing. **Measured, after two drafts that measured the wrong thing:** the first version read **0 stalls** for a readback because an earlier `GetBackBufferData` had already drained the queues; the second credited the readback's stall to the sampling draws because its counting window enclosed both. With the windows corrected: **24 sprites sampling a target cost 0 stalls; one `RenderTarget2D.GetData` issued while the target still has queued work costs exactly 1, at 0.68 ms** on llvmpipe under Xvfb. **Decision: the stall stays, and this is not reluctance.** A CPU readback is inherently synchronizing — the caller has asked to see finished pixels — so a device wait on that path is expected rather than pathological, and §13's concern is a stall in a *routine* path, which this is not. Replacing it with a fence would save nothing a caller did not already ask for. **Non-goal honoured:** the row forbade removing the stall because it is a stall, and the measurement is what makes "left alone" a verdict rather than a shrug (§26 rule 3). **Test:** `Vulkan_RtFlushStallCost` (`vulkan_rt_flush_stall_cost_test.cpp`), **4/4**, kept as a permanent record. Structural facts are asserted — the target really was sampled, the readback really read it, sampling costs **zero** stalls and a readback costs a bounded one — while the timings are printed and never failed on, for the reason `VULKAN-396` recorded. **Consequence for the matrix:** §10's *Full-device stalls in ordinary paths* row can now read `PARITY`: the dispose stall is gone (`VULKAN-392`), the one-time-command wait is a correctness requirement (`VULKAN-396`), and this one is not in an ordinary path at all. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254**. Full `ctest` not re-run: the production change is instrumentation-free — only a test was added. |
| VULKAN-400 | Determine whether a full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch` draw before a 3D draw breaks the frame on Vulkan | ✅ | **It does not — and the control run is what makes that worth saying, because it also does not break on EasyGL, where the finding is documented.** **Where the question came from:** `easygl_spritebatch_blendstate_leak_test.cpp` deliberately draws its sprite in a small corner and says why (`:9`-`:12`, `:88`) — to avoid a "separate, unrelated, still-open" finding that a full-backbuffer `SpriteBatch` draw before any 3D draw in the same frame breaks that frame's 3D rendering entirely. `docs/migration-guide.md:119`-`120` lists it among the known issues, scoped to EasyGL, marked "investigated, root cause not yet isolated" (Task 933). No Vulkan verdict existed anywhere. **Test:** `fullscreen_spritebatch_then_3d_test.cpp`, written renderer-neutral in `modules/graphics/examples/` so one source answers the question for any family, and registered for **both** — `Vulkan_FullscreenSpriteThen3D` and `EasyGL_FullscreenSpriteThen3D`.
