Two legs, identical but for the sprite's size: a corner sprite (A) and a full-backbuffer sprite (B), each followed by a full-viewport red 3D quad with nothing touched in between, then a centre readback. Leg A is what isolates full coverage as the trigger — a B-only failure would name it, a failure in both would mean something broader. **Result: A and B are `(255,0,0)` on Vulkan and `(255,0,0)` on EasyGL.** So the case is correct here, and the answer this row owes is "no Vulkan defect". **Why the EasyGL run was not optional:** without it, "absent on Vulkan" could not be told apart from "this scene cannot detect the thing anywhere", and a test that fails nowhere proves nothing. Registering the control cost one target build in the existing `cmake-build-easygl`. **What this does NOT establish, stated rather than glossed:** that the original finding is fixed. Its conditions survive only as prose in a comment and a docs bullet, and this scene is one reading of them; "not reproducible by this scene on either renderer" is weaker than "fixed", and the difference matters because the work-around in the blend test and the known-issues bullet are both still in place. **Handed on rather than acted on unilaterally:** both belong to whoever owns EasyGL — `docs/migration-guide.md:119` and the corner-sprite work-around comment should be revisited with this measurement attached, and what would settle it is the original Task 933 reproduction, not this scene. Editing another renderer's known-issues list on the strength of one scene is exactly the shortcut this plan's own §9.2 treatment of stale documentation forbids. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **248/248**; `EasyGL_FullscreenSpriteThen3D` passes in its own build directory. |
| VULKAN-401 | A texture upload issues three one-time commands where one suffices | ✅ | **Fixed: 48 uploads now cost 48 one-time commands, down from 144 — exactly one each.** **Observed (by `VULKAN-396`'s measurement, which is why this row exists at all):** creating a `Texture2D` performed `TransitionImageLayout(UNDEFINED → TRANSFER_DST)`, `CopyBufferToImage`, `TransitionImageLayout(TRANSFER_DST → SHADER_READ)` — three separate `BeginOneTimeCommands`/`EndOneTimeCommands` pairs, so three submissions and three full `vkQueueWaitIdle` calls per texture. **Done:** `TransitionImageLayout` and `CopyBufferToImage` are each split into a **recording** half (`RecordImageLayoutTransition`, `RecordBufferToImageCopy`, taking the caller's `VkCommandBuffer`) and a thin standalone wrapper that opens one, records, and ends. Every other caller keeps the wrapper and is unchanged. The texture-creation path and `UpdatePixels` each open one buffer and record all three steps into it. Ordering inside the buffer is exactly what the barriers express, so batching changes the number of waits and nothing else. **A wrong turn, recorded because the measurement is what caught it:** the first attempt batched `UpdatePixels` only, and the count stayed at 144 — a freshly created texture never goes through `UpdatePixels`, it uploads from the constructor. Changing a path and *not* re-measuring would have shipped a no-op described as a fix. **The numbers, measured back to back on the same machine, four runs each rather than one:** command count 144 → **48**, which is deterministic and device-independent; wall clock 36.4/38.5/38.9/43.8 ms → 32.5/35.4/36.3/66.2 ms; wait share 36.7–40.3 % → 30.8–36.5 %. **What that does and does not support:** the structural claim is exact. The wall-clock improvement is real but modest — roughly 10 % at the median — and one *after* run landed at 66.2 ms, so no single run supports a speed claim on a machine shared with another session's builds. The row states the medians and the spread rather than the flattering number. **Test:** `Vulkan_OneTimeCommandCost` leg A tightened from "at least one each" to **exactly** one each, which is the assertion that fails at 144 and passes at 48; the timing lines stay printed and unasserted for the reason `VULKAN-396` recorded. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **249/249**, full `ctest` **9081/9103** — which also covers `VULKAN-396`'s instrumentation, committed before this run existed. The 22 failures are the known audio/content/lifecycle/C-API set; the only difference from the previous row's list is one audio-timing case swapping for another — `CueTest.PlayScalesVolumeByRpcCurve…` out, `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCountOnlyDropsOnceAWholeChunkIsActuallyConsumed` in — and the newcomer **passes serially in 3.83 s**, so it is the same contention family rather than a regression. This run used `-j2` after a `-j3` attempt was OOM-killed by memory pressure from other work on the machine; the lower parallelism is a local remedy, not a project-wide change. |
| VULKAN-402 | The startup capability diagnostic wrote to stdout (found inside the "known failures") | ✅ | **Found by `VULKAN-475`'s discipline, and it is the reason that row demands failures be *attributed* rather than merely counted.** **Observed:** `GraphicsDeviceRendererTest.StartupDiagnosticNeverWritesToStdout` has been in this plan's "known audio/content/lifecycle/C-API set" since the first baseline, carried forward in every §7.5 row as a pre-existing failure unrelated to this renderer. It is not unrelated. Its captured stdout is `CNA: Vulkan capabilities -- device=llvmpipe …` — **this renderer's own startup line**, printed with `std::cout` at `VulkanRenderer.cpp:2746`. **Classification:** implementation bug, and a rule violation with its own test: stdout belongs to the program's output, so a host that pipes it gets this line mixed into its data. **The fix is one word** — `std::clog`, which every other diagnostic in this renderer already used; that `std::cout` was the **only** one in the file. **Why it hid for so long, which is the part worth keeping:** a failure attributed to a bucket stops being looked at. Nineteen failures were named "the known set" in row after row, and re-running them serially (`VULKAN-475`'s own stated discriminator) showed all nineteen reproduce — so none was a flake, and one of them was ours. Counting them was never going to find it; reading one was. **Test:** none added. `GraphicsDeviceRendererTest.StartupDiagnosticNeverWritesToStdout` is the regression guard, it already existed, and it now **passes** — going from red to green is stronger evidence than any test written afterwards. **Evidence:** that test 1/1, `^Vulkan_` **254/254**. |
| VULKAN-403 | Two `.cnj` effect tests asked the capability instead of the query (F-09's tail) | ✅ | **The second defect `VULKAN-475`'s attribution pass found inside the "known failures", and the first that was a *test* asking the wrong question rather than a renderer answering wrongly.** **Observed:** `CnjEffectTest.LoadsRealCnjFixture` and `CnjStockEffectTest.CustomGlslEffectStillWorks` were the **only two** of the eighteen remaining failures that do **not** reproduce on EasyGL — the attribution method being to run the same list against `cmake-build-easygl`, where sixteen reproduce and are therefore shared rather than this renderer's. Both fail with `[ShaderEffect] Compile error: SPIR-V size must be a multiple of 4 bytes`: the `.cnj` fixtures carry GLSL and this renderer's `ShaderEffect` takes SPIR-V. **The mistake is one `CLAUDE.md` names explicitly:** both tests gated on `GraphicsCapability::CustomEffects`, which means the renderer **accepts** an effect, not that it **runs your shader source**. Vulkan accepts effects and reports `true`, so the gate opened and the test then asserted a GLSL payload was valid. The comment beside the gate had the right instinct — "gate on the capability rather than the renderer name" — and the wrong query. `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` exists for exactly this distinction and is `false` here. **Done:** both gates now also ask that query, so a renderer that does not execute shader source takes the same branch as one with no compiler at all. **Checked, not assumed, that this does not hollow the tests out:** EasyGL's `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` returns `true` (`EasyGLRenderer.hpp:1608`), so on that renderer both still take the `EXPECT_TRUE(IsEffectValid())` path — verified by building and running them in `cmake-build-easygl`, where they pass. A gate that silently disabled the test everywhere would be worse than the failure it removed. **Why this is not `VULKAN-256`:** that row is about **not** porting 37 EasyGL shader tests, and says so. These two are different — they run in *every* build, on shared content fixtures, and were failing rather than being skipped. **Evidence:** both tests 2/2 on Vulkan **and** 2/2 on EasyGL; `^Vulkan_` **254/254**. The known-failure count drops from 18 to **16**. |
| VULKAN-404 | `vkMapMemory` maps 8 000 bytes of a 3 404-byte allocation (validation) | ✅ | **Fixed. The row's arithmetic was right and its mechanism was wrong, and correcting that is what found the fix.** **Reproduced first, per leg:** the fixture forks eight legs, and exactly one produces the message — `--leg=G1`, the *growing* runtime resize (37×23 → 50×40). `G2` shrinks and is clean, and so are the other six. **Measured, not inferred:** a `gdb` breakpoint on `ReadBackbuffer` prints `w=37 h=23 | extent=37x23 allocWH=0x0 valid=0` then `w=50 h=40 | extent=50x40 allocWH=37x23 valid=1`. So the map is **50·40·4 = 8 000 = 0x1f40** of the **new** extent out of **37·23·4 = 3 404 = 0xd4c** allocated for the **old** one. (This row said 7 936; 0x1f40 is 8 000. The corrected figure is exactly the new backbuffer, which is the point — it is not a near-miss.) **The premise this row carried, and why it was wrong:** it guessed *“the staging buffer sized from the full swapchain extent while this test asks for a sub-region”*. There is no sub-region here. The staging buffer is deliberately whole-frame and the request was whole-frame too; what was stale was the **allocation**, left behind by the swapchain the resize destroyed. **Root cause:** `RecreateSwapchain` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:3613`) rebuilt the swapchain, the image views, the depth resources and the framebuffers, and left `readbackStagingValid_` **true**. `ReadBackbuffer` then took its no-submit cache path — and the reallocation that grows the staging buffer lives inside `RecordCommandBuffer`'s deferred-readback block, which only a submit records. So the one path that could have resized the buffer was exactly the path being skipped. **The second consequence, which is worse than the map and which no test asserted:** that cached path would have served the pre-resize 37×23 frame *reinterpreted at the new 50-pixel width*. A caller reading the backbuffer after a resize with no intervening clear or draw got the old picture, not a blank one and not an error. This fixture does not check post-resize pixel exactness (its own comment says so and hands that to the Software control), which is why 4/4 passed over undefined behaviour. **Done — two lines, one defect.** (a) `RecreateSwapchain` clears `readbackStagingValid_`, which is the correctness half: the cache describes a swapchain that no longer exists. (b) `ReadBackbuffer` treats the cache as usable only when it actually covers the frame about to be mapped — `readbackStagingBuf_ != VK_NULL_HANDLE && readbackAllocW_ >= fullW && readbackAllocH_ >= fullH`, the same two-dimension test the reallocation below it already uses — which is the safety half: the map size is bounded by the allocation locally, so no future caller can reintroduce the overstep by forgetting to invalidate. **Test:** `Vulkan_BackbufferReadbackDimension` gains `CheckValidationClean()`, called from `Finish()` so **every leg** judges its own process: layer liveness (`VulkanRenderer::IsValidationActiveEXT()`) first, because an empty list from a layer that never loaded is not evidence, then `GetValidationMessagesEXT()` empty. Per leg rather than per suite because only one leg reaches the defect, and a suite-wide count would not say which. The block is `#if defined(CNA_RENDERER_VULKAN)`, so the six other renderers registering this source are untouched. **Mutation-checked, both halves:** with (a) and (b) both reverted, `G1` is **5/6** and names the message verbatim while `A1` stays 4/4 — the leg that fails is the leg that resizes. With (b) alone restored, `G1` is 6/6 again, which is how the safety half was shown to bound the map on its own rather than being decoration. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254**; the suite-wide validation inventory `VULKAN-009` took drops **3 → 2**, and the two that remain are `VULKAN-405` and `VULKAN-406`. Full `ctest` in §7.5. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-405 | A `VkFramebuffer` outlives the device it belongs to (validation) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the row's proposed test could not have caught it — that is the substance of this row.** **Reproduced standalone:** `cna_test_vulkan_mrt_mixed_formats` prints `2/2 PASS` and then `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking - For VkDevice …, VkFramebuffer … has not been destroyed.` The test binds two `RenderTarget2D`s through `SetRenderTargets` and exits **without presenting**, so the MRT proxy created for that bind is never replayed. **Root cause:** a `VulkanMRTProxy` is reference-counted. The destructor's Step 1b reset `mrtProxy_` and cleared `retiredMrtProxies_` — the renderer's own two handles — but **four other members hold a share**: `currentRT_` for as long as the MRT is bound, and `Pending3DDraw::rt` / `PendingBatch::rt` / `PendingClear::rt` for every record the frame never replayed. Those members are destroyed **after** the destructor body, i.e. after `vkDestroyDevice`, and `~VulkanMRTProxy` then finds `device_` already null and returns without freeing anything. **A second defect the report did not name:** that same early-return reads `owner_->device_` out of a `VulkanRenderer` whose destructor has already finished — a use-after-free on every path where a proxy outlives its renderer, benign only because the read happens to land on freed-but-unwritten memory. **The fix chosen, and why not the obvious one.** Dropping the four shares in Step 1b works and was measured to work, but it is a fix that has to **enumerate the holders** and stays correct only while that list does — `VulkanSpriteBatchRenderer::activeRT_` is a fifth holder the renderer cannot reach at all, because it belongs to a public `SpriteBatch`. So the fix is the release-then-disconnect pair every other owned Vulkan resource in this file already uses: `VulkanMRTProxy::ReleaseVulkanResources()` destroys the framebuffer while the device is alive, `DisconnectOwner()` makes a surviving proxy harmless, and Step 1b applies both to `mrtProxy_` and to every entry of `retiredMrtProxies_` — **which together are every proxy that exists**, since a proxy reaches the second list exactly when it leaves the first. Who else holds a share stops mattering. `~VulkanMRTProxy` is unchanged and becomes a no-op after the release. **Test, and the premise this row got wrong:** the row asked for *“`Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats` gains an assertion that the renderer's validation-message set is empty at teardown.”* It cannot. The message is emitted **during** `vkDestroyDevice`, which happens after the last line of the test body, and `validationMessages_` is a member of the very object being destroyed — there is no moment at which a test can read it and see this entry. The instrument that can is CTest's output gate, which this file's neighbouring Vulkan registrations already use: `set_tests_properties(Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats PROPERTIES FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION "\\[Vulkan Validation\\]")`. It is deliberately the whole `[Vulkan Validation]` prefix rather than the `"VUID-|Validation Error|Validation Warning|SYNC-HAZARD"` pattern the neighbours use: an Object Tracking report contains none of those words, so the neighbours' pattern would have let this exact message through. No test source changed. **Mutation-checked:** with the two release calls removed, `ctest -R '^Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats$'` **fails** on the output gate while the test's own `2/2 PASS` is unchanged — so the gate fails for the leak and for nothing else. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **254/254**, and its `[Vulkan Validation]` inventory is now **0** — `VULKAN-009`'s three-message inventory is fully closed. Full `ctest` in §7.5; it is mandatory here because the change is in the renderer destructor, which every test in this configuration runs. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-406 | An image array layer is submitted in `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_UNDEFINED` (validation) | ✅ | **Fixed, and the count was wrong in the direction that matters: it is not one layer, it is all six.** **Reproduced:** `cna_test_vulkan_cube_volume_setdata_contract` alone, one message naming `mipLevel = 0, arrayLayer = 2`. **The resource:** the `RenderTargetCube` built by `RunRenderTargetCubeCheck` — `mipMap=false`, `DepthFormat::None`, `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents` — whose face 2 is then read back. **This row's stated mechanism was wrong.** It said `TransitionImageLayout`'s barrier hardcodes a single layer, so a cube's other five faces are never transitioned by that helper. True of the helper, irrelevant here: **no cube class uses it**, and both cube classes say so in their own comments (`VulkanTextureCubeRenderer::GetData`, `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer::GetData`) — they write inline barriers scoped to the exact face and level. **The actual defect:** `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer`'s constructor ran its up-front all-layers/all-levels `UNDEFINED → SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` barrier only `if (levelCount_ > 1 || preserveContents_)` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:14360`). Both conditions are about **binding** — Task 907's mip-blit cascade precondition and REMED-GFX-136's `LOAD_OP_LOAD` initialLayout — and binding is not the only way a layer is reached. A face nobody renders is still readable and still sampleable, and both of those paths assert the opposite: `GetData`'s own barrier declares `oldLayout = VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` with the comment *“every level of every layer sits in SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL outside a render pass (the constructor's up-front barrier …)”*, and `cubeView_` hands all six layers to a descriptor that declares the same. On a single-level `DiscardContents` cube that constructor barrier never ran, so the comment was false and the barrier lied about a layout the image was never in. **The fourth time this class has lagged its 2D twin** (`VULKAN-215` records the first three): `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer` has done exactly this barrier **unconditionally** since Task 878. **Done:** the condition is deleted; the barrier is unconditional, matching the 2D twin. Cost is one extra one-time command at construction for the single-level discard case — the same one the 2D twin already pays, and the price of a layout that is defined rather than merely lucky. The depth barrier keeps its `preserveContents_` guard, which is correct: a discarding pass declares depth `initialLayout UNDEFINED` and reads it from nowhere else. **Test:** the existing contract fixture, in the two ways the row asked for. (1) `RunRenderTargetCubeCheck` now probes **all six faces** instead of face 2 — one face cannot tell a cube whose layers are each independently initialised from one whose single touched layer happened to be right. (2) `CheckValidationClean()` asserts layer liveness and then an empty `GetValidationMessagesEXT()`, `#if defined(CNA_RENDERER_VULKAN)` so the **thirteen** other renderers registering this source are untouched. **Mutation-checked, and this is the result worth keeping:** restoring the condition turns the test red with **six** messages, `arrayLayer = 0` through `arrayLayer = 5`. The original inventory saw one only because one face was asked for. **Cross-renderer check, because the fixture is shared:** rebuilt and run in `cmake-build-easygl` — `EasyGL_CubeVolume_SetDataContract` passes with the six-face probe, so the extension is not a Vulkan-shaped assumption. The other twelve registrations cannot be built here and are unchanged in behaviour by construction (the added block is renderer-guarded; the six-face loop repeats a probe each already ran once). **Evidence:** `Vulkan_CubeVolume_SetDataContract` **56/56 → 63/63**, `^Vulkan_` **254/254**, full `ctest` in §7.5. The `^Vulkan_` run's `[Vulkan Validation]` inventory falls **2 → 1**, and the one that remains is `VULKAN-405`'s framebuffer. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-407 | Three resource classes are untracked, so one that outlives its device leaks every Vulkan object it owns | ✅ | **Fixed, and the mutation results are the part worth keeping: each of the three registrations, removed alone, reproduces exactly its own class's object list.** **Observed (unchanged from the opening text):** `MetalResourceHealth.RenderTargetCubeRendererEscapesThroughTextureCubeBaseMove` produced 10 `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking … has not been destroyed` messages — 7 `VkImageView`, 1 `VkImage`, 1 `VkDeviceMemory`, 1 `VkFramebuffer` — while passing. **Done:** `VulkanTexture3DRenderer`, `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` each gain the `ReleaseVulkanResources()`/`DisconnectOwner()` pair every other Vulkan-owning class here already had, split out of their existing destructors so the mechanism is untouched — the two cube classes still retire through the frame-fence-gated queue, the volume still frees directly. Each registers itself in a new live list at construction and removes itself at destruction, exactly as `VulkanTextureRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetRenderer` do, and the renderer's destructor walks the three new lists beside the two that were there. **The second half of the defect, which the report did not name:** `~VulkanTexture3DRenderer` and its two siblings each began `if (!owner_ || owner_->device_ == VK_NULL_HANDLE) return;` — reading a `VulkanRenderer` whose destructor had already finished. `DisconnectOwner()` is what settles that, and it is the reason the fix is this pair rather than a release call alone. **Test:** new CTest `Vulkan_ResourceOutlivesDevice` (`vulkan_resource_outlives_device_test.cpp`), **3/3**. It builds a `Texture3D`, a `TextureCube` and — through the same `TextureCube` base move the `MetalResourceHealth` case documents — a `RenderTargetCube`, keeps a `shared_ptr` to each renderer, and destroys the `GraphicsDevice` while all three are still held. **Where the discriminator lives, and why it is not in the test file.** The layer reports the leak *during* `vkDestroyDevice`, after the last statement that could read the renderer's message list and out of the object being destroyed; no in-process assertion can see it. `VULKAN-393`'s output gate can, and applies to this registration like every other. What the file itself asserts is that the escape really happened — three renderers alive after their device is gone — so a build that constructed nothing cannot pass the gate having proved nothing. **Mutation-checked, one registration at a time, and each names its own class:** removing `liveTexture3Ds_`'s registration reproduces `VkImage`+`VkDeviceMemory`+`VkImageView`; removing `liveTextureCubes_`'s reproduces the same three for the cube; removing `liveRenderTargetCubes_`'s reproduces **7 `VkImageView`, 1 `VkImage`, 1 `VkDeviceMemory`, 1 `VkFramebuffer`** — the original report's exact list. So the fix is attributed per class rather than in aggregate. **Not proved here, and named rather than implied:** the use-after-free half is argued from the code, not observed — an ASan build of `Vulkan_ResourceOutlivesDevice` is what would observe it, and ASan on this renderer is `VULKAN-397`. **Evidence:** `^Vulkan_` **256/256** plus `Metal_PortableHelpers` and the six `MetalResourceHealth` cases, **264/264** together, with **0** `[Vulkan Validation]` lines where there were 20. Full `ctest` in §7.5; mandatory here because the change is in the renderer destructor. **Dependencies:** none.
| VULKAN-408 | The validation gate reaches `Vulkan_*` only, and 8 861 other CTests run this renderer too | ✅ | **Done. The gate is now on every CTest in the configuration that can create a `VkDevice`, and the decision this row was required to record is a measured one rather than a preference.** **The decision: neither of the two options this row offered.** The root-level sweep was built first — a recursive walk of every directory's `TESTS` property, called once from the root `CMakeLists.txt` exactly the way `cna_apple_configure_all_bundles()` is, and for the same stated reason. **It does not work**, and the error is worth recording because it is not obvious from the documentation: `set_tests_properties()` is *directory-scoped*, so from the root it cannot touch a test another directory registered — `set_tests_properties Can not find test to add properties to: ContentLostProbe`. The `set_property(TEST … DIRECTORY …)` form that could is **CMake 3.28**, and this project's floor is **3.20**. **What was built instead: the two hooks that are always in the right scope.** (1) `cna_register_renderer_test()` in `cmake/TestHelpers.cmake` appends the pattern to every registration it makes — ~600 across every renderer's examples, including `Metal_PortableHelpers`, one of the two that carried the 20 messages. (2) `gtest_discover_tests(… PROPERTIES FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION …)` at its own call site in `cmake/UnitTests.cmake` reaches the **8 774** `PRE_TEST`-discovered cases, which do not exist at configure time at all and are therefore in no directory's `TESTS` property — the other carrier, `MetalResourceHealth.RenderTargetCubeRendererEscapesThroughTextureCubeBaseMove`. Verified in the generated files, not assumed. **One thing still had to stay directory-local, and it is a trap worth naming:** nine registrations in `modules/renderers/vulkan/examples/CMakeLists.txt` call `set_tests_properties(… FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION …)` **after** their registration, which *replaces* the property rather than adding to it. `cna_apply_vulkan_validation_gate()` re-merges over that directory's tests and must stay last in the file; checked in `CTestTestfile.cmake`, where `Vulkan_MrtMipFinalization` reads `"VUID-|…|SYNC-HAZARD;\\[Vulkan Validation\\]"`. **`VULKAN-393`'s directory-local first version is superseded rather than duplicated** — the mechanism and the (empty) exemption list now live in one place, `cmake/TestHelpers.cmake`. **Mutation-checked with exactly the discriminator this row named:** with `VULKAN-407`'s `liveRenderTargetCubes_` registration removed, **both** `MetalResourceHealth.RenderTargetCubeRendererEscapesThroughTextureCubeBaseMove` **and** `Metal_PortableHelpers` turn **red**. Before this row they passed while leaking ten Vulkan objects past `vkDestroyDevice`. **Applied only where a `VkDevice` can exist** (`CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER STREQUAL VULKAN` or `VULKAN` in `CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERERS`), so the other renderer configurations are untouched — the pattern would be inert there, but a gate nobody can trigger is still a gate somebody has to reason about. **Evidence:** full `ctest` in §7.5, run with the gate live on the whole suite. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-393`, `VULKAN-407`.

---

## 24. Phase 12 — Cross-renderer conformance (`VULKAN-430`–`VULKAN-469`)

The infrastructure already exists and is under-used: `cross_renderer_diagnostic_scene.cpp` is built
for EasyGL, Software, WebGPU **and Vulkan**, with `cna_diag_compare` as the comparator; three Vulkan
CTests already compare against EasyGL-authored golden PNGs. This phase turns that into a gate.

Rules for every row here: assert exact logical properties where the property is exact; use pixel
probes chosen to discriminate; use a tolerance only with a written justification; **never** demand
bit-identical pixels for floating-point rasterization or lighting; and never accept "renders without
throwing" as proof of a visual feature.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-430 | Make the shared diagnostic scene a real EasyGL↔Vulkan gate | ⬜ | `cna_diag_vulkan` and `cna_diag_easygl` already produce 64×64 RGBA8 dumps from one source. Add a script (not a CTest — it needs two builds) that runs both and diffs them with `cna_diag_compare` under a documented tolerance, plus a documented expected-difference list. Acceptance: the script fails when one renderer's output is deliberately perturbed. |
| VULKAN-431 | Register the shared 2D corpus on Vulkan | ⬜ | `cross_renderer_2d_corpus.cpp` is registered for EasyGL and Direct2D only. Register `cna_corpus2d_vulkan` and extend `VULKAN-430`'s script to diff the corpus too. |
| VULKAN-432 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for the stock-effect goldens | ⬜ | Register `easygl_basiceffect_golden_test.cpp`, `easygl_alphatesteffect_golden_test.cpp`, `easygl_dualtextureeffect_golden_test.cpp` on Vulkan against the same `examples/golden/` PNGs, following the precedent of `Vulkan_PbrEffect_Golden` (repo-root working directory, per-check tolerance). Each tolerance is justified in the registration comment. Depends on `VULKAN-014`'s policy. |
| VULKAN-433 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for `EnvironmentMapEffect` and `SkinnedEffect` | ⬜ | Same for `easygl_environmentmapeffect_golden_test.cpp` and `easygl_skinnedeffect_golden_test.cpp`. `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s golden is one where EasyGL is itself under suspicion (`VULKAN-260`); if Vulkan disagrees, the XNA semantics decide which is right and the golden may need to change rather than Vulkan. |
| VULKAN-434 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for the state goldens | ⬜ | `easygl_blendstate_additive_golden_test.cpp`, `easygl_depthstencilstate_write_enable_golden_test.cpp`, `easygl_rasterizerstate_cullmode_golden_test.cpp`. These are exact-arithmetic cases; a tolerance above a couple of LSBs needs a reason. |
| VULKAN-435 | Cross-renderer golden comparison for the 2D goldens | ⬜ | `easygl_spritebatch_rotation_golden_test.cpp`, `easygl_texture_filter_linear_golden_test.cpp`, `easygl_goldenimage_smoke_test.cpp`, `easygl_pixeltestgame_smoke_test.cpp`. Filtering differs legitimately between backends; the rotation and smoke cases should be near-exact. |
| VULKAN-436 | Register the shared sample scenes on Vulkan | ⬜ | `easygl_sample_dualtexture_swap_test.cpp`, `_keyboard_cube3d_`, `_layered_blend_`, `_moving_quad3d_`, `easygl_fullscreen_field_test.cpp`. These are whole-scene smoke tests through the public API; they are the cheapest broad regression net Vulkan does not have. |
| VULKAN-437 | Write the cross-renderer conformance methodology down | ⬜ | A section in `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` (or a dedicated doc) stating: what must match exactly, what may differ and why, how a tolerance is chosen, and the standing rule that a conformance test must be able to fail for the defect it claims to detect. Depends on `VULKAN-430`–`VULKAN-436` having produced real numbers. |

## 25. Phase 13 — Final parity gate (`VULKAN-470`–`VULKAN-489`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| VULKAN-470 | Re-verify capability reporting against measured behaviour | ✅ | **Every one of the 93 snapshot entries now has a test that observes the behaviour, and four capabilities did not before this row.** The audit ran against `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot as its oracle. **The 19 `GraphicsCapability` members, each with its observer:** `ThreeD` → the 65 stock-effect draw tests; `DepthStencilBuffer` → `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_CompareFunction` and `Vulkan_DepthRangeContract`; `MultiSampleAntiAliasing` → `Vulkan_MSAA_4x_Readback` plus `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` leg E, which compares the report against the count `ApplyMultiSampleCount` actually reaches; `MultipleRenderTargets` → `Vulkan_MRT_MixedFormats`; `AnisotropicFiltering` → `Vulkan_TextureAnisotropic_DualTextureEffect`; `WireFrame` → `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`; `OcclusionQuery` → `Vulkan_OcclusionQuery_PixelCount` and `_Precision`; `CustomEffects` → `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV`; `Texture3D` → `Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`; `Instancing` → `Vulkan_DrawInstanced_3Instances`; `StencilBuffer` → `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_StencilOps`; `AdditiveBlending` → `Vulkan_AdditiveBlendContract`; `MultiStreamVertexInput`, `FloatRenderTargets` and `HalfFloatRenderTargets` → `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` leg C, which already observed each false report as a real refusal. **The four that had no observer, closed here rather than filed as gaps:** `HalfFloatTextureLinearFiltering` — its falseness is one step earlier than filtering, and the new leg shows why: a half-float `Texture2D` cannot be created at all, so there is no surface for a sampler to filter (and the refusal names `GraphicsProfile.Reach` as the layer that turned it away, which is worth knowing — it is the profile's restriction, not the renderer's). `IndirectDraw` and `ComputeShaders` — both need a storage buffer for their arguments and their memory, and `CreateStorageBuffer` returns null here, so the route does not exist rather than being checked and turned away. That is a **stronger** observation than a refusal message, and it is the only one available: `DrawPrimitivesIndirectEXT` takes its argument buffer by reference, so there is literally nothing to hand it. `CompiledEffects` — false because `CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS` is **OFF** in `cmake-build-vulkan`, verified in that directory's `CMakeCache.txt`. Only a build that turns it on can observe the other answer, so this entry is **build-scoped**, and no test here can close it; that is stated rather than papered over. **The derived rows:** the 30 `RendererFeature` rows are covered by `VULKAN-008`'s 19 mirror-pair assertions plus `Vulkan_ShaderDialectContract` and `Vulkan_ShaderEffect_SpirV` for the source-execution and dialect rows; the 10 `RendererLimit` rows by the multi-stream refusal, the storage-buffer absence and the snapshot's own limit-vs-query invariant; the 27 render-target format rows by `Vulkan_SurfaceFormat_Throws` for the class, with `Vector4` and `HdrBlendable` observed individually. **The one thing that looks like a blocker and is not:** `GetShaderDialectEXT` says `GlslVulkan` while every `feature.ShaderDialect*` row says `unsupported`. Under this row's own rule — *true with no reachable path, or false with a working path* — the feature rows are **right**: there is no GLSL-source path, the renderer takes SPIR-V. It is the query that cannot express that, which is `VULKAN-264`, not a gate blocker. Saying so explicitly matters in both directions: the gate is not falsely blocked, and the discrepancy is not quietly cleared. **Evidence:** `Vulkan_CapabilityContract` grows from 25/25 to **27/27**. |
| VULKAN-471 | Confirm every in-scope parity-matrix row is resolved | ✅ | **Run three times, and the third (2026-09-06) is the one that closes it: §10 has **zero** rows reading `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT`, down from ten.** **The distance travelled, kept because it is the point of re-running rather than re-deciding:** 2026-09-05 measured ten, blocked on `VULKAN-023`, `-025`, `-026`, `-055`, `-090`, `-094`, `-141`, `-392`, `-394`, `-395` and `-396`; two more were **stale rather than open** and were corrected then (`PixelCountIsPreciseEXT`, whose owner had closed while the row still said the virtual was not overridden, and "Capability reaching the renderer at all"). A second run on 2026-09-06 had one left — *Full-device stalls in ordinary paths* — whose remaining third was the mid-frame render-target flush that counting the other two revealed. `VULKAN-399` measured that one and it is now `PARITY`, because rendering to a target and sampling it never reaches the flush at all. **What this row does NOT claim, stated so ✅ is not read as more than it is:** §10 still holds **46 `TEST_GAP`** and **17 `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP`** rows out of 138. Those are not this row's subject — `VULKAN-472` owns the implementation gaps and `VULKAN-473` the test gaps, and §28's definition of done needs both. What `VULKAN-471` owns is the `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT` state itself: §26 rule 8 calls it "a real state with an owner", and a row in it is one nobody has yet looked at hard enough to classify. **There are none left, so every row in the matrix now carries a verdict somebody measured.** **Current tally, so the next run has a baseline:** 138 rows — 46 `TEST_GAP`, 37 `PARITY`, 17 `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP`, 16 `VULKAN_STRONGER`, 5 `NOT_APPLICABLE`, 3 `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE`, 1 `CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`. |
| VULKAN-472 | Confirm every `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` row has a closed task | 🟨 | Each of F-01…F-14 either has a ✅ task or a ⛔ row with the reason written here. **Measured 2026-09-06: 10 of the 14 findings are closed** — F-01, F-02, F-03, F-04, F-05, F-06, F-07, F-12, F-13, F-14 — leaving four, each with its owner named so the remaining distance is a list rather than a feeling. **F-08** → `VULKAN-264`, the shader-dialect enumerator that cannot distinguish GLSL source from SPIR-V bytecode; it is a **C-ABI change** and is deliberately blocked on agreement with `plan_binding.md`, not on work. **F-09** → `VULKAN-256`, whose own closing condition depends on `VULKAN-254`. **F-10** → `VULKAN-170`/`VULKAN-174`, the compressed and packed `SurfaceFormat`s. **F-11** → `VULKAN-170`, and its row already records that it is **inert** until F-10 closes, because every Vulkan texture is `Color` today. **Worth stating for whoever picks up `VULKAN-170`:** its acceptance forbids widening a verdict past what `CreateTexture` can allocate, so classifying formats honestly and *implementing* them are the same task, not two — which is why this row's remainder is one large feature and two rows that follow it, not four independent gaps. 🟨 rather than ⬜ because the confirmation has been run and produced a definite answer; what is left is the four findings it names. |
| VULKAN-473 | Confirm every meaningful `TEST_GAP` row is closed | ⬜ | Every `TEST_GAP` row in §10 points at a registered, passing Vulkan CTest, or at a written justification for why it is not a Vulkan requirement. |
| VULKAN-474 | Confirm no silent no-op remains where EasyGL implements the behaviour | ✅ | **Re-run 2026-09-06 with the same committed tool, and the movement since `VULKAN-027` is accounted for exactly rather than merely observed to be smaller.** **The numbers:** 186 virtuals unchanged; both-override 102 → **106**; EasyGL-only 54 → **50**; Vulkan-only 3 → **7**; neither 27 → **23**. §31's census table now carries both measurements side by side, because "it improved" is not a verification — matching each moved virtual to the row that moved it is. **The four that left EasyGL-only** are `IEffectRenderer`'s `SetUniformFloatArray`/`Vec2Array`/`Vec3Array`/`Mat4Array`, which `VULKAN-265` made Vulkan override in order to **refuse**. That is the distinction this row exists to enforce: refusing by name is implementing the contract; inheriting `{}` is the silent no-op it forbids. **The four that joined Vulkan-only** came out of *neither*, not out of EasyGL-only, so they are not this row's subject at all — `GetAppliedMultiSampleCountEXT` (`VULKAN-347`) and `GetAppliedBackBufferFormatEXT` plus both `GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT` virtuals (`VULKAN-348`) are places where Vulkan now answers and EasyGL still takes the default, correctly, because EasyGL substitutes nothing. **The acceptance:** zero rows read "Vulkan inherits a no-op default while EasyGL implements it" without a justification. All **50** survivors carry one, individually, in §31 A.1 — and the two whose justification was "owned by an open row" when `VULKAN-027` wrote it have since been answered and their cells updated in place (`CanBeginDrawEXT` by `VULKAN-026`, `AcquireThreadContextLeaseEXT` by `VULKAN-025`). **No new gap was found by the re-run**, which is the expected result and is stated rather than left implied: this row's value is that the count cannot drift silently, since the tool is committed and the census is written down twice. |
| VULKAN-475 | Full-suite green, with pre-existing failures named | ✅ | **`cmake --build cmake-build-vulkan` exits **0**, and every remaining failure is named, attributed by cross-renderer reproduction, and justified. Attributing them — rather than counting them — found three defects that had been carried as somebody else's for the whole implementation phase.** **The discriminator this row names, applied first:** all **19** failures were re-run individually with `-j1`, and **all 19 reproduced**. So none was a contention flake, and every one was a real, standing failure — which is what made reading them worthwhile. (Separately, five *other* tests that failed only in parallel runs — `CueTest.PlayScalesVolumeByRpcCurve…`, `DynamicSoundEffectInstanceTest.PendingBufferCount…`, `TwoProcessLoopbackTest.HostMigration…`, `ENetDiscoveryServiceTest…`, `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection` — do pass serially and are load-sensitive, not members of this set.) **The attribution method:** run the same list against `cmake-build-easygl`. A failure both renderers reproduce is shared; one only Vulkan reproduces is this renderer's. **Result: 16 shared, 3 ours.** **The three that were ours, each now fixed and holding its own row:** `GraphicsDeviceRendererTest.StartupDiagnosticNeverWritesToStdout` was capturing **this renderer's own** `CNA: Vulkan capabilities …` line on stdout (`VULKAN-402`); `CnjEffectTest.LoadsRealCnjFixture` and `CnjStockEffectTest.CustomGlslEffectStillWorks` gated on `CustomEffects` where the question is `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` (`VULKAN-403`). **The 16 that remain, named and attributed:** audio timing — `CueTest.PlayingCueNaturallyTransitions…`, `CueTest.PauseAfterNaturalCompletionIsANoOp`, `SoundBankTest.IsInUseFalseSoonAfterFireAndForget…`, `WaveBankTest.IsInUseFalseSoonAfterCue…`; content — `ContentManagerVideoXnbTest.TheObjectReferencedForm…`; device lifecycle — the six `GraphicsDeviceSubsystemLifecycleTest` cases, whose subject is a video-subsystem reference count that comes out 2-acquired/1-released **on both renderers**; networking — `PacketReaderTest.ReadColorReadsSixteenBytesAsFloats`; platform — `GameWindowPlatformTest.DelegatesStateAndGeometry…`; and the three C-API gates `CApiCoverageMatrix`, `CApiLimitations`, `CApiReleaseGate`. **Justification for leaving all sixteen:** each reproduces identically under EasyGL, so none is caused by this renderer and none is this plan's to fix — `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is to reproduce upstream bugs, not fix them. The evidence is the EasyGL run, not an assertion. **The lesson, written down because it cost three defects:** a failure filed into a bucket stops being read, and "the known set" is therefore the best hiding place a defect has. Nineteen were carried under that label — by this plan's own rows — and three of them were ours. **Confirming run after the three fixes: `cmake --build` exit **0**, full `ctest` **9095/9111**, **16** failures — exactly the sixteen named above, no others, and none in `Vulkan_*`. Gathered in two `-I` halves for the OOM reason §7.5 records. |
| VULKAN-476 | Dedicated Vulkan suite green | ✅ | **`ctest -R '^Vulkan_' -j2` → 254/254, and there are no skips at all to justify.** Measured 2026-09-06 under `env -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY DISPLAY=:99 SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11`, 164 s wall clock. The suite grew from 218 at the start of this plan's implementation phase to **254**, every addition carrying its own row. **The limitation this row is required to name, because "green" without it would be read as more than it is:** the device is **`llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7)`**, a software rasterizer, not the machine's `AMD Radeon 780M (RADV PHOENIX)`. That is not a choice — §7 records why: under Xvfb, RADV reports `No DRI3 support detected - required for presentation`, so the renderer's own present-capable-queue check rejects it and selects llvmpipe. **What must be rerun where:** the whole `^Vulkan_` suite on the real display against the RADV ICD, which needs the owner's go-ahead rather than a test-runner change, and which `VULKAN-008`'s capability snapshot is already built for — it classifies its 93 entries `Fixed` or `Device` precisely so a hardware run compares rather than fails. **Why this is nevertheless ✅ rather than 🟨:** the row's acceptance is about *skips*, and there are none; the device question is a re-run this row is asked to name, not a gap it is asked to close. `VULKAN-487` re-audits at gate time and is where a hardware run belongs. |
| VULKAN-477 | Validation clean | ✅ | **Closed on the wider reading, not the literal one — which is why it took four rows and two measurements rather than a re-run.** **What this row measured on 2026-09-06 and refused to close on:** `VULKAN-393`'s gate passed over 254 `Vulkan_*` CTests with an empty exemption list, and that satisfied this row's own wording. Taking the same inventory over the whole 9 117-test configuration — every one of which runs this renderer — found **20** more messages, all `vkDestroyDevice(): Object Tracking … has not been destroyed`, in two registrations of one test outside that prefix. §28 point 8 is about validation being clean, not about one prefix's worth of it, so this stayed 🟨 and opened two rows. **Both are closed.** `VULKAN-407`: `VulkanTexture3DRenderer`, `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` and `VulkanRenderTargetCubeRenderer` were in no live list, so an instance outliving its `GraphicsDevice` freed nothing and read a destroyed `VulkanRenderer`; all three now release and disconnect like every other resource, and the whole-suite count went **20 → 0**. `VULKAN-408`: the gate now reaches every CTest that can create a `VkDevice` — ~600 `cna_register_renderer_test` registrations plus **8 774** `gtest_discover_tests` cases — instead of one directory's 254. **The final evidence, in the order it has to be read:** (1) the full `ctest` after `VULKAN-407` has **zero** `[Vulkan Validation]` lines in `LastTest.log`, across all 9 117 tests, where this row measured 20; (2) the full `ctest` after `VULKAN-408`, with the gate live on the whole suite, produces **no gate hit at all** — its 24 failures are `VULKAN-475`'s named 16 plus 8 contention artefacts that pass serially; (3) the exemption list `CNA_VULKAN_VALIDATION_GATE_EXEMPT` is **empty**, so there is no CNA-attributable entry because there is no entry. **The gate cannot pass vacuously:** four tests assert `VulkanRenderer::IsValidationActiveEXT()` before judging anything, so a build in which the layer never loaded goes red rather than reporting a clean suite. **What this does NOT claim:** the device is `llvmpipe`, not hardware. A validation layer is driver-independent for the classes it checks — object tracking, layout mismatches, map sizes are all layer bookkeeping rather than driver behaviour — but sync-validation hazards and anything driver-reported are not proven here. `VULKAN-482`'s environment note stands. **Dependencies:** `VULKAN-009`, `VULKAN-393`, `VULKAN-404`, `VULKAN-405`, `VULKAN-406`, `VULKAN-407`, `VULKAN-408` — all ✅.
| VULKAN-480 | Write `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` | ⬜ | The capability boundary for this renderer in one place, in the style of `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`: what is supported, what is deliberately not, what differs from EasyGL and why, the shader dialect, the format list, and the environment the numbers were measured on. Vulkan is the only mature renderer family without such a document. **STARTED, not by this row: `VULKAN-098` created the file** because its own acceptance required the depth-range divergence to be written down, and there was nowhere to write it. What exists is one section, plus a header that says in as many words that the document is incomplete and that this row owns the rest — so it cannot be read as the finished capability boundary. **What this row still owes:** everything else the acceptance names, and it should be written AFTER `VULKAN-470`–`VULKAN-474`, so the claims are checked rather than remembered. The sections already earned by completed rows and not yet written up: the declaration-driven vertex layout and which four factories are exempt (`VULKAN-144`), the stride refusals and what each family accepts (`VULKAN-148`, `-151`, `-155`, `-156`), the `_USCALED` device requirement for `Byte4` bone indices (`VULKAN-151`), the descriptor-allocation contract (`VULKAN-391`), the shader dialect (`VULKAN-250`), and the swap-interval/present-mode mapping (`VULKAN-332`, `-333`). |
| VULKAN-481 | Correct the stale current-status claims in the shared docs | ✅ | **All six corrected, each saying what was true when written and what is true now, and each citing the test that settles it — the row's own requirement, and the reason none of them was simply deleted.** **D-01** `docs/texture3d-texturecube-support.md`: "total no-op — neither `VulkanTexture3DRenderer` nor `VulkanTextureCubeRenderer` overrides `GetData`, the caller's buffer is left untouched, no exception, no error, no log". Both override it now, through a host-visible staging buffer; cited: `Vulkan_Texture3D_Slices_RoundTrip`, `Vulkan_TextureCube_PartialRect_RoundTrip`, `Vulkan_TextureCube_Mip_RoundTrip`. **D-02** `docs/rendertarget-support.md`, two cells: MSAA "honestly reports `0`, not implemented" and mips "`LevelCount` correct, no real GPU mips". Both real; cited: `Vulkan_MSAA_4x_Readback`, `Vulkan_MRT_MsaaResolve`, `Vulkan_MsaaDepthContract`, `Vulkan_MsaaMipReadback`, `Vulkan_MrtMipFinalization`, plus `VULKAN-347` for the applied count. **D-03** feature matrix: Texture2D mip-level `SetData` "silent no-op" — `UpdatePixelsLevel` performs a real staged upload. **D-04** `IGraphicsRenderer.hpp`'s header comment: "Vulkan always allocates a combined depth+stencil buffer … regardless of the exact value requested … a real architectural change, tracked as Task 911". Task 911 landed; each target picks its own `VkFormat` and `None` means no attachment. **The correction keeps the one place the old sentence still half-applies** — the back buffer allocates depth unconditionally — and points at `VULKAN-349`, which measured that and left it. **D-05** three feature-matrix cells reading "not separately re-audited"/"not separately re-confirmed this pass". **These were honest phrasing for unmeasured cells rather than false claims**, so the correction had to be careful not to replace an honest gap with an overclaim: each now names the tests that cover it, and the SpriteFont cell says explicitly that flip is covered for sprites by `Vulkan_SpriteEffects_Flip` rather than by a SpriteFont-specific test. **D-07** feature matrix: the July baseline "`ctest` 126/127 — 1 remaining failure, `Vulkan_DepthBias`". Superseded by 254/254 and 9095/9111, and two of its claims were wrong in ways worth showing: `Vulkan_DepthBias` was a **test** written against OpenGL's depth range rather than a renderer defect (§7.4), and the numbers came from **Mesa lavapipe**, which the line never said. **One more, added by `VULKAN-055` and not in the original list:** `docs/migration-guide.md` listed "Vulkan `RasterizerState.DepthBias` has no effect" among the **known issues**. Withdrawn, struck through rather than deleted — a known-issues list that warns readers away from a working feature does the same harm as one that hides a broken one. **`NEXT.md`'s share stays with `VULKAN-482`**, which owns it by name. |
| VULKAN-482 | Update `NEXT.md` with the measured Vulkan baseline | ✅ | **Done, and two of this row's own premises were wrong — both corrected here rather than worked around.** **Premise 1: "the Vulkan lines currently describe a lavapipe run."** They say **llvmpipe**, not lavapipe: `NEXT.md` carries exactly one Vulkan suite figure, `VULKAN 211 = 210 pass + the accepted Vulkan_DepthBias llvmpipe residual`. The distinction matters because the plan's own §7 records the device as `llvmpipe (LLVM 19.1.7)` with `DRIVER_ID_MESA_LLVMPIPE`, and a row that misnames the driver invites a reader to go looking for a run that never happened. **Premise 2: "replace them with the measured RADV numbers."** RADV numbers cannot be produced in this environment at all — under Xvfb it reports `No DRI3 support detected - required for presentation` and the renderer's own present-capable-queue check rejects it, which is why every measurement in this plan is llvmpipe's (`VULKAN-476` records the same constraint). Producing them needs the real display **and the owner's go-ahead**, which is not something a task row can assume. **What was actually done:** a dated section added to `NEXT.md` carrying the measured baseline — `^Vulkan_` **254/254** with no skips, full `ctest` **9095/9111** with all 16 failures reproducing under EasyGL — and naming the device explicitly, because a suite number without one says less than it looks. **The old figure was left exactly where it is.** `NEXT.md` is a cross-branch ledger whose own top says existing history is not rewritten, so the entry supersedes rather than edits, and states what is now known about the old line: the suite is much larger, and `Vulkan_DepthBias` was never a renderer defect but a **test** written against OpenGL's depth range (§7.4). **What a hardware run would need is named rather than deferred silently:** the real display, the owner's go-ahead, and `VULKAN-008`'s snapshot, which classifies its 93 entries `Fixed` or `Device` precisely so a different adapter compares instead of failing. |
| VULKAN-487 | Re-audit against the **then-current** EasyGL before declaring parity | ⬜ | Recompute `VULKAN-007`'s comparison against EasyGL as it exists at gate time, not as it existed on 2026-09-04 (`VULKAN-010` recorded the starting point). Every EasyGL CTest added in the meantime is classified with §9.3's taxonomy, and every `REAL_VULKAN_TEST_GAP` or `REAL_VULKAN_IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` it finds becomes a new `VULKAN-*` row **before** this task can close. Parity cannot be declared while this task has open children. |

---

## 26. Dynamic backlog-expansion rules

This plan is **expected to grow**. That is the mechanism that prevents false closure, not a sign the
planning was incomplete.

1. **An audit task cannot close on a finding.** When an audit row discovers a defect or a meaningful
   test gap, closing it requires, in order: (a) the evidence recorded in this file — §9 for a new
   finding, §10 for a matrix row; (b) one or more new concrete `VULKAN-*` rows in the owning phase,
   each with the eight fields §27 requires; (c) only then ✅.
2. **New IDs are taken from the owning phase's free range.** Never renumber, never reuse. External
   documents and commit messages cite these IDs.
3. **A finding that turns out not to be a defect is recorded as such.** "Audited, no Vulkan bug
   found, here is the evidence" is a valid close. Do not invent work to justify an audit.
4. **A test-gap task may close with no production change.** If the new test passes on first run,
   that is a real result — the evidence now exists where it did not. Say so in the row.
5. **Splitting is preferred to widening.** A row that turns out to be larger than one commit is
   split into new rows before work starts.
6. **Every deviation and refusal is written in the row itself**, never only in a commit message
   (`CLAUDE.md`, `plan_modern.md`'s own convention).
7. **A status may move backwards.** A ✅ row contradicted by a later measurement returns to 🟨 with
   the contradicting evidence, exactly as §9.2's stale documentation is being treated here.
8. **`NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT` is a real state with an owner.** Every such matrix row names the audit
   task responsible for resolving it; a row with no owner is a planning defect.

---

## 27. What a remediation task must contain

Every non-audit row states, at minimum:

1. the exact observed problem or the exact missing evidence;
2. its classification — implementation bug, test gap, or documentation gap;
3. the source files and functions involved, with line numbers where they help;
4. the EasyGL evidence (or the explicit note that EasyGL has none either);
5. the current Vulkan evidence;
6. the intended behaviour, judged against §5's authority order;
7. the exact permanent regression test to add, and why it discriminates — it must be able to fail
   for the defect it claims to detect;
8. the validation command, the dependencies, and the explicit non-goals where scope could creep.

A row that says only "improve X parity" is not a task and must be split or specified before work
begins.

---

## 28. Definition of done

Vulkan parity is complete when **all** of the following hold at the same commit:

1. Every in-scope row of §10 is `PARITY`, `VULKAN_STRONGER`, or an explicitly justified
   `NOT_APPLICABLE` / `SEMANTIC_DIVERGENCE` / `CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`. No `NEEDS_DEEPER_AUDIT`
   remains.
2. Every `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` in §9.1 has a ✅ task or a ⛔ row whose reason is written here.
3. Every meaningful `TEST_GAP` is closed by a registered, passing Vulkan CTest, or is justified in
   writing as not a Vulkan requirement.
4. No silent no-op remains where EasyGL implements the behaviour (`VULKAN-474`).
5. Capability reporting matches measured behaviour at the `GraphicsDevice` seam (`VULKAN-470`).
6. `cmake --build cmake-build-vulkan` exits 0 and the full suite is green apart from failures that
   are individually named, attributed and justified.
7. The `^Vulkan_` suite is green apart from explicitly justified environment/hardware skips, each
   naming the exact command, the exact failure, what remains unverified and where it must be rerun.
8. Vulkan validation produces no unexplained CNA-attributable errors or warnings.
9. `docs/vulkan-renderer.md` exists and matches measured current behaviour; §9.2's stale claims are
   corrected without deleting the historical record.
10. `VULKAN-487` has re-audited against the **then-current** EasyGL, and every gap that re-audit
    found has been closed — not merely filed.

Point 10 is deliberately the last: the comparison is against EasyGL as it is at gate time, never
against EasyGL as it was on 2026-09-04.

---

## 29. Dependencies on other plans

| Plan | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `plans/plan_graphics.md` | Historical owner of Vulkan work (Phase 73, Tasks 664–665 and 825–861, archived in `plan_graphics_20260709.md`). Cite it for history; it owns no current Vulkan parity work. |
| `plans/plan_fx.md` | Owns compiled XNA `.fx` bytecode on every backend, including `CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS`, `VulkanCompiledEffect.cpp` and `VulkanCompiledEffectTests.cpp` (`FX-065`, `FX-110`, `FX-112`). This plan does not duplicate a single row of it. Where Phase 7 touches an effect path shared with compiled effects, it references `plan_fx.md` rather than re-owning it. |
| `plans/plan_modern.md` | Owns the CNAEXT engine layer and its Phase 22 modern-GPU rollout, including every Vulkan row `MOD-2240`–`MOD-2254`. §4 lists exactly what stays there. `MOD-2096`'s mention of `plan_vulkan.md` is a pointer, not a transfer. |
| `plans/plan_csl.md` | Owns a portable shader language, so one `ShaderEffect` source can serve GLSL and SPIR-V backends. It is why this plan does **not** port EasyGL's 37 GLSL shader tests to Vulkan (`VULKAN-256`). |
| `plans/plan_gltf.md`, `plans/plan_cnj.md` | Own glTF/`.cnj` parsing. Phase 8 asks only whether the Vulkan renderer executes the already-parsed semantics correctly. |
| `plans/plan_binding.md` | Owns the C API/C ABI. `VULKAN-250` may need a new `ShaderDialectEXT` enumerator, which is an ABI change and must be coordinated there (`modules/c-api/src/CnaCApiGraphics.cpp:924`). |
| `plans/plan_platform.md` | Owns `IPlatform` and the SDL boundary. Vulkan is one of the intentional native edges; the boundary gates in `CLAUDE.md` must still pass after any change here. |
| `plans/plan_runtimerenderer.md` | Multi-renderer builds. `CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERERS` combinations that include `VULKAN` must keep configuring; renderer-local changes are preferred to common-interface changes for exactly this reason. |
| `plans/plan_bindings_upstream.md` | Where an XNA-over-FNA divergence taken here is recorded, per `CLAUDE.md`. |

## 30. Historical notes and superseded Vulkan work

Kept because it is evidence about how the renderer reached its current shape. **None of it is a
current-status claim**, and none of it may be used to suppress a gap this plan measures today.

- **Phase 73 of `plans/plan_graphics.md` — "Vulkan: full 2D+3D pixel-verified parity (gap closure)"
  (Tasks 664–665, 825–861).** Every row complete and archived into `plan_graphics_20260709.md` on
  2026-07-09. That phase closed against the target that existed in July 2026; EasyGL has kept
  moving, which is the entire reason this plan exists.
- **Task 861 (2026-07-09) concluded Vulkan had "exactly 1" confirmed-open limitation**
  (`Vulkan_DepthBias`) and that `SpriteBatch` sort-mode ordering, rotation/scale/crop and
  `SpriteFont` had "no dedicated pixel test" on Vulkan. The second half has since been closed by
  twelve CTests (§9.2 D-05); the first half was measured on a software driver (§9.2 D-07).
- **Tasks 864/865/867/875/876/877/878/879/911** — the Vulkan gaps `docs/rendertarget-support.md` and
  `docs/texture3d-texturecube-support.md` still describe as open. Every one of them is contradicted
  by code and tests in the current tree (§9.2 D-01…D-04). `VULKAN-481` corrects the documents and
  keeps the history.
- **`REMED-GFX-*` remediation series.** Vulkan carries a large amount of this work — `011`
  (orientation oracle), `077` (per-MRT write masks), `095` (ShaderEffect pipelines are render-pass
  state), `127`/`130`/`134`/`135`/`136` (readback and preserve-contents contracts), `144`
  (synchronization validation), `201` (multi-stream refusal), `212`/`243`/`244`. These are the
  reason several §10 rows read `VULKAN_STRONGER` rather than `PARITY`.
- **`plans/plan_webgpu.md` `WEBGPU-123`** contributed the shared cross-renderer diagnostic scene that
  Phase 12 builds on; `cna_diag_vulkan` exists because of it.
- **`plans/plan_cnj.md` `CNB-58`/`CNB-67`/`CNB-91` and `plans/plan_gltf.md` `GLTF-463`** brought the
  PBR/skinned-PBR vertex layouts (strides 48/56/60/68/76/80) to Vulkan — and, unremarked at the
  time, the stride range that finding F-01 shows the buffer allocator never caught up with.

## 31. Appendix A — interface-by-interface contract table (`VULKAN-027`)

Produced 2026-09-05 by `tools/vulkan/iface_contract_table.py` (committed with this row, so the
table is reproducible rather than remembered), which reads every `virtual` declaration out of
`modules/graphics/include/CNA/Internal/Renderers/Common/IGraphicsRenderer.hpp` between the eleven
interface class boundaries, then asks whether each renderer family declares an `override` for it.
Destructors are excluded. Two parser hazards were found and fixed before the numbers below were
trusted, and both are worth knowing if this table is ever regenerated: a declaration whose parameter
list contains a braced default (`= {}`) defeats a naive `[^;{]*override` pattern and reports a real
override as missing, and the word "virtual" in prose (`/// the game's own virtual resolution`)
manufactures three methods that do not exist. The counts before those fixes were 203/66 and
189/54-with-`Height`-`logical`-`transform`; they are stated here so a different number later is read
as a method change rather than a rediscovered bug.

**The census — 186 virtuals across the eleven interfaces. Two columns: as `VULKAN-027` first
measured it on 2026-09-05, and as `VULKAN-474` re-measured it on 2026-09-06 after the rows this
table opened had landed. The movement is accounted for exactly, which is the point of keeping
both:**

| Group | 2026-09-05 | 2026-09-06 | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| Both override | 102 | **106** | Vulkan implements what EasyGL implements. The four newcomers are `IEffectRenderer`'s array uniform setters, which `VULKAN-265` made Vulkan override — to **refuse**, which still counts as implementing the contract rather than falling silent. |
| **EasyGL overrides, Vulkan does not** | **54** | **50** | The substance of this audit — table A.1. Down by exactly those same four. |
| Vulkan overrides, EasyGL does not | 3 | **7** | `VULKAN_STRONGER`. The three originals — `GetShaderDialectEXT`, `SetStringMarkerEXT`, `ISpriteBatchRenderer::SetImmediateMode` — plus four that moved out of *neither*: `GetAppliedMultiSampleCountEXT` (`VULKAN-347`), and `GetAppliedBackBufferFormatEXT` with both `GetAppliedDepthStencilFormatEXT` virtuals (`VULKAN-348`). |
| Neither overrides | 27 | **23** | Both take the shared default, so there is no Vulkan-specific divergence. Down by the same four. |

**The finding.** Of the 54, exactly **one family is a real defect**: the four array uniform setters
of `IEffectRenderer` are silently ignored on Vulkan while EasyGL implements them. The gap itself was
already owned by **`VULKAN-252`**; what no row covered is that the calls stay *silent* until that
implementation lands, which is now `VULKAN-265`. Every other one of the 54 is a default that is correct, honest,
unreachable, or already owned by an existing row — each justified individually below rather than
dismissed as a group. Two candidates were investigated and **withdrawn** on evidence, which is why
they are named here: the `SetDataOptions` overloads look like dropped semantics but cannot be,
because the deferred draw model copies vertex and index bytes at draw time; and
`CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` looks like a dropped `SurfaceFormat` but cannot be, because Vulkan's
`Defer` verdict reduces to *Color only* and a non-`Color` render target is refused one layer above.

### A.1 EasyGL overrides, Vulkan does not (50)

| Interface | Virtual | Kind | Inherited default | Classification | Justification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IVertexBufferRenderer` | `SetDataWithOptions` | default | forwards to `SetData`, dropping `SetDataOptions` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | The hint has no observable meaning here: `Pending3DDraw` stores **copied** `vbData`/`ibData` taken at draw time (`VulkanRenderer.hpp:2356`), so a later `SetData` cannot alias an already-recorded draw and `Discard`/`NoOverwrite` cannot be violated. |
| `IIndexBufferRenderer` | `SetData16WithOptions` | default | forwards to `SetData16` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Same reason as `SetDataWithOptions` — index bytes are copied into `ibData` at draw time. |
| `IIndexBufferRenderer` | `SetData32WithOptions` | default | forwards to `SetData32` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Same reason as `SetDataWithOptions`. |
| `ITextureCubeRenderer` | `SetCompressedDataEXT` | default | `return false` | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | `false` is an explicit refusal, not silence. Vulkan has no BC path (zero `VK_FORMAT_BC*` occurrences in the family). Owned by `VULKAN-172`. |
| `ITextureCubeRenderer` | `BindGL` | default | no-op | NOT_APPLICABLE | Names a GL texture-unit binding. There is no Vulkan meaning; the no-op default is the only correct answer. |
| `ITextureCubeRenderer` | `ShareCpuPixels` | default | no-op | NOT_APPLICABLE | An EasyGL-internal shortcut that hands the renderer the CPU pixel vector `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` already own. Callers treat it as advisory — nothing reads back through it — so ignoring it changes no observable behaviour. |
| `ITextureCubeRenderer` | `GetSizeEXT` | default | `return 0` | NO_CONSUMER | Zero callers anywhere in the tree outside its own declaration, so the `0` cannot be observed. Recorded rather than fixed. |
| `ITexture3DRenderer` | `BindGL` | default | no-op | NOT_APPLICABLE | Names a GL texture-unit binding. There is no Vulkan meaning; the no-op default is the only correct answer. |
| `ITextureRenderer` | `GetSurfaceFormatEXT` | default | `return 0` | NO_CONSUMER | Zero callers outside its own declaration. |
| `ITextureRenderer` | `BindGL` | default | no-op | NOT_APPLICABLE | Names a GL texture-unit binding. There is no Vulkan meaning; the no-op default is the only correct answer. |
| `ITextureRenderer` | `ShareCpuPixels` | default | no-op | NOT_APPLICABLE | An EasyGL-internal shortcut that hands the renderer the CPU pixel vector `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` already own. Callers treat it as advisory — nothing reads back through it — so ignoring it changes no observable behaviour. |
| `IRenderTargetRenderer` | `GetColorGLHandle` | default | `return 0` | NOT_APPLICABLE | Names a GL object handle. |
| `IRenderTargetRenderer` | `HasRealDepthBuffer` | default | `return depthFormatWasRequested` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Vulkan allocates a real per-RT depth attachment exactly when a depth format was requested and none at all otherwise (`VulkanRenderTargetRenderer`, `PickDepthFormat`), so the default already tells the truth. |
| `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` | `GetGLHandle` | default | `return 0` | NOT_APPLICABLE | Names a GL object handle. |
| `IRenderTargetCubeRenderer` | `HasRealDepthBuffer` | default | `return depthFormatWasRequested` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Vulkan allocates a real per-RT depth attachment exactly when a depth format was requested and none at all otherwise (`VulkanRenderTargetRenderer`, `PickDepthFormat`), so the default already tells the truth. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `AcquireThreadContextLeaseEXT` | default | returns a null lease | OWNED_ELSEWHERE | Already owned by `VULKAN-025`, which must decide what a lease means with no context to lease. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `GetDefaultViewportRect` | default | `x=y=0` then `GetViewportSize(w,h)` | DEFAULT_CORRECT, **conditionally** | Vulkan overrides `GetViewportSize`, so the default composes into the right answer — **but only because this renderer has no letterbox/overscan rectangle to be wrong about.** That is F-04's point and this cell missed it when first written: the correctness is a consequence of F-03 (`SetPresentationMode` is a silent no-op, `VULKAN-330`), not an independent property. Whoever implements presentation modes must revisit this row — `VULKAN-331` owns it. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` | default | `Defer` | DEFERRED_POLICY | `Defer` hands the decision to the shared framework rule rather than asserting an answer. Observed by `Vulkan_SurfaceFormat_Throws`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` | default | `Defer` | DEFERRED_POLICY | `Defer` reduces to *Color only* (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:2104`), which is what `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT` reports and what `RenderTarget2D` enforces. This is also why `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` needs no override — see that row. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `ClassifyColorTransferFormatEXT` | default | `Defer` | DEFERRED_POLICY | As above. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT` | default | `return false` | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | Owned by `VULKAN-172`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT` | default | `return false` | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | Owned by `VULKAN-172`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `LoadsCompressedContentNativelyEXT` | default | `return false` | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | Owned by `VULKAN-172`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` | default | forwards to `CreateRenderTarget2D`, dropping `surfaceFormat` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | The dropped argument cannot carry information: `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` defers, which reduces to *Color only*, so a non-`Color` render target is refused one layer up and this entry point never sees one. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsHalfFloatTextureLinearFilteringEXT` | default | `return false` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | `VULKAN-470` observed why: a half-float `Texture2D` cannot be created at all under `GraphicsProfile.Reach`, so there is no surface for a sampler to filter. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `CreateRenderTargetCubeEXT` | default | forwards to `CreateRenderTargetCube` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Same reduction as `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `CreateComputeShader` | default | `return nullptr` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence; `VULKAN-470` observed that the compute route does not exist rather than being refused. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `CreateStorageBuffer` | default | `return nullptr` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | As above. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `DispatchCompute` | default | no-op | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Unreachable without a compute shader. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `MemoryBarrierEXT` | default | no-op | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Unreachable without compute or storage buffers. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT` | default | `return false` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Truthful and load-bearing: Vulkan's `ShaderEffect` consumes SPIR-V against a fixed push-constant layout and never executes the GLSL source the engine layer writes. `CLAUDE.md` tells callers to ask exactly this query. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsShadowSamplingEXT` | default | `return false` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT` | default | `return false` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsComputeShadersEXT` | default | `return false` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence, observed by `VULKAN-470`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsIndirectDrawEXT` | default | `return false` | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | Honest absence, observed by `VULKAN-470`: `DrawPrimitivesIndirectEXT` takes its argument buffer by reference and `CreateStorageBuffer` returns null, so there is nothing to hand it. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsComputeImageBindingEXT` | default | `return false` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `GetMaxVertexShaderStorageBlocksEXT` | default | `return 0` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Consistent with no storage buffers. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `BindStorageBufferForDrawEXT` | default | no-op | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Unreachable without a storage buffer. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SupportsGpuTimerEXT` | default | `return false` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `CreateGpuTimerEXT` | default | `return nullptr` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Honest absence. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `GetMaxComputeWorkGroupCountEXT` | default | `return 0` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Consistent with no compute. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `GetMaxComputeWorkGroupSizeEXT` | default | `return 0` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Consistent with no compute. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `GetMaxComputeWorkGroupInvocationsEXT` | default | `return 0` | CNAEXT_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Consistent with no compute. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SetRenderTargetCubeFace` | default | `rt->BindAsRenderTargetFace(face)` | DEFAULT_CORRECT | Vulkan implements `BindAsRenderTargetFace` (`VulkanRenderer.cpp:14353`) and the replay path calls it (`:13196`), so the default composes into working per-face binding. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `DrawPrimitivesIndirectEXT` | default | no-op | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | Unreachable: its argument buffer is taken by reference and none can be created. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `DrawIndexedPrimitivesIndirectEXT` | default | no-op | UNSUPPORTED_HONEST | As above. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `SetContextRecoveryEnabled` | default | no-op | OWNED_ELSEWHERE | GL context loss is not `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST`. Owned by `VULKAN-334`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `CanBeginDrawEXT` | default | `return true` | OWNED_ELSEWHERE | Already owned by `VULKAN-026`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `DebugSimulateContextLoss` | default | no-op | OWNED_ELSEWHERE | Owned by `VULKAN-334`. |
| `IGraphicsRenderer` | `DebugRestoreContext` | default | no-op | OWNED_ELSEWHERE | Owned by `VULKAN-334`. |
